# Connectivity: progress report, 2025-09-01 to 2026-09-04

*54 countries. Each row below is carried verbatim from that country's own progress report, which answers a fixed frame of indicators over the period; nothing is written here. A country not listed under an indicator has ***No evidence*** on it in its own report.*

**Progress values.** *Advanced* — a system entered service, a stage was completed or an instrument was made. *Stalled* — a stated target passed without delivery. *Regressed* — an instrument was withdrawn or neutralised, or a reported position worsened. *Mixed* — the indicator's instruments moved in different directions in the period; the clause after the comma names which moved which way. *No change* — the base holds a standing position and nothing in the period touched it. ***No evidence*** — the base holds nothing on this indicator at all. A value may carry a qualifying clause after a comma, as in *Advanced, regulations still pending*.

*The place reports do not share one window; the period above is the range they span.*

## Mobile penetration

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | [Three 5G licences were awarded in December 2025 for DZD 63.9bn](https://itweb.africa/article/mobilis-djezzy-ooredoo-lead-algerias-492m-5g-rollout/lwrKxq3YVEP7mg1o), and one operator is live in all wilayas. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A [tender was launched in mid-2025](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D8%B2%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%B1-%D8%AA%D8%AF%D8%AE%D9%84-%D8%B1%D8%B3%D9%85%D9%8A%D8%A7%D9%8B-%D8%B9%D8%B5%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D9%8A%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AE%D8%A7%D9%85%D8%B3) with no award, [reported across the trade press](https://www.connectingafrica.com/5g-networks/algeria-begins-tender-process-for-5g-licenses), and [one operator secured its licence in July](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/reports-and-coverage/12320-ooredoo-algeria-secures-5g-license-following-competitive-tender-process/). 2025-12-08 - [three licences were awarded for DZD 63.9bn, about US$492m, on a six-year plan to nationwide coverage by 2031](https://itweb.africa/article/mobilis-djezzy-ooredoo-lead-algerias-492m-5g-rollout/lwrKxq3YVEP7mg1o), the [rollout beginning that month](https://www.connectingafrica.com/5g-networks/algeria-launches-5g-rollout). By March 2026 [one operator was live in all wilayas](https://www.connectingafrica.com/5g-networks/ooredoo-expands-5g-network-to-all-algerian-provinces) and [another in eighteen](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/04/djezzy-launches-nationwide-5g-network-offering-high-speed-connectivity-across-18-algerian-states/).<br><br>2026-02-18 - [345 new 4G base stations were announced across 44 wilayas, toward 4,500 localities by 2027](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1802-53004-algeria-s-state-owned-telecom-plans-345-new-4g-stations-nationwide). [Draft decrees renewing two GSM licences were examined by the Council of Government in July 2026 with no term, fee or coverage obligation published](https://www.echoroukonline.com/الحكومة-تقف-دقيقة-صمت-على-ضحايا-الحرائ).<br><br>Market entry below licence level is inert: the regulator had [registered two authorisations for private cabled networks by the end of 2024 and received no applications at all under the simple-declaration regime, operators on general authorisations generating 1.28% of sector revenue](https://www.arpce.dz/en/file/q6i1l8).</details> | Advanced |
| Angola | A third operator [installed 20 towers in an exclave province](https://angop.ao/es/noticias/tecnologia/servicos-de-telefonia-movel-africell-chegam-a-cabinda/) and [enterprise 5G launched commercially](https://angop.ao/en/noticias/economia/filda-2026-servico-5g-empresarial-da-mercury-chega-a-provincia-de-cabinda/); no national subscriber figure is published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-19 — the third network [installed 20 telecommunications towers in the exclave province of Cabinda with satellite backhaul agreed, stating commercial voice, data and satellite-linked service would start within days](https://angop.ao/es/noticias/tecnologia/servicos-de-telefonia-movel-africell-chegam-a-cabinda/). No coverage percentage, investment figure, population reached or launch date is published, and "in the coming days" is the whole of the commitment held.<br><br>2026-07-23 — [an enterprise 5G service launched commercially in the same province, with the capital at over 70% city coverage, about US$20m budgeted and every provincial capital targeted by 2027](https://angop.ao/en/noticias/economia/filda-2026-servico-5g-empresarial-da-mercury-chega-a-provincia-de-cabinda/). This is enterprise connectivity rather than consumer mobile, and the throughput figures are the operator's own.<br><br>2026-06-11 — [national roaming and infrastructure sharing extended voice and data to remote areas through shared towers, solar power and satellite backhaul](https://angop.ao/noticias/tecnologia/projecto-ilumina-angola-garante-conectividade-digital-no-lago-dilolo/), with no national site count, budget or target published. The standalone fifth-generation network [remains at a trial call in a live environment with no commercial launch date](https://www.ericsson.com/en/press-releases/1/2025/unitel-and-ericsson-strengthen-angolas-connectivity-with-network-modernization), on a vendor release.<br><br>No regulator return and no operator subscriber total appears anywhere on this ledger, so penetration itself is unmeasured.</details> | Advanced, on network build, with no subscriber series on the base |
| Benin | A second operator [launched fifth-generation service in April 2026](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/28/benin-enters-the-5g-era-as-moov-africa-benin-launches-high-speed-network-at-novotel-orisha-cotonou/), two years after the first. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-04-28 — [a second operator launched fifth-generation service, after the first launched in 2024](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/28/benin-enters-the-5g-era-as-moov-africa-benin-launches-high-speed-network-at-novotel-orisha-cotonou/). That is the whole of the window's movement in network capability.<br><br>No subscriber total, penetration rate, coverage footprint or regulator return appears on this ledger for either generation of network, so what is recorded is the arrival of a second competitor and nothing about who is on either network. The regulator's own 2025 report is held for its mobile-money and usage findings, reported elsewhere, and carries no connection figure in the account held.</details> | Advanced |
| Botswana | [Mobile telephony subscriptions reached 4,370,350 by September 2025, a rise of 6%%](https://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/State_of_ICTs%2C_2026_%28v2.pdf), and the state-held operator [targets commercial 5G in Gaborone by October 2026](https://www.itweb.africa/article/btc-steps-into-botswanas-5g-race/KWEBbvyLb2RqmRjO), four years behind the other two. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-03-01 — [the regulator reported 4,370,350 mobile telephony subscriptions at September 2025 against 4,104,304 a year earlier, and 3,112,962 mobile broadband subscriptions against 2,915,158](https://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/State_of_ICTs%2C_2026_%28v2.pdf). [Prepaid accounts were 96% of the mobile base and mobile money subscriptions rose 24% to 1,455,986](https://www.bocra.org.bw/sites/default/files/sites/default/files/documents/State_of_ICTs%2C_2026_%28v2.pdf).<br><br>2025-09-22 — [all three mobile operators cut retail tariffs after the regulator’s costing and pricing study, with inter-network termination rates reduced](https://www.bocra.org.bw/node/62773).<br><br>The report publishes subscription counts and no penetration rate, so the share of people connected cannot be stated from the base.<br><br>2026-08-31 — the state-held operator [commits more than P240 million for 2026/27 and targets commercial 5G in Gaborone by October, having spent P225.5 million in 2025/26 upgrading 151 sites to 4G, and putting population coverage at 97 per cent for 2G and 4G and 62 per cent for 3G](https://www.itweb.africa/article/btc-steps-into-botswanas-5g-race/KWEBbvyLb2RqmRjO). The other two operators have been live on 5G since 2022, so a launch closes a gap rather than opening a market.</details> | Advanced |
| Burkina Faso | Operators [delivered their 2025 site commitments in full at 200, 200 and 150 sites](https://www.sidwaya.info/secteur-des-telecommunications-un-taux-dexecution-des-engagements-situe-entre-80-et-90/), against 800 planned for the year across the fund and the acceleration project. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-06-21 — [the three operators' 2025 commitments were delivered in full at 200, 200 and 150 sites, and for 2026 one operator is at about half of 100 planned while another has five live, twenty installing and 125 in build](https://www.sidwaya.info/secteur-des-telecommunications-un-taux-dexecution-des-engagements-situe-entre-80-et-90/), against [800 new sites planned for 2025 across the universal-access fund and the acceleration project with the tender then in progress](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/general-news/12008-burkina-faso-plans-800-new-telecom-sites-to-close-coverage-gaps/).<br><br>The 800 planned and the 550 committed-and-delivered are never reconciled in any held source, so a programme that reports full delivery is reporting against a smaller number than the one announced.<br><br>No subscriber total, penetration rate or regulator connection series appears anywhere on this ledger, so towers are counted and users are not. Power is the standing constraint on the towers themselves, reported under energy.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | Two operators [were contracted to build 4G on 178 hills](https://www.burunditimes.com/5-9m-onatel-deal-marks-new-push-for-digital-access-in-rural-burundi/), a third [was licensed with no launch](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/telecom/lyca-mobile-advances-telecom-and-e-health-plans-in-burundi), and no national penetration figure exists. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-03-04 — [one operator was contracted at more than US$10m for 86 hills across 14 provinces](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/04/lumitel-burundi-launches-10m-high-speed-internet-project-across-the-country/), and 2026-05-04 — [the incumbent for 92 hills at US$5.9m, stated to cover 372,035 people](https://techafricanews.com/2026/05/04/burundi-signs-usd-5-9-million-contract-to-expand-rural-network-connectivity/), [confirmed the following day](https://www.burunditimes.com/5-9m-onatel-deal-marks-new-push-for-digital-access-in-rural-burundi/). [A mast-deployment programme under the same financing is named by the regulator as in train, addressing coverage rather than outages](https://www.iwacu-burundi.org/telecommunications-un-casse-tete-pour-les-abonnes/).<br><br>2026-03-02 — [a third operator holds a licence granted in October 2025 with telecommunications and e-health plans and no service launch on file](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/telecom/lyca-mobile-advances-telecom-and-e-health-plans-in-burundi).<br><br>[An infrastructure-sharing law is named by the regulator as in draft, with no text, tabling date or sponsoring ministry published](https://www.iwacu-burundi.org/telecommunications-un-casse-tete-pour-les-abonnes/). No subscriber total, penetration rate or regulator return appears anywhere on this ledger, so coverage contracts are visible and take-up is not.</details> | Advanced, on contracted coverage rather than on any measured subscriber base |
| Cape Verde | The regulator counted [588,603 active SIMs at 113.9% penetration at the end of March 2026](https://www.arme.cv/index.php/noticia-geral/1392-arme-publica-relatorio-indicadores-estatisticos-das-comunicacoes-eletronicas-1o-trimestre-de-2026), down 0.8% on the quarter. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[ARME's Q1 2026 market indicators, published on 5 August 2026, count 588,603 active SIM cards at a penetration rate of 113.9%, a fall of 0.8% against the fourth quarter of 2025 and a rise of 0.2% against the same quarter a year earlier, with CVTelecom holding 68% of active SIMs and Unitel T+ 32%](https://www.arme.cv/index.php/noticia-geral/1392-arme-publica-relatorio-indicadores-estatisticos-das-comunicacoes-eletronicas-1o-trimestre-de-2026).<br><br>The quarterly series had stopped at Q2 2025 in the base and now resumes. Nothing in it is broken down by island or municipality, which the ledger carries as an open row.</details> | No change |
| Central African Republic | A third operator [launched fourth-generation service in the capital](https://www.techreviewafrica.com/news/5420/telecel-centrafrique-launches-4g-services-in-bangui-to-expand-national-connectivity), while [subscribers at a northern town had no calls, no internet and no mobile money for close to a month](https://corbeaunews-centrafrique.org/centrafrique-a-batangafo-un-mois-sans-reseau-orange-bloque-les-communications-et-les-services-financiers/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05-14 — [the third operator launched fourth-generation service in the capital, completing commercial service at all three](https://www.techreviewafrica.com/news/5420/telecel-centrafrique-launches-4g-services-in-bangui-to-expand-national-connectivity), having been [licensed in December 2025](https://telecelgroup.com/telecel-centrafrique-obtient-sa-licence-4g-une-nouvelle-ere-pour-la-connectivite-en-republique-centrafricaine/) against [one operator live since May 2025](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/telecom/rca-orange-lance-la-4g-pour-repondre-a-la-demande-croissante-en-connectivite). A licence is not coverage: [fourth-generation population coverage was measured at 0.3% in 2023](https://www.techreviewafrica.com/news/5420/telecel-centrafrique-launches-4g-services-in-bangui-to-expand-national-connectivity).<br><br>2026-07-28 — [subscribers at a northern town reported no calls, no internet and no mobile money for close to a month, with no restoration date, and some officials unpaid for the previous month because salary transfers run over mobile services](https://corbeaunews-centrafrique.org/centrafrique-a-batangafo-un-mois-sans-reseau-orange-bloque-les-communications-et-les-services-financiers/). Single-operator dependency with no redundancy, attributed to off-grid relay sites, equipment theft and repair crews travelling hundreds of kilometres.<br><br>The instrument that would fix that is dormant: [the country's first passive-infrastructure licence for pylons, antennas and technical sites to be built and leased to the operators was signed in July 2025](https://oubanguimedias.com/2025/07/21/centrafrique-signature-officielle-du-cahier-des-charges-pour-la-licence-dexploitation-des-infrastructures-passives-de-sos-telecoms/) fourteen days before the window opened, and no tower count, site build or lease has been recorded since.</details> | Mixed, all three operators now hold fourth-generation licences while one town lost every service for a month |
| Chad | The larger operator's [network investment programme](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/chad-pushes-telcos-for-network-upgrades) has contested delivery, and [fifty telecom sites and fifty kilometres of fibre](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1302-52879-chad-plans-50-telecom-sites-50-km-of-fiber-in-n-djamena) were announced by an outgoing minister. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The investment figure is reported at group level rather than for the national entity, and no source reconciles it with the figures the minister cited ([programme](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/chad-pushes-telcos-for-network-upgrades), [sites](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1302-52879-chad-plans-50-telecom-sites-50-km-of-fiber-in-n-djamena), [operator](https://www.digitalbusiness.africa/internet-tchad-starlink-pourrait-passer-de-fai-a-operateur-telecom/)).<br><br>The fifty sites and fifty kilometres were announced at a ministry ceremony by the outgoing minister seven weeks before the portfolio changed hands, and nothing is recorded against them since ([announcement](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1302-52879-chad-plans-50-telecom-sites-50-km-of-fiber-in-n-djamena)).<br><br>A third private mobile operator is named in the national strategy with no licensing process on record ([third operator](https://techafricanews.com/2025/07/18/chad-launches-1-5b-digital-strategy-to-connect-citizens-by-2030/)).</details> | Stalled |
| Comoros | Both fifth-generation services stand where they launched: [one pilot site](https://www.lagazettedescomores.com/soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9/t%C3%A9l%C3%A9communications-comores-telecom-lance-%C3%A0-son-tour-la-5g-.html) and [two districts of the capital](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/3822/axian-telecom-advances-next-2030-vision-in-comoros). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The incumbent's service is [live at one pilot site at a claimed rate of up to 1 Gbit/s](https://www.lagazettedescomores.com/soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9/t%C3%A9l%C3%A9communications-comores-telecom-lance-%C3%A0-son-tour-la-5g-.html), the rate being the operator's own claim rather than a measurement, and [no second site, coverage or subscriber figure has been published since](https://alwatwan.net/societe/comores-telecom-i-la-direction-pr%C3%A9sente-son-bilan-2024-et-ses-perspectives-pour-2025.html).<br><br>The second operator's service is [live in two districts of Moroni, with extension to the other two islands announced](https://lagazettedescomores.com/soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9/t%C3%A9l%C3%A9communications-yas-comores-a-lanc%C3%A9-officiellement-la-5g-.html), and its [group's own 2026 restatement names no new site](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/3822/axian-telecom-advances-next-2030-vision-in-comoros).<br><br>No penetration, subscriber or coverage series exists for this country at all - the measurement body the 2018 strategy designed was never created.</details> | No change |
| Congo | The regulator's first market report holds [3.757m mobile-internet subscribers at December 2024, up 9.4%](https://arpce.org/api/publications/rapport-du-marche-linternet-mobile-2024/download). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's 2024 mobile-internet market report, the first Congolese telecom market data the base holds, records [3.757m mobile-internet subscribers at December 2024, up 9.4%, against an over-15 population of 3.738m](https://arpce.org/api/publications/rapport-du-marche-linternet-mobile-2024/download).<br><br>Subscriptions now exceed the addressable over-15 population, so the count no longer measures people. It is an older source arriving late, which makes it a baseline rather than news, and no later regulator market report is held - so the most recent penetration position the base can state is twenty months old.</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | The ministry [put mobile coverage at 95% and telecom penetration above 130% of population](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6286/cote-divoire-positions-digital-technology-as-key-driver-of-economic-transformation), while [5G's July 2026 launch passed](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/15/cote-divoire-accelerates-digital-push-with-starlink-licence-and-5g-launch/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>At the window's opening the base held [4G covering 73.34% of localities and 93.71% of the population](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2636184/economie/africa-bb-maps-connectivite-haut-debit-la-cote-divoire-dessine-sa-cartographie-nationale-des-reseaux-de-telecommunications).<br><br>2026-07-13 - the ministry [stated 95% mobile coverage, telecom penetration above 130% of population and smartphone adoption of 41%](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6286/cote-divoire-positions-digital-technology-as-key-driver-of-economic-transformation), a figure [repeated at an investment forum against a stated ambition of 80% by 2030](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6058/ansut-highlights-connectivity-as-foundation-for-digital-and-financial-inclusion-at-apide-2026). Coverage figures switch between territory, localities and population without a stated base, so the two ends are not the same measurement.<br><br>5G went the other way. It was [announced for July 2026 in towns above 25,000 inhabitants with all operators said to be equipped](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/15/cote-divoire-accelerates-digital-push-with-starlink-licence-and-5g-launch/), after [spectrum was re-planned and financial terms settled following a missed 2023 target](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2635241/economie/deploiement-de-la-5g-en-cote-divoire-bientot-la-connectivite-a-la-technologie). No launch, spectrum award terms or coverage obligation is on file: the second stated date to pass undelivered.</details> | Mixed, the ministry's coverage and penetration figures rose while a second stated 5G launch date passed undelivered |
| Djibouti | The incumbent's [statutory monopoly stands under two laws, with no competing licensed operator](https://www.lanation.dj/du-hub-regional-au-catalyseur-continental-la-nouvelle-ere-de-djibouti-telecom/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [monopoly is restated in the operator's own profile and no competing licensed operator exists](https://www.lanation.dj/du-hub-regional-au-catalyseur-continental-la-nouvelle-ere-de-djibouti-telecom/), unchanged at both ends of the window. The profile's cable and capacity figures are stale and are not carried here.<br><br>No subscriber count, penetration rate, coverage figure or tariff is published by the operator or by any regulator, for any date in the window. A country carrying a substantial share of the world's submarine cable traffic publishes no measure of how many of its own people are connected.</details> | No change |
| DR Congo | Mobile subscriptions reached [73.93m at 67.8% penetration in 2025, from 63.96m and 60.5% in 2024](https://admin.eaco.int/assets/e774142a-21fd-48e2-bdfd-3309d7d559d7?download=EACO%20Regional%20Communications%20Sector%20Statistics%20Report%202025.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The first regulator-submitted regional dataset the base holds puts the country at [73.93m mobile subscriptions and 67.8% penetration in 2025, against 63.96m and 60.5% in 2024](https://admin.eaco.int/assets/e774142a-21fd-48e2-bdfd-3309d7d559d7?download=EACO%20Regional%20Communications%20Sector%20Statistics%20Report%202025.pdf).<br><br>It is a regional compilation of the national regulator's own returns rather than a published national series, and the regulator itself publishes nothing comparable that the base holds. The same return reports no mobile-money transaction value at all, so a country with tens of millions of wallets contributes no payment volume to the regional statistics it now files into.<br><br>The next generation is not moving: a [public consultation on introducing 5G opened in March 2025](https://actualite.cd/2025/03/18/larptc-lance-une-consultation-publique-sur-lintroduction-de-la-5g-en-rdc-communique) and seventeen months later no consultation report, spectrum award, licence or revised timetable has been published.</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | [Fifth-generation service has run since June 2025 on refarmed 2.6 GHz](https://www.ookla.com/articles/5g-north-africa-2026), while [combined median download speed fell to 34.77 Mbps](https://www.ookla.com/articles/5g-north-africa-2026). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Service has been [in place since June 2025, initially focused on Cairo and Alexandria, on shared refarmed blocks of 20 to 30 MHz](https://www.trade.gov/country-commercial-guides/egypt-information-and-communications-technology-and-digital-economy); no coverage obligation, subscriber count or population-coverage figure is held, and the country is the only one of four North African launch markets with no published coverage or quality obligation.<br><br>Crowdsourced measurement puts [fifth-generation median download at about 89 Mbps at month six against 110.5 Mbps on launch day, and the combined median at 34.77 Mbps against a pre-launch baseline of 37.68 Mbps, with fourth-generation down 17%](https://www.ookla.com/articles/5g-north-africa-2026) - cited as a measurement, not absorbed.<br><br>[About 3,000 new towers are planned in 2026 and more than 9,000 over three years, against a target of 40,000 by 2028](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/02/egypt-invests-over-6-billion-in-telecom-infrastructure-and-digital-connectivity-development-since-2019/). 2026-08-19 - a [first upper 6 GHz mobile trial ran at a reported 1.7 Gbps per user, with no allocation, award or commercial date](https://itweb.africa/article/telecom-egypt-tests-high-capacity-spectrum/RgeVDqPRka37KJN3).</details> | Mixed, fifth-generation service is in place across four operators and towers are expanding while measured combined download speeds fell below the pre-launch baseline |
| Equatorial Guinea | A [semestral plan targets 85 per cent second- and fourth-generation coverage](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/secundo_consejo_directivo_extraordinario_del_ministerio_de_transportes) through 34 new towers in the islands and the interior. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The plan was presented to the Prime Minister in July 2026 and also names a zero-roaming arrangement with four neighbours and a national digital postcode system; the plan document itself is not held ([connectivity plan](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/secundo_consejo_directivo_extraordinario_del_ministerio_de_transportes)).<br><br>No current coverage figure, subscription series or penetration rate is published at any date, so the 85 per cent target ([plan](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/secundo_consejo_directivo_extraordinario_del_ministerio_de_transportes)) has no starting point on the record.</details> | Advanced |
| Eritrea | Active connections [reached 859,000, or 23.7% of the population, up 8.2% year on year](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-eritrea), among the lowest rates in Africa. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-11-08 — DataReportal's Digital 2026 report for Eritrea [puts active mobile connections at 859,000, 23.7% of the population, 49.3% of them broadband, and up 8.2% on the year](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-eritrea). [The previous edition](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2025-eritrea) is the comparison.<br><br>The direction is upward and the level is among the lowest in Africa. Both statements are true, and the second governs what sits downstream of it: a foundational digital ID, mobile money and any service assumed to reach a citizen through a handset all rest on this figure. No operator or regulator return on the ledger tests it.</details> | Advanced |
| Eswatini | [Mobile coverage against mobile-internet use](https://independentnews.co.sz/46514/business/aviation/digital-eswatini-project-hinges-on-leadership-ps-warns/) has not moved, while a [smart-city deployment](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/04/eswatini-ramps-up-tech-reform-with-new-legislation-5g-rollout-and-coding-training-for-all/) fits a fifth-generation network, 59 camera systems and public wireless access in the capital. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The coverage and usage figures come from the ICT ministry, given at a change-management workshop for the digital project ([coverage](https://independentnews.co.sz/46514/business/aviation/digital-eswatini-project-hinges-on-leadership-ps-warns/), [reform](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/04/eswatini-ramps-up-tech-reform-with-new-legislation-5g-rollout-and-coding-training-for-all/)).<br><br>The capital's deployment fits a fifth-generation network, 59 artificial-intelligence-enabled cameras and public wireless access ([deployment](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/04/eswatini-ramps-up-tech-reform-with-new-legislation-5g-rollout-and-coding-training-for-all/)).<br><br>No lawful basis, retention rule or oversight arrangement for the camera network is stated anywhere, and the four laws named as forthcoming alongside it — including critical infrastructure and artificial intelligence — have not appeared.</details> | No change |
| Ethiopia | The incumbent reports [99.8% population coverage](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/20/ethio-telecom-reaches-99-8-coverage-accelerates-smart-infrastructure-development/) on about 90m subscribers, and the second operator 14.7m active customers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Mobile connections reached 93.2m in late 2025, 68.4% of the population, from 85.4m and 63.8% in early 2025 ([connections](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-ethiopia)).<br><br>The incumbent reported 99.8% population coverage in April 2026 ([coverage](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/20/ethio-telecom-reaches-99-8-coverage-accelerates-smart-infrastructure-development/)), and the second operator 14.7m ninety-day active customers in August after upgrading more than 67 sites across nine southern towns against congestion and power problems ([site upgrades](https://itweb.africa/article/safaricom-ethiopia-upgrades-67-sites/PmxVEMKEOolvQY85)).<br><br>Contracts signed at the 2026 industry congress cover 1,500 new sites and 502 upgrades from one vendor and 647 from another, under a three-year corporate strategy to 2028 ([expansion contracts](https://addisstandard.com/ethio-telecom-announces-strategic-network-expansion-agreements-with-ericsson-zte-huawei-nokia-at-barcelona/), [strategy](https://www.ethiotelecom.et/ethio-telecom-announces-its-new-three-year-strategy-next-horizon-digital-beyond-2028-strategy/)); a review in August put 879 sites complete under the second programme ([progress review](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/10/ethio-telecom-and-zte-advance-network-modernisation-as-4g-coverage-hits-92/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Gambia | The mobile incumbent's [network modernisation is contracted](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/headlines/gamtel-ycell-sign-landmark-share-investment-and-network-modernization-agreement) at GMD 6.1bn, covering fourth- and fifth-generation radio access and core systems. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The agreement was signed on 9 July 2026 alongside the equity transfer ([modernisation](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/headlines/gamtel-ycell-sign-landmark-share-investment-and-network-modernization-agreement)).<br><br>No subscription series, penetration rate, coverage figure or market-share statistic is published by the regulator at any date, so a contracted upgrade is the only forward-looking connectivity evidence the base holds and there is nothing to measure it against.</details> | Advanced |
| Ghana | The latest regulator bulletin records [38.47m mobile voice subscriptions](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/Q3-2024-Statistical-Bulletin-Third-Quarter-Volume-9-Isuue-3.pdf) at 116.84 per cent penetration, published about eight months in arrears. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The July to September 2024 bulletin is still the latest issue held; it also records 25.56m mobile data subscriptions, one operator on 75.09 per cent of the market and fixed broadband down 6.83 per cent in the quarter to 216,143 ([bulletin](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/Q3-2024-Statistical-Bulletin-Third-Quarter-Volume-9-Isuue-3.pdf)). The penetration rate exceeds 100 per cent because it counts subscriptions rather than people ([bulletin](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/Q3-2024-Statistical-Bulletin-Third-Quarter-Volume-9-Isuue-3.pdf)).<br><br>Against a ten-year average of 223 new sites a year, 1,150 are planned for 2026 across two operators ([site plan](https://www.myjoyonline.com/sam-george-unveils-massive-1150-cell-site-rollout-to-end-network-woes/)), with the regulator putting more than 500 under construction in August 2026 to address dropped calls, slow speeds and coverage gaps and no delivery figure published against the target ([regulator](https://www.myjoyonline.com/nca-director-general-urges-journalists-to-protect-public-trust-in-digital-era/)).<br><br>The first shared 5G wholesale network was activated in March 2026 ([activation](https://www.graphic.com.gh/business/business-news/ngic-secures-regulatory-clearance-activates-ghanas-first-shared-5g-network.html)), and its operator's exclusivity was deleted by regulator amendment with effect from 15 July 2026 after its objection was heard and overruled, the rest of the licence and the spectrum assignment retained ([exclusivity ended](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/17/ghana-ends-ngics-exclusive-wholesale-5g-licence-to-boost-competition/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea | The state [holds 87.5 per cent of the second mobile operator](https://www.connectingafrica.com/investment/mtn-sells-guinea-conakry-business-to-guinean-government), transferred at the end of 2024, on about 3m subscribers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The transfer was made official on 30 December 2024 ([transfer](https://www.connectingafrica.com/investment/mtn-sells-guinea-conakry-business-to-guinean-government)).<br><br>No regulator subscription series, penetration rate or market-share figure is published at any date, so a single operator's subscriber count carried in a transfer report is the whole of the connectivity measurement the base holds.</details> | No change |
| Guinea-Bissau | No new penetration series was published; the ledger's own measures remain the base's figures. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Nothing in this batch updates the mobile or internet penetration measures the ledger already carries.<br><br>What moved instead is supply and enforcement: the [national fibre backbone tender was relaunched in March 2026 after two cancelled rounds](https://wardip.gw/guine-bissau-lanca-concurso-internacional-para-rede-nacional-de-fibra-optica/), and the regulator [warned that only the licensed satellite operator may sell its service, with illegal resale to face sanctions](https://arn.gw/comunicado-a-imprensa-fornecimento-ilegal-de-servicos-de-acesso-a-internet/).</details> | No change |
| Kenya | The dominant operator took a [25-year unified licence](https://itweb.africa/article/safaricom-secures-landmark-25-year-licence/VgZeyqJlRgo7djX9) in May 2026, consolidating spectrum and authorisations against a historic ten-year term. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Licence-related costs were about US$126.7m in the year to March 2026, and the prior position was a temporary two-year unified permit ([licence](https://itweb.africa/article/safaricom-secures-landmark-25-year-licence/VgZeyqJlRgo7djX9)).<br><br>A twenty-five-year term is the longest regulatory commitment in the base, and it was granted to the operator the base separately records as dominant in mobile money and designated systemically critical.</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | The larger operator reports [1.21m customers and 85 per cent market share](https://publiceyenews.com/2026/06/17/vodacom-lesotho-at-30-from-one-portable-antenna-to-a-digital-nation/), and the other has [149 5G sites](https://techafricanews.com/2025/06/20/econet-powers-lesothos-digital-leap-with-major-network-upgrade/) on 700MHz. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The thirtieth-anniversary account of June 2026 gives 1.21m customers, 85 per cent market share, 422 base stations of which 101 are solar, and coverage of 97 per cent on 2G, 98 per cent on 3G and 96 per cent on 4G ([account](https://publiceyenews.com/2026/06/17/vodacom-lesotho-at-30-from-one-portable-antenna-to-a-digital-nation/)).<br><br>A US$40m phased network upgrade was announced in March 2026 ([upgrade](https://itweb.africa/article/vodacom-lesotho-invests-40m-to-supercharge-connectivity/P3gQ2qGAJZZ7nRD1)), following an upgrade pledged at the group chief executive's visit in August 2025 ([visit](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/25/vodacom-lesotho-upgrades-network-as-group-ceo-pledges-continued-investment-in-digital-transformation/)).<br><br>The second operator grew its 5G sites from 54 to 149 on 700MHz by June 2025, and no later figure is held ([5G](https://techafricanews.com/2025/06/20/econet-powers-lesothos-digital-leap-with-major-network-upgrade/), [network](https://lestimes.com/?p=87091)).</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | A [universal licence was revoked](https://www.connectingafrica.com/regulation/liberia-strips-starcell-of-operating-license) in July 2026 after six years without operation, leaving two mobile operators of any scale. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Spectrum, numbering and regulatory privileges reverted to the regulator, the licensee having been suspended for 90 days from February 2026; the market at mid-2026 is estimated at 3.2m subscribers for the leading operator, 2.3m for the second and 14,000 for the state operator ([revocation](https://www.connectingafrica.com/regulation/liberia-strips-starcell-of-operating-license)).<br><br>Fifth-generation services are stated as planned with investors still being engaged, and no spectrum award, licence condition or launch date is published ([plans](https://www.liberianobserver.com/news/liberia-on-the-verge-of-5g-revolution/article_992b027e-4c8a-4578-bf70-baf2e2eabeeb.html)).</details> | Mixed, a dormant licence was revoked while a fifth-generation plan has no spectrum award behind it |
| Libya | Commercial [5G](https://libyaherald.com/2026/01/almadar-mobile-launches-libyas-first-5g-service-in-tripoli-countrywide-roll-out-to-follow-soon/) began in central Tripoli in January 2026 and the state operator is in [talks with Nokia](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/524936); no penetration figure is published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Almadar Aljadid began the country's first commercial 5G service in central Tripoli on 29 January 2026, with extension to the rest of the country stated to follow ([launch](https://libyaherald.com/2026/01/almadar-mobile-launches-libyas-first-5g-service-in-tripoli-countrywide-roll-out-to-follow-soon/)). A regional review a year later placed Libya among the North African markets where 5G is live ([review](https://www.ookla.com/articles/5g-north-africa-2026)).<br><br>LTT met Nokia on 15 July 2026 on 5G cooperation, with no agreement announced ([meeting](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/524936)). No subscriber count, coverage share or penetration measure is published in this base, so what is recorded here is network availability rather than take-up.</details> | Advanced |
| Madagascar | A [5G-Advanced trial](https://www.lexpress.mg/2026/07/service-public-la-technologie-au.html) ran in July 2026 with no commercial 5G on record, above a [4G and 5G expansion programme](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/11/eib-grants-100m-to-axian-telecom-for-4g-5g-rollout-in-tanzania-and-madagascar/) funded at US$100m. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The trial ran at an operator campus on 23 July 2026; the operator claims to be the fourth in the world to test the technology, and the claim is its own ([trial](https://www.lexpress.mg/2026/07/service-public-la-technologie-au.html), [account](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/27/madagascar-launches-5-5g-pilot-becomes-fourth-country-globally-to-test-technology/)).<br><br>A development bank lent US$100m to the operator group under a European connectivity programme, US$40m of it for Madagascar, alongside US$82m of the group's own investment ([loan](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/11/eib-grants-100m-to-axian-telecom-for-4g-5g-rollout-in-tanzania-and-madagascar/)); no coverage or site figure has been published since ([loan](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/11/eib-grants-100m-to-axian-telecom-for-4g-5g-rollout-in-tanzania-and-madagascar/), [investment](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/01/axian-telecom-invests-82m-to-expand-4g-in-madagascar-comoros-tanzania/)).<br><br>No subscriber count, penetration rate or coverage share for the country is held at all.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | An [industry report puts 4G coverage at 87 per cent against mobile-internet penetration of 12.5](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/13/digital-africa-summit-heads-to-malawi-as-the-country-enters-its-next-digital-chapter/), while [5G launched in 2025](https://malawi24.com/2025/07/26/chiyamwaka-hails-5g-rollout-as-key-friver-of-malawi-2063-vision/) and reached [111 sites](https://techafricanews.com/2026/05/22/airtel-malawi-eyes-satellite-connectivity-as-it-boosts-network-expansion-strategy/) on the other network. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The industry association's report gives fourth-generation coverage at 87 per cent of the population against mobile-internet penetration of 12.5 per cent, scores the country 36 of 100 on its digital nations index and 55 of 100 on policy and regulation, and describes mobile-money adoption as near universal ([report](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/13/digital-africa-summit-heads-to-malawi-as-the-country-enters-its-next-digital-chapter/)); it is the association's own index and the base holds no official national subscriber and coverage series ([report](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/13/digital-africa-summit-heads-to-malawi-as-the-country-enters-its-next-digital-chapter/)).<br><br>The first 5G network launched at Lilongwe in July 2025 with four further cities named as extension targets, and no later site count is held for it ([launch](https://malawi24.com/2025/07/26/chiyamwaka-hails-5g-rollout-as-key-friver-of-malawi-2063-vision/)).<br><br>The other operator reported 111 sites across ten districts, after 58 new sites on MWK 50.8bn of network capital expenditure in the 2025 financial year ([sites](https://techafricanews.com/2026/05/22/airtel-malawi-eyes-satellite-connectivity-as-it-boosts-network-expansion-strategy/)), and a fixed-wireless operator extended 4G coverage to nineteen neighbourhoods of the capital ([fixed wireless](https://itweb.africa/article/lilongwe-gains-expanded-4g-access/kYbe9MXbZyzvAWpG)).<br><br>[A 36th tower under the Last Mile Rural Connectivity Project was switched on at Kalira Village, Traditional Authority Kalumbu, in September 2026](https://techafricanews.com/2026/09/03/malawi-activates-36th-rural-telecom-tower-digital-connectivity/). [The wider World Bank-financed programme carries US$150m across digital connectivity, a 100-tower universal service fund rollout and an e-services platform](https://www.itweb.africa/article/malawi-drives-next-wave-of-connectivity/KA3WwqdzP647rydZ). Neither account gives a population figure for the coverage the 36 towers have added.</details> | Mixed, 5G launched and expanding against fourth-generation coverage of 87 per cent that only 12.5 per cent of people use |
| Mali | A [development-bank loan](https://www.ifc.org/en/pressroom/2025/ifc-invests-in-orange-mali-to-expand-digital-connectivity-and-support-women-s-digi) of November 2025 puts 300 4G towers, half of them rural, and fibre to about 300,000 households into build. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The build is the base's only forward commitment on mobile coverage: 300 towers with half in rural areas, alongside fibre to about 300,000 households ([loan](https://www.ifc.org/en/pressroom/2025/ifc-invests-in-orange-mali-to-expand-digital-connectivity-and-support-women-s-digi)).<br><br>No subscriber count, penetration rate or coverage share is published for the country at all, so the addition is on record and the base it adds to is not.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritania | [Four fifteen-year 5G licences](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/15/mauritania-advances-5g-rollout-as-regulator-names-provisional-winners-for-national-licences/) were awarded in 2026 and the first commercial network launched at Nouakchott in August, with no coverage or subscriber figure published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Licences went to the three incumbents and a new entrant at bids of MRU 252.6m to 305m plus 5 per cent of revenue, breaking the three-operator structure ([licences](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/15/mauritania-advances-5g-rollout-as-regulator-names-provisional-winners-for-national-licences/)).<br><br>One operator launched the country's first commercial 5G network at Nouakchott on 10 August 2026 under the digital transformation minister; no coverage footprint, tariff or subscriber figure is published ([launch](https://ghabess.net/23385), [account](https://lequotidien.mr/?p=39791)).<br><br>The same operator had completed a full network modernisation at Nouakchott with a foreign vendor in November 2025, again with no coverage, capacity or subscriber figure published ([modernisation](https://techafricanews.com/2025/11/13/chinguitel-and-huawei-complete-major-network-modernization-across-mauritania/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | Mobile penetration is [reported in the 2025 ICT statistics](https://statsmauritius.govmu.org/Documents/Statistics/ESI/2026/EI1939/ICT_Yr25_100726.pdf), a first observation on this ledger. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The statistics office's 2025 ICT release is the source of record ([release](https://statsmauritius.govmu.org/Documents/Statistics/ESI/2026/EI1939/ICT_Yr25_100726.pdf)).<br><br>Two regulator decisions bear on what those subscriptions run over: the 700 MHz band opened in December 2025 ([decision](https://www.icta.mu/documents/2025/12/final_decision_700MHz.pdf)), and 3G is being phased out ([communique](https://www.icta.mu/documents/2026/05/communique_sunsetting28May.pdf)).<br><br>This is a first figure with no earlier observation held, so the level is stated and the direction is withheld. The 3G sunset has no switch-off date or migration figure behind it.</details> | No change |
| Morocco | The regulator's own observatory puts the [mobile park at 57.06m and penetration at 154.93 per cent](https://www.anrt.ma/observatoires/donnees-du-secteur/mobile), with the park down slightly year on year and [5G in service](https://www.hespress.com/%D8%A5%D8%B7%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%82-%D8%A3%D9%86%D8%AA%D8%B1%D9%86%D9%8A%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D9%8A%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AE%D8%A7%D9%85%D8%B3-5g-%D9%8A%D8%AF%D8%AE%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%BA-1652175.html) since November 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Independent measurement put median 5G speed down 38% in six months, from an opening figure near 267 Mb/s ([measurement](https://en.bladi.net/morocco-5g-is-already-slowing-down-in-six,123393.html)) — a fall in service quality as subscriber numbers rise.<br><br>The first regulator-published periodic observatory, for the first quarter of 2026, gives a mobile park of 57.06m, down 0.58 per cent year on year, penetration of 154.93 per cent, outgoing traffic down 18.79 per cent and messaging down 9.12 per cent, with sector revenue up 2.89 per cent ([observatory](https://www.anrt.ma/observatoires/donnees-du-secteur/mobile)).<br><br>Three operators launched 5G on the same day in November 2025 on 3.5 GHz and 700 MHz ([launch](https://www.hespress.com/%D8%A5%D8%B7%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%82-%D8%A3%D9%86%D8%AA%D8%B1%D9%86%D9%8A%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D9%8A%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AE%D8%A7%D9%85%D8%B3-5g-%D9%8A%D8%AF%D8%AE%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%BA-1652175.html), [account](https://en.hespress.com/125066-what-5g-could-mean-for-moroccos-economy-and-its-people.html)).<br><br>A dual-band deployment on rail and motorway corridors entered commercial service in 2026 with no additional sites built; the regulator put that operator at 19.4m mobile subscriptions and a 34.04 per cent share at the end of March 2026 ([deployment](https://shore.africa/2026/08/12/inwi-partners-huawei/)).</details> | Mixed, subscriber numbers rose while measured 5G speed fell |
| Mozambique | [5G spectrum was assigned](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/mozambique-incm-awards-5g-licences-to-mobile-operators/) to three operators in June 2026 with a [provincial-capital deadline of June 2027](https://www.diarioeconomico.co.mz/2026/08/25/trends/telecom/mercado-financas-5g-em-todas-as-capitais-provinciais-ate-junho-de-2027/), against [209 of 1,222 localities uncovered](https://www.incm.gov.mz/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Relatorio-de-Regulacao-das-Comunicacoes_2024.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Licences were awarded to the three operators in June 2026 by administrative consignment rather than auction, covering 5G and 4G expansion; one group reports 210 MHz assigned to its Mozambican operator for US$56m with payment and licence terms still being finalised ([award](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/mozambique-incm-awards-5g-licences-to-mobile-operators/), [revenue](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/27/vodafones-africa-business-delivers-double-digit-service-revenue-growth-in-q1-fy27/)).<br><br>The licences carry a deadline of June 2027 for 5G in every provincial capital, with 4G obligations reaching border and rural areas in a later phase; two operators put on record that many target localities have no grid electricity, roads or backhaul ([terms](https://www.diarioeconomico.co.mz/2026/08/25/trends/telecom/mercado-financas-5g-em-todas-as-capitais-provinciais-ate-junho-de-2027/)).<br><br>The regulator's 2024 report has 6 of 453 administrative posts and 209 of 1,222 localities uncovered, at 82.9 per cent locality coverage, with no later figure published ([coverage](https://www.incm.gov.mz/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Relatorio-de-Regulacao-das-Comunicacoes_2024.pdf)).</details> | Mixed, 5G licensed with 2027 coverage deadlines while six administrative posts and 209 localities still have no service at all |
| Namibia | [4G population coverage was 88.4 per cent on 2023 data](https://www.cran.na/download/cran-ict-market-sector-report-nov-2024/), [commercial 5G is live in three centres](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/features/28266-towards-a-5g-enabled-digitalized-namibia-in-2026/), and operators have been [instructed to retire 2G and 3G](https://www.namibiansun.com/local-news/mtc-suggests-smartphone-subsidies-before-2g-3g-shutdown-nmh015578-1-19462). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's series gives 4G population coverage of 85 per cent in 2022, with six of fourteen regions below 80 per cent and one at 41 per cent, 456 of 1,380 schools and 84 of 452 health facilities without 4G coverage, and about 2,000,000 of 2,900,000 mobile cards actively used for internet ([2022](https://www.cran.na/download/cran-ict-market-report-december-2023/)); the 2023 edition gives coverage of 88.4 per cent, fibre to the home at 27 per cent of fixed broadband subscribers and a 6 per cent fall in active cards ([2023](https://www.cran.na/download/cran-ict-market-sector-report-nov-2024/), [release](https://www.cran.na/download/media-release-cran-unveils-2023-ict-sector-report/)). The published series is irregular and no edition reporting 2024 data is held ([series](https://www.cran.na/download/cran-ict-market-sector-report-nov-2024/)).<br><br>Commercial 5G launched at the end of August 2025 in three centres, with nationwide deployment stated as phased and city-first ([5G](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/features/28266-towards-a-5g-enabled-digitalized-namibia-in-2026/)).<br><br>The regulator has instructed operators to retire 2G and 3G and refarm the spectrum; the incumbent asked government to subsidise smartphones before switch-off, and no switch-off date or government response is on file ([retirement](https://www.namibiansun.com/local-news/mtc-suggests-smartphone-subsidies-before-2g-3g-shutdown-nmh015578-1-19462)).</details> | Mixed, 5G live in three centres and 2G and 3G ordered retired while the regulator's own published series stops at 2023 |
| Nigeria | [188m mobile subscriptions and 157m internet subscribers](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/06/telecom-subscriptions-rise-to-188m-as-broadband-penetration-hits-55-67/) sit above [4G population coverage of about 85 per cent](https://ncc.gov.ng/sites/default/files/2025-11/2024-Year-End-Performance-Report.pdf), while a [roaming remedy](https://itweb.africa/article/9mobile-secures-approval-for-roaming-on-mtn-network/KBpdgvpmZDw7LEew) has produced no subscriber recovery. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Two regulator series run side by side: mobile subscriptions at 188m with broadband penetration 55.67 per cent in April 2026, up from 50 per cent in November 2025, and internet subscribers at about 157m in May 2026, up 2.67m in the month ([subscriptions](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/06/telecom-subscriptions-rise-to-188m-as-broadband-penetration-hits-55-67/)); the internet series counts active data-using lines, so reactivation and acquisition are indistinguishable in it ([subscriptions](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/06/telecom-subscriptions-rise-to-188m-as-broadband-penetration-hits-55-67/), [internet](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/04/globacom-leads-nigerias-internet-subscriber-growth-in-may/)).<br><br>Population coverage runs 4G at about 85 per cent and 5G at about 13 per cent against 2030 targets of at least 96 and at least 50 ([coverage](https://ncc.gov.ng/sites/default/files/2025-11/2024-Year-End-Performance-Report.pdf)); 2G reaches 95.31 per cent and 3G 89.42 ([coverage](https://ncc.gov.ng/sites/default/files/2025-11/2024-Year-End-Performance-Report.pdf), [roadmap](https://ncc.gov.ng/sites/default/files/2026-03/Spectrum-Roadmap-for-the-Communications-Sector-2026-to-2030.pdf)).<br><br>Close to a year after national roaming was approved as a remedy, the beneficiary operator was flat at 802,534 internet subscribers for a second month ([roaming](https://itweb.africa/article/9mobile-secures-approval-for-roaming-on-mtn-network/KBpdgvpmZDw7LEew)), while a competitor grew from about 13,000 to about 17,200 sites in three years ([retail](https://techcabal.com/2026/07/22/airtel-nigeria-grows-retail-footprint/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | [Regulator quarterly statistics](https://www.rura.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RURA/Documents/Sectors/ICT/Statistics/Quarterly_publication/ICT_Sector_Statistics_Report_as_of_the_First_Quarter_2026.pdf) carry mobile and internet subscriptions to the first quarter of 2026, and [voice over fourth-generation networks](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/03/mtn-rwanda-rolls-out-volte-to-enhance-mobile-voice-experience/) launched in August 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's quarterly report is the subscription series, and it carries no printed publication date ([statistics](https://www.rura.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RURA/Documents/Sectors/ICT/Statistics/Quarterly_publication/ICT_Sector_Statistics_Report_as_of_the_First_Quarter_2026.pdf)).<br><br>Voice over fourth-generation networks launched in August 2026. It is the technical precondition for retiring circuit-switched voice, though the source makes no reference to the sunset ([voice](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/03/mtn-rwanda-rolls-out-volte-to-enhance-mobile-voice-experience/)).<br><br>The fifth-generation network launched in June 2025 and nothing on the build has entered the base in the fourteen months since ([fifth generation](https://techcabal.com/2025/06/12/rwanda-internet-penetration-rate/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Sao Tome and Principe | The incumbent operator [inaugurated its 35th antenna](https://rstp.st/2026/03/27/cst-inaugura-antena-de-telecomunicacao-em-agua-ize-para-reforco-de-cobertura-nacional/) in March 2026, on a network expansion running alongside a school connectivity plan. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 35th antenna was inaugurated in a coastal district in March 2026 ([antenna](https://rstp.st/2026/03/27/cst-inaugura-antena-de-telecomunicacao-em-agua-ize-para-reforco-de-cobertura-nacional/), [expansion](https://rstp.st/2025/01/25/cst-celebra-35-anos-apostando-na-expansao-da-rede-e-melhoria-da-conectividade-nas-escolas/)).<br><br>No coverage percentage, subscriber count or population-served figure accompanies it, so the antenna count is the only measure of the network the base holds. For a two-island state, a count of masts is a more meaningful measure than it would be elsewhere, and it is still not coverage.</details> | Advanced |
| Senegal | [Rural household internet access](https://www.connectingafrica.com/cybersecurity/senegal-s-free-internet-plan-could-pose-cybersecurity-risks) was stated at end-2025 against fourth-generation coverage reaching 97 per cent of the population, so the shortfall is usage rather than coverage. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The rural figure sits against fourth-generation coverage reaching 97 per cent of the population in 2025, which makes it a usage gap rather than a coverage gap ([access](https://www.connectingafrica.com/cybersecurity/senegal-s-free-internet-plan-could-pose-cybersecurity-risks)).<br><br>That distinction decides what would close it. Coverage obligations and tower financing do not reach a household that is covered and not connected, and the instruments recorded elsewhere in this report — the connectivity project, the device programme, the affordability measures — are the ones that would.</details> | Advanced |
| Seychelles | One operator [launched commercial fifth-generation service](https://nation.sc/articles/31374/airtel-seychelles-launches-5g-network-and-partners-with-sbc-by-vidya-gappy) in July 2026, and the incumbent [launched fifth-generation with a Chinese vendor](https://www.telecompaper.com/news/cable-and-wireless-seychelles-launches-5g-network-with-huawei--1345434) in 2020. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The commercial launch came in July 2026 with a broadcaster content partnership alongside; plan and pricing detail is paywalled and not held ([launch](https://nation.sc/articles/31374/airtel-seychelles-launches-5g-network-and-partners-with-sbc-by-vidya-gappy), [turnkey](https://itweb.africa/article/airtel-seychelles-partners-ericsson-on-turnkey-5g-project/lLn147mjg6bMJ6Aa)).<br><br>The incumbent launched fifth-generation service in 2020 with a Chinese vendor supplying it ([incumbent](https://www.telecompaper.com/news/cable-and-wireless-seychelles-launches-5g-network-with-huawei--1345434)).<br><br>An emergency-communications memorandum signed with a Chinese supplier at a 2025 trade congress, for a resilient mission-critical core integrating with the incumbent's network, has had no update in more than seventeen months ([memorandum](https://www.developingtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/wireless-networks/18097-hytera-and-cws-aim-to-drive-digital-transformation-in-seychelles.html)).</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | The state operator is being [relaunched as a mobile virtual network operator](https://mail.developingtelecoms.com/telecom-business/operator-news/20142-sierratel-to-relaunch-as-an-mvno.html), with a commercial partner appointed in April 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The investment partner was appointed in April 2026 ([relaunch](https://mail.developingtelecoms.com/telecom-business/operator-news/20142-sierratel-to-relaunch-as-an-mvno.html)).<br><br>No launch date, subscriber target, spectrum arrangement or wholesale price is published. A state operator re-entering the market as a virtual operator on a competitor's network is a structural choice the record states without explaining.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | [11.5 million connections at the end of 2025](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-somalia), 58.1 per cent of the population and up 757,000 year on year, with three-quarters on broadband networks. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The most recent figure the base holds puts active cellular connections at 11.5 million against a 19.8 million population, of which 75.7 per cent are classed as broadband, growing 757,000 or 7.0 per cent over the year ([estimate](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-somalia)).<br><br>Connections are not people: no unique-subscriber figure is published, and multi-SIM use in a market with five operators makes the penetration rate an upper bound.<br><br>What the market is now addressing is the handset, through a device-finance scheme targeting 100,000 units by the end of 2026 ([programme](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/hormuudtelecom_somalia-digitalinclusion-smartphones-activity-7457442289615863808-8GfU)).</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | 93.8% of households held a cellphone only in 2025, with 3.7% having no telephone at all ([figures](https://groundup.org.za/article/a-look-at-south-africas-mobile-revolution/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Official household figures charted and published in August 2026 record 93.8% of households holding a cellphone only in 2025, 2.5% holding both a cellphone and a landline, and 3.7% — about 740,000 households — holding no telephone at all, against nearly 55% with none in 2002 ([figures](https://groundup.org.za/article/a-look-at-south-africas-mobile-revolution/)).<br><br>The only subscription movement the base holds for the window is at the resale layer. An analyst house [put active virtual-operator SIMs at about 4.4 million at end-2025, up roughly 43% year on year and forecast to reach 14.4 million by 2030](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/south-africa-shows-africa-how-to-make-mvnos-work/KA3WwMdzpRevrydZ), placing the country first on the continent. One operator separately [reported 5.7 million virtual-operator subscribers on its own wholesale platform for the twelve months to 31 May 2026](https://www.connectingafrica.com/investment/cell-c-gains-1-3m-subscribers-reduces-debt), which it put at an estimated 80 to 85% share.<br><br>The two are not a series and cannot be netted: one counts SIMs across the market on an undisclosed method, the other counts subscribers on a single platform. No regulator return and no operator-reported national subscriber figure appears on this ledger, so growth in resale is visible and total penetration is not.</details> | Advanced |
| South Sudan | A [fifteen-year operator licence](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/14/mtn-secures-15-year-license-to-deepen-digital-footprint-in-south-sudan/) was awarded in April 2025, while a [coverage extension expected from February 2026](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/kauto-leaders-call-for-urgent-expansion-of-mobile-network-coverage) has not been delivered. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The licence was awarded in April 2025 ([licence](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/14/mtn-secures-15-year-license-to-deepen-digital-footprint-in-south-sudan/)), and an operator launched embedded subscriber identity service in January 2025 ([service](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/telecom-operators/4695-mtn-south-sudan-pioneers-esim-technology-redefining-connectivity/)).<br><br>The coverage extension to one administrative area was expected from February 2026 and has not arrived. Operators are reported as citing doubts about revenue from the area, and no operator statement is held ([coverage](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/kauto-leaders-call-for-urgent-expansion-of-mobile-network-coverage)).<br><br>That is the market answer to a low-income rural area with no universal service instrument to override it, and this base holds no such fund for the country.</details> | Mixed, a fifteen-year licence awarded to a second operator while a promised coverage extension went undelivered |
| Sudan | The ministry reports [60 fourth-generation towers built with 200 more planned](https://spokesperson-sd.com/ar/archives/38364) during 2026, and no later source confirms progress against the target. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The tower count and the 2026 target come from the digital ministry's own account, and no later source confirms progress against it ([towers](https://spokesperson-sd.com/ar/archives/38364)).<br><br>The digital-transformation minister separately praised the two operators for keeping services running through the war; that is ministerial praise rather than an independent operational assessment, and no coverage, outage or availability figure accompanies it ([operators](https://www.suna.sd/posts/ozyr-alatsalat-oalthol-alrkmy-yshyd-baldor-almhory-lshrk-sodany-fy-daam-msyr-althol-alrkmy)).<br><br>Every connectivity figure in this unit is the government's or an operator's own, and none is measured by a regulator.</details> | Advanced |
| Tanzania | [Fifth-generation facilities rose 139 per cent](https://dailynews.co.tz/nationwide-5g-rollout-gains-momentum/) from 754 in June 2024 with data consumption up from 426 to 932 petabytes, while the [smartphone user count fell](https://www.thecitizen.co.tz/tanzania/business/telecom-infrastructure-investment-drives-5g-expansion-despite-low-smartphone-uptake-5491080) quarter on quarter as the percentage rose. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's quarterly report to March 2026 carries the facilities and consumption figures ([deployment](https://dailynews.co.tz/nationwide-5g-rollout-gains-momentum/), [expansion](https://www.thecitizen.co.tz/tanzania/business/telecom-infrastructure-investment-drives-5g-expansion-despite-low-smartphone-uptake-5491080)).<br><br>The smartphone user count fell quarter on quarter while the percentage rose and the base does not reconcile the two; the regulator names low smartphone penetration and a 23.7 per cent geographic broadband gap as sector challenges ([smartphones](https://www.thecitizen.co.tz/tanzania/business/telecom-infrastructure-investment-drives-5g-expansion-despite-low-smartphone-uptake-5491080)).<br><br>Active subscriptions and penetration come from the regulator's own quarterly report for the quarter to June 2026, now held first-hand ([subscriptions](https://www.tcra.go.tz/tcra-tovuti/2026/mamlaka-website/documents/quarterly-statistics/Communications_Sector_performance_report_for_quarter_ending_June_2026_ad44159230.pdf), [report](https://dailynews.co.tz/tanzanias-digital-rise-what-the-latest-tcra-report-tells-us/), [regional](https://admin.eaco.int/assets/e774142a-21fd-48e2-bdfd-3309d7d559d7?download=EACO%20Regional%20Communications%20Sector%20Statistics%20Report%202025.pdf)).</details> | Mixed, fifth-generation facilities up 139 per cent while the smartphone user count fell quarter on quarter |
| Togo | [Fifth-generation service on 100 MHz](https://watra.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/WATRA-5G-Deployment-Framework_EN-updated.pdf) has been commercial since 2020, restated in a regional regulators' survey, and a [licence for internet-of-things networks](https://togopresse.tg/les-grandes-decisions-du-conseil-des-ministres-du-4-aout-2026/) was authorised by decree. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The fifth-generation launch date is held at year precision, and both sources are regional reports faceted to West Africa, so a national query misses them ([service](https://watra.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/WATRA-5G-Deployment-Framework_EN-updated.pdf), [report](https://watra.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/EN_WG_ID_WG2-Final-Report.pdf)).<br><br>The internet-of-things licence decree was adopted in August 2026; it authorises the grant rather than recording it, and no licence number, fee, coverage obligation or ownership of the licensee is published ([licence](https://togopresse.tg/les-grandes-decisions-du-conseil-des-ministres-du-4-aout-2026/)).<br><br>No subscriber, coverage or population-served figure for mobile service appears anywhere in this ledger.</details> | No change |
| Tunisia | All three operators [launched fifth-generation service within days of each other](https://www.developingtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/wireless-networks/18000-orange-tunisia-ooredoo-tunisia-and-tunisie-telecom-launch-5g.html) in February 2025, on a market where [mobile broadband is the dominant access point](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/features/28394-whats-behind-north-africas-digital-generation-tunisia/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The three launches came within days in February 2025, and independent measurement of speed a year on is a reference study cited rather than absorbed as country state ([launch](https://www.developingtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/wireless-networks/18000-orange-tunisia-ooredoo-tunisia-and-tunisie-telecom-launch-5g.html), [measurement](https://www.ookla.com/articles/5g-north-africa-2026)).<br><br>Regulator data shows mobile broadband as the dominant access point ([market](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/features/28394-whats-behind-north-africas-digital-generation-tunisia/), [growth](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/11/tunisias-telecom-sector-sees-strong-data-driven-growth/)).<br><br>Three simultaneous launches indicate a coordinated award rather than competitive timing, and no spectrum award document is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | [Active mobile subscriptions](https://itweb.africa/article/uganda-outlines-next-ict-goals/KzQenqjyXWdMZd2r) and [smartphones in use](https://itweb.africa/article/uganda-outlines-next-ict-goals/KzQenqjyXWdMZd2r) both rose on regulator statements, and the regulator states its figures come from licensees' own submissions. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The subscription figure was stated in July 2026, and the regulator states the figures come from licensees' own submissions ([subscriptions](https://itweb.africa/article/uganda-outlines-next-ict-goals/KzQenqjyXWdMZd2r), [report](https://www.ucc.co.ug/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/UCC-Market-Report-for-Q3-2025-Sep-2025.pdf), [submission](https://cipesa.org/wp-content/files/Uganda_UPR_Review.pdf)).<br><br>The smartphone figure's start carries no date ([smartphones](https://itweb.africa/article/uganda-outlines-next-ict-goals/KzQenqjyXWdMZd2r)).<br><br>The leading operator's coverage and fibre footprint come from its own unaudited half-year results, with the base holding no position at the window's opening ([operator](https://itweb.africa/article/mtn-group-opcos-deliver-double-digit-growth/GxwQDM1DPdW7lPVo), [sustainability](https://www.mtn.co.ug/wp-content/uploads/sites/7/2026/08/MTN-Uganda-Sustainability-Report-2025-Digital-1.pdf)). Every connectivity figure in this unit originates with an operator.</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | The technology ministry puts the country at [about 26.2 million mobile subscriptions in 2025 against 23.2 million in 2024](https://www.lusakatimes.com/2026/08/28/govt-to-strengthen-cyber-security-capacity/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The technology ministry put the country at about 26.2 million mobile subscriptions in 2025, up from 23.2 million in 2024, with about 14.7 million of them mobile internet ([ministry](https://www.lusakatimes.com/2026/08/28/govt-to-strengthen-cyber-security-capacity/)). These are the ministry's own figures, given in a seminar address rather than a regulator publication.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | [Fifth-generation base stations](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200058335/internet-traffic-surges-57) rose on first-quarter 2026 sector data, and the state mobile operator's [turnaround plan](https://www.263chat.com/netone-at-30-from-zimbabwes-first-mobile-call-to-a-digital-future/) restated a US$1bn-by-2029 target weeks before its chief executive was terminated. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base-station and rural-coverage figures are the regulator's own ([stations](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200058335/internet-traffic-surges-57), [report](https://technomag.co.zw/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Q4-2025-Abridged-Sector-performance-report-HMed-final-1.pdf), [comparison](https://equityaxis.net/post/19033/2026/4/econet-s-5g-network-now-exceeds-netone-and-telecel-s-combined-infrastructure)).<br><br>The turnaround target was restated at an annual general meeting; revenue figures are operator-reported and the same source states two growth rates that cannot both be right, and the chief executive's contract was terminated in August 2026 ([plan](https://www.263chat.com/netone-at-30-from-zimbabwes-first-mobile-call-to-a-digital-future/), [termination](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200059025/netone-terminates-mushanawanis-contract-ending-turbulent-five-year-tenure)).<br><br>Base stations deployed by the infrastructure company are company-reported for the quarter to May 2026 ([deployment](https://itweb.africa/article/infraco-expands-ai-powered-towers/RgeVDvPRmeoMKJN3)), and a broadband mapping programme is working with operators to identify poor-coverage areas ([mapping](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200055470/govt-defends-starlink-rollout)).</details> | Advanced |

## Internet usage

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | The regulator's 2024 report records [fixed internet at 6,216,146 subscribers, up 12.48% on 619,779 new fibre connections](https://www.arpce.dz/en/file/q6i1l8). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [2024 annual report records 60.98m combined fixed and mobile subscribers at 31 December 2024, of which 54.05m mobile and 6.93m fixed, with 47.30m of the mobile base on 4G against 2.73m on second-generation service, that older base down by a fifth in the year; fixed internet stands at 6,216,146, up 12.48% on 619,779 new fibre connections](https://www.arpce.dz/en/file/q6i1l8).<br><br>It is the only official statistical release held: no quarterly observatory, statistics-office series or payment-instrument report exists. 2026-07-14 - [generalisation of the newer internet addressing standard was ordered, with no completion date](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%D9%87%D9%83%D8%B0%D8%A7-%D8%B3%D9%8A%D8%A4%D8%AB%D8%B1-%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%85%D9%8A%D9%85-%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%84-%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%88%D8%AA%D9%88%D9%83%D9%88%D9%84-ipv6-%D8%B9%D9%84).</details> | Advanced |
| Benin | The regulator's weekly readings placed the state-linked operator ahead of the two international groups on 3G and 4G data speed ([readings](https://www.lanation.bj/numerique/telecoms-debit-internet-3g-4g-les-donnees-de-larcep-placent-celtiis-devant-mtn-et-moov-africa)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's weekly readings placed the state-linked operator ahead of the two international groups on 3G and 4G data speed ([readings](https://www.lanation.bj/numerique/telecoms-debit-internet-3g-4g-les-donnees-de-larcep-placent-celtiis-devant-mtn-et-moov-africa)). The measurement is the regulator's own and no absolute speed is carried here.</details> | Advanced |
| Botswana | Penetration is [put at 81.4% on 2.09m users at end-2025](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/botswana-outlines-new-digital-priorities-under-minister-david-tshere), against a regulator figure of about 73% three weeks earlier, and the two are unreconciled. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-11-26 — [an end-2025 estimate puts internet users at 2.09m and penetration at 81.4%](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/botswana-outlines-new-digital-priorities-under-minister-david-tshere). It is a reference-study figure, and [the regulator is separately reported at about 73% three weeks earlier](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/botswana-outlines-new-digital-priorities-under-minister-david-tshere), with the base reconciling neither against the other.<br><br>No official penetration figure is held for the country at any date — only a venture fund's promotional claim and a reference study — so the eight-point spread between the two numbers in circulation cannot be settled.<br><br>That matters more here than in most countries in this report, because the roadmap reported under strategy is built on an ICT share of output and a jobs target, and the denominator for both is a population whose connection rate the state does not publish.</details> | No change |
| Burkina Faso | [800 towers are planned to connect 1,000 white-zone localities by 2027](https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/burkina-faso-govt-to-build-800-telecom-towers-to-plug-coverage-gaps/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The government plans 800 telecom towers, 250 through the Digital Transformation Acceleration Project and 550 through the Universal Access and Service Fund, to connect 1,000 white-zone localities by 2027](https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/burkina-faso-govt-to-build-800-telecom-towers-to-plug-coverage-gaps/). [The regulator's own figures put attacks on telecom infrastructure at 106 in 2022 against 11 in 2019, cutting network coverage by 10 to 20 per cent](https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/burkina-faso-govt-to-build-800-telecom-towers-to-plug-coverage-gaps/).<br><br>The plan is the largest structural expansion on the record and it has no contract, budget or site list behind it. The coverage the towers would restore was lost to attack, not to underinvestment.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | [12.5 per cent of Burundians were using the internet in early 2025](https://abpinfo.bi/2026/02/28/quand-la-5g-arrive-avant-la-3g-fonctionnelle/), while a fifth-generation network launched commercially in December. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The regulator's coverage figures put 3G at 50 to 54 per cent of the population, 4G at 32 to 34 per cent and 2G at 97 per cent, with only 12.5 per cent of Burundians actually using the internet in early 2025](https://abpinfo.bi/2026/02/28/quand-la-5g-arrive-avant-la-3g-fonctionnelle/), against 80 installed fifth-generation sites after a commercial launch in December 2025. A separate account [cites international union data that nearly 90 per cent of Burundians had no internet access in 2023](https://abpinfo.bi/2026/03/01/le-burundi-ambitionne-detendre-la-4g-aux-zones-rurales-pour-reduire-la-fracture-numerique/).<br><br>[The regulator publishes a quarterly market observatory covering voice, SMS, internet and mobile financial services, its fourth-quarter 2025 edition appearing in April 2026](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/OBSERVATOIRE-4EME-TRIM-2026.pdf) — the standing subscription-based series the indicator would be read from. Coverage and use are the gap this row records: a network reaching most of the population that most of the population does not use.</details> | Advanced, on a low base |
| Cameroon | The regulator's 2024 observatory records [over 15m internet subscriptions, 99% of them mobile, with data traffic up 26%](https://techafricanews.com/2025/12/09/cameroons-telecom-sector-grows-18-in-2024-driven-by-mobile-and-digital-services/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's 2024 annual market observatory records [over 15m internet subscriptions, 99% of them mobile, data traffic up 26%, fixed teledensity described as extremely low, and over 11m active mobile financial services subscribers](https://techafricanews.com/2025/12/09/cameroons-telecom-sector-grows-18-in-2024-driven-by-mobile-and-digital-services/).<br><br>The account held is secondary; the regulator's own report is not. The base holds no comparable dated prior-year subscription figure and no penetration or gender-gap rate at all, so the only movement statable is the traffic growth, and the level is carried as the observatory states it rather than promoted into a current position.</details> | Advanced |
