| Cape Verde | The regulator counted [511,702 internet subscriptions at the end of March 2026, up 1% on the quarter and down 4% on the year](https://www.arme.cv/index.php/noticia-geral/1392-arme-publica-relatorio-indicadores-estatisticos-das-comunicacoes-eletronicas-1o-trimestre-de-2026). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[ARME's Q1 2026 indicators count 511,702 internet access subscriptions, 86% of them delivered over mobile broadband on a small screen, a rise of 1% against the fourth quarter of 2025 and a fall of 4% against the same quarter of 2025 — mobile broadband subscriptions fell 16.4% year on year while fixed broadband rose 12.9%](https://www.arme.cv/index.php/noticia-geral/1392-arme-publica-relatorio-indicadores-estatisticos-das-comunicacoes-eletronicas-1o-trimestre-de-2026). [Market shares by subscription are CVTelecom 72%, Unitel T+ 27.3% and Starlink 0.64%, with Starlink up 0.20 percentage points on the quarter](https://www.arme.cv/index.php/noticia-geral/1392-arme-publica-relatorio-indicadores-estatisticos-das-comunicacoes-eletronicas-1o-trimestre-de-2026).<br><br>The shift from mobile to fixed broadband is the movement in the quarter; the base holds no household or individual-use survey against which to read it.</details> | Mixed, subscriptions up on the quarter and down on the year |
| Central African Republic | Internet use [is put at about 670,000 users, 12.0%, at October 2025](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-central-african-republic), while [the state's higher figures describe coverage rather than use](https://africa24tv.com/republique-centrafricaine-le-taux-de-penetration-dinternet-atteint-30/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-11-08 — [an end-2025 measurement puts users at about 670,000, 12.0%, at October 2025, with 2.49m mobile subscriptions and 38.1% penetration at end-2025](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-central-african-republic), against an earlier international estimate of 13.8% for 2024 and a publisher estimate of 15.5% in January 2025. The publisher reports fewer users against a larger population, so the earlier figure is withdrawn rather than showing a decline.<br><br>[Government statements of about 30% describe network coverage rather than use](https://africa24tv.com/republique-centrafricaine-le-taux-de-penetration-dinternet-atteint-30/), and the two are routinely conflated.<br><br>Every penetration figure held traces to a single international estimate redistributed by intermediaries, and the regulator publishes none — so there is no independent measurer of the most basic number in this report.</details> | No change |
| Comoros | [316,000 people were online at the end of 2025, 35.7 per cent of the population, up 1.9 per cent on the year](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-comoros). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator publishes no series. Its [own homepage, current at the end of August 2026, still shows a single internet-penetration figure of 28 per cent which it dates to 2021](https://www.anrtic.km/), with no methodology and no affordability measure beside it.<br><br>2025-11 - an aggregator working from operator and industry datasets put [316,000 individuals online at the end of 2025, 35.7 per cent of the population, a rise of 5,781 users or 1.9 per cent year on year, against 760,000 cellular connections at 85.7 per cent of population](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-comoros). The gap between connections and users is the country's digital divide stated as a number.<br><br>2025-10 - a [nationally representative survey of Comorian adults, fielded May to December 2024, was catalogued with an inaugural digital connectivity tracker](https://microdata.worldbank.org/catalog/7886); its Comoros figures are not in what the base holds.</details> | No change, a first measured level with a rise of under two per cent behind it |
| Congo | Traffic ran at [90.904bn megabytes on FCFA 63.569bn of revenue in 2024](https://arpce.org/api/publications/rapport-du-marche-linternet-mobile-2024/download), on the regulator's first market report. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2024 market report records [90.904bn megabytes of mobile-internet traffic on FCFA 63.569bn of revenue](https://arpce.org/api/publications/rapport-du-marche-linternet-mobile-2024/download).<br><br>Against that, an industry country report puts [unique mobile-internet use at about 19% against about 86% national coverage](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/22/gsma-report-policy-reforms-could-unlock-fcfa-870-billion-and-144000-jobs-in-republic-of-congo-by-2030/) - a usage gap of some sixty-seven points between where service reaches and who takes it. No household survey, no fixed-broadband count and no gender or income disaggregation exists anywhere on this ledger to explain it.</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | The ministry [put active internet users at nearly 15 million and e-commerce users at about 2 million](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6286/cote-divoire-positions-digital-technology-as-key-driver-of-economic-transformation). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-13 - the ministry [stated nearly 15m active internet users and about 2m e-commerce users, alongside connectivity reaching nearly one million homes](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6286/cote-divoire-positions-digital-technology-as-key-driver-of-economic-transformation).<br><br>No earlier user count on the same definition is held, so what is stated is a level rather than a movement, and nothing on file says how the count is derived or as at what date. The base holds no regulator market statistics later than 2019, which is the gap under every subscriber and usage figure in this report.</details> | Advanced |
| Djibouti | [65.30% of the population used the internet in 2024](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/ITNETUSERP2DJI), the latest observation on file and one predating this period. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The standing series puts internet use at [65.30% of the population in 2024, against 63.78% in 2022 and 59.93% in 2020](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/ITNETUSERP2DJI). No observation dated inside this period has been published, so the figure at both ends of it is the same one.<br><br>2026-03-01 - in his general-policy speech the Prime Minister put [mobile-internet penetration at 56.7%, up from 42.5% five years earlier](https://primature.gouv.dj/discours-de-politique-generale-du-premier-ministre-abdoulkader-kamil-mohamed-a-loccasion-de-la-session-ordinaire-de-lassemblee-nationale-1er-mars-2026/). That is the government's own figure, on a different measure, with no published series behind it.<br><br>Neither the regulator nor the operator publishes a subscription or usage series on file.</details> | No change, the latest observation predates the period |
| DR Congo | Mobile-internet subscriptions stand at [36.98m, 33.9% penetration, with mobile-internet revenue of US$970.2m in 2025](https://admin.eaco.int/assets/e774142a-21fd-48e2-bdfd-3309d7d559d7?download=EACO%20Regional%20Communications%20Sector%20Statistics%20Report%202025.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regional dataset records [36.98m mobile-internet subscriptions at 33.9% penetration and mobile-internet revenue of US$970.2m in 2025, nearly matching voice revenue](https://admin.eaco.int/assets/e774142a-21fd-48e2-bdfd-3309d7d559d7?download=EACO%20Regional%20Communications%20Sector%20Statistics%20Report%202025.pdf).<br><br>The earlier position the base holds is a Council of Ministers record of [30.79% mobile-internet access and under 0.02% fixed access](https://actualite.cd/2025/06/22/rdc-le-gouvernement-appele-redoubler-defforts-pour-reduire-la-fracture-numerique-en), which is close enough to the 2025 figure to say the level is stated rather than a movement measured. Fixed access remains statistically absent, and no household or individual usage survey exists on this ledger.</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | Fixed broadband reached [12.60 million subscriptions in November 2025, growing 8.98% year on year](https://english.ahram.org.eg/NewsContentP/3/562063/Business/Africa-cable-boosts-Egypt-digital-exports-amid-ris.aspx). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base held [11.51 million fixed broadband subscriptions for 2024](https://english.ahram.org.eg/NewsContentP/3/562063/Business/Africa-cable-boosts-Egypt-digital-exports-amid-ris.aspx). By November 2025 there were [12.60 million, up from 12.50 million in October, growing 8.98% annually, with mobile carrying 75.44% of internet access against 72.97% a year earlier](https://english.ahram.org.eg/NewsContentP/3/562063/Business/Africa-cable-boosts-Egypt-digital-exports-amid-ris.aspx).<br><br>The same monthly bulletin records [rising consumer complaints over data caps, upgrade fees and post-cap speed reduction](https://english.ahram.org.eg/NewsContentP/3/562063/Business/Africa-cable-boosts-Egypt-digital-exports-amid-ris.aspx). So subscriptions grow while the terms attached to them draw complaint, and no later monthly figure than November 2025 is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | No national internet-use series exists; the [statistical yearbook](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/inege_presenta_la_octava_edicion_del_anuario_estadistico_nacional_de_guinea_ecuatorial) publishes administrative counts and no penetration figure. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [eighth national yearbook](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/inege_presenta_la_octava_edicion_del_anuario_estadistico_nacional_de_guinea_ecuatorial) carries 19,586 civil servants, 10,468 online visas approved and public debt at 33.3 per cent of output — administrative counts, not connectivity.<br><br>The population it would be measured against is itself estimated: the [2025 compendium still projects population off the 2015 census](https://inege.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/GE-en-cifras-2025-Final.pdf). The [fifth population census now has its legal basis](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/inege_presenta_los_avances_estrategicos_para_el_v_censo_de_poblacion_y_el_i_censo_agropecuario) and has not been taken.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | The regulator [proposed raising the minimum service standard from 2 Mbps to 10 Mbps](https://times.co.sz/39355/technology-today/eswatini-set-for-major-digital-leap/), and [free statutory complaint adjudication](https://times.co.sz/40791/technology-today/consumers-guide-to-type-approved-communications-equipment/) runs on windows of one to six months by sector. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The proposal was issued as a public notice in July 2026 ([proposal](https://times.co.sz/39355/technology-today/eswatini-set-for-major-digital-leap/)).<br><br>Adjudication is free under the 2013 communications Act, with windows of one month for post and courier, three for broadcasting and six for telecommunications; these are structural provisions of the Act rather than dated state ([adjudication](https://times.co.sz/40791/technology-today/consumers-guide-to-type-approved-communications-equipment/)).<br><br>A six-month window for a telecommunications complaint is long enough that the remedy is unlikely to reach the problem it was raised about, and no complaint volume or outcome series is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | Data and internet became the incumbent's largest revenue line in the year to June 2026, at [31.1% against mobile voice at 23.5%](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/52133/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>It was the first financial year in which data and internet, not mobile voice, were the largest share of the incumbent's revenue ([data overtakes voice](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/52133/)).<br><br>Total revenue was 215.8bn birr with operating profit of 92.9bn birr, on the operator's own unaudited figures ([results](https://www.fanamc.com/english/215-8-billion-birr-revenue-and-9-13-trillion-birr-in-digital-transactions-ethio-telecoms-new-economic-footprint/)).<br><br>A streaming service and a zero-touch broadband product launched in February 2026 ([new services](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/23/ethio-telecom-launches-telestream-and-zero-touch-broadband-under-digital-2028-strategy/)).<br><br>No independent measure of internet use is held: the base carries revenue mix and connection counts, not a survey of users.</details> | Advanced |
| Gambia | [1.30m internet users, 45.9 per cent](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-gambia) on a commercial estimate; the statistics office publishes no penetration figure at all. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [October 2025 estimate](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-gambia) puts internet users at 1.30m, 45.9 per cent of the population, against 3.19m mobile connections. It is a commercial estimate, not an official series.<br><br>The official series does not answer the question: the statistics office's [2024 communication statistics](https://www.gbosdata.org/downloads-file/580-communication-statistics-summary-report-2024) publish 3.48m mobile subscribers and no internet penetration figure. A [microdata release on connectivity and financial inclusion](https://microdata.worldbank.org/catalog/7905) in October 2025 supplies survey-based mobile ownership and internet use, which is a different instrument again.</details> | No change, a first estimate with no comparable earlier figure |
| Ghana | Fourth-generation networks [reach more than 90 per cent of the population while usage runs below 60 per cent](https://africa.businessinsider.com/local/markets/ghana-strikes-ai-training-deal-with-chinese-multinational/), on the ministry's own framing. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Many users remain on feature phones, and the gap is the ministry's own statement of the device-affordability problem with no later reading held ([coverage against usage](https://africa.businessinsider.com/local/markets/ghana-strikes-ai-training-deal-with-chinese-multinational/)).<br><br>The last household survey was fielded in June 2019 and published in November 2020, with no repeat round despite a stated biennial intent: 16.8 per cent of households had internet access against 73.8 per cent owning a television, 7.9 per cent of people aged five and over owned a computer of any kind, and smartphone ownership ran at 58.4 per cent urban against 28.1 per cent rural ([household survey](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2021/12/Household-Survey-on-ICT-in-Ghana-Abridged-NRF.pdf), [access gaps](https://www.connectingafrica.com/digital-divide/why-are-parts-of-accra-still-struggling-to-get-online-)).<br><br>An unlimited 100 Mbit/s fibre package fell from GH¢987 to GH¢299 a month, with 300 Mbit/s at GH¢444 and 500 Mbit/s at GH¢999, a cut extracted by ministerial pressure on one operator rather than a licensing change that lets others compete ([tariff cut](https://www.myjoyonline.com/sam-george-announces-major-reduction-in-mtn-fibre-broadband-prices/)).<br><br>Of 78 authorised internet and public-data providers, 42 are operational and 36 are not, and the single authorised rural provider is itself not operational ([provider register](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/Q2-2025-Final-LIST-OF-AUTHORISED-ISPS-IN-GHANA-1.pdf)).</details> | Mixed, coverage and price both moved the right way while measured household access has not been re-read since 2019 |
| Guinea-Bissau | Internet use was [last measured at 32.5% penetration, 723,000 users, with two-thirds of the population offline](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2025-guinea-bissau), before the window opened. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-03-03 — the last measurement the base holds [puts internet users at 723,000 and penetration at 32.5%, leaving 67.5% of the population offline](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2025-guinea-bissau).<br><br>The figure predates the twelve-month window and nothing fresher was published into it: no regulator return, no operator disclosure and no household survey covering internet use appears for Guinea-Bissau. The census reported under Censuses and surveys is the one national data collection inside the window, and its preliminary release carries no connectivity variable.<br><br>So the direction of internet use over the window is unmeasured, at the point where the national backbone is still in design and satellite is the only service to have entered the market.</details> | No change |
| Kenya | [Smartphone connections passed 50m](https://nation.africa/kenya/business/smartphones-hit-50m-basic-handsets-ditched-5502650) for the first time in March 2026, with feature phones falling to 28.5m. <details><summary>Full record</summary>They stood at 48.7m against 29.6m in December 2025 ([devices](https://nation.africa/kenya/business/smartphones-hit-50m-basic-handsets-ditched-5502650)).<br><br>The statistics office's own chapter, a year behind, puts fixed and wireless internet subscriptions at 57.8m in 2024 ([survey](https://www.knbs.or.ke/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/2025-Economic-Survey.pdf)).<br><br>The crossover is the clearest usage signal the base holds, and the regulator publishes it quarterly while the statistics office publishes annually and later.</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | The ministry puts [broadband coverage at 100 per cent against about 50 per cent regular internet use](https://itweb.africa/article/lesotho-pushes-for-equitable-ai-future/VgZeyqJlW957djX9), a gap no programme on file addresses directly. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The communications ministry stated the two figures at the United Nations Global Dialogue on AI Governance in July 2026 ([statement](https://itweb.africa/article/lesotho-pushes-for-equitable-ai-future/VgZeyqJlW957djX9)). No earlier measurement is held, so the level is recorded and the direction is not.<br><br>The one product launched into that gap is a fixed-mobile convergence service, live since July 2025 with nothing later on record ([service](https://lestimes.com/?p=87434)).</details> | No change, a first statement of the gap with no earlier figure behind it |
| Liberia | [1.84m internet users at 32.4 per cent of the population](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2025-liberia) in early 2025, with about 3.8m offline and no later national figure published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The reading is a commercial compilation rather than a regulator or statistics-office release ([penetration](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2025-liberia)).<br><br>Eighteen months on, no regulator subscription series, household survey or statistics-office chapter has replaced it, so the country's connectivity is measured once a year by an outside compiler.</details> | No change |
| Libya | [74 per cent of women use the internet and household internet stands at 92 per cent](https://bsc.ly/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%84%D9%82%D8%B7%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D8%AD%D8%B5%D8%A7%D8%A6%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%B3%D8%AD-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B9%D9%86%D9%82%D9%88%D8%AF%D9%8A-%D9%85%D8%AA%D8%B9%D8%AF%D8%AF-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%A4%D8%B4%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D9%84%D9%8A%D8%A8%D9%8A%D8%A7-2024-2025%D9%85-1.pdf) on the 2024-25 survey. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's own dashboard puts [9.06m internet users and 15.4m mobile lines for 2024](https://www.cim.gov.ly/web/content/30476?download=true&unique=490a0fd903c681dbceed329b5541e896d8ea27dc), a line count well above the population.<br><br>The household survey is the better instrument and gives a different picture: [92 per cent of women using mobile phones, 74 per cent using the internet, household internet at 92 per cent](https://bsc.ly/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%84%D9%82%D8%B7%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D8%AD%D8%B5%D8%A7%D8%A6%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%B3%D8%AD-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B9%D9%86%D9%82%D9%88%D8%AF%D9%8A-%D9%85%D8%AA%D8%B9%D8%AF%D8%AF-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D8%A4%D8%B4%D8%B1%D8%A7%D8%AA-%D9%84%D9%8A%D8%A8%D9%8A%D8%A7-2024-2025%D9%85-1.pdf). The international agency series [carries 88 per cent internet use for 2022 and leaves every household and gender indicator unreported](https://www.itu.int/en/ITU-D/Statistics/Documents/DDD/ddd_LBY.pdf).</details> | No change |
| Madagascar | [18.7 per cent of the population used the internet](https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/MDG/indicator/IT.NET.USER.ZS?date=2024:2025&format=json) in the 2024 reference year, the most recent populated observation. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The World Bank series records 18.7 per cent for the 2024 reference year, with 2025 unpopulated at a refresh of July 2026 ([series](https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/MDG/indicator/IT.NET.USER.ZS?date=2024:2025&format=json)).<br><br>An operator launched a second mobile prefix in June 2026, citing a subscriber base approaching five million active monthly users ([launch](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/22/airtel-madagascar-launches-new-035-prefix-to-support-network-growth/)).<br><br>The regulator published four-region 4G population coverage figures for the south in September 2025, showing uneven expansion ([coverage](https://fr.linkedin.com/posts/autorit%C3%A9-de-r%C3%A9gulation-des-technologies-de-communication_artecvousinforme-artecleschiffres-cartor%C3%A9seau-activity-7374409542408855552-38_A)). This is a first usage figure in the base, with no earlier observation to compare it against.</details> | No change |
| Malawi | [80 per cent of Malawians are offline](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/20/gsma-malawi-digital-transformation-policy-action/) despite 87 per cent fourth-generation coverage, and no state measurement is held against the target of 80 per cent usage by 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The appraisal document put internet use at around 24 per 100 inhabitants on a 2022 baseline against a regional average of 28 ([appraisal](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099050524041520240/txt/P5050951dae66d0e1bc631717e7e7a4e70.txt)), and a separate account put access at about 20 per cent of citizens ([access](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202405/malawi-on-path-to-inclusion-with-digital-id-project-supported-by-eu-undp)).<br><br>The first usable current figure comes from the operators' trade association, which puts 80 per cent of the population offline against 87 per cent coverage, so the gap is usage rather than coverage, and estimates MWK 1.1 trillion of economic value and 490,000 jobs by 2030 from closing it ([report](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/20/gsma-malawi-digital-transformation-policy-action/)). That is a modelled figure from an interested party whose policy asks include cutting the taxes its members pay.<br><br>The presidential target of 80 per cent usage by 2026 is a target and not a measurement, with no baseline given ([target](https://itweb.africa/article/malawi-president-orders-digital-revolution/kYbe9MXbob9vAWpG)).<br><br>A national survey published in December 2025 puts seven in ten adults as never using the internet and gives a penetration rate of 27.7 per cent, the first non-industry current measurement the base holds ([survey](https://www.afrobarometer.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/AD1094-Leave-no-Malawian-offline-Understanding-the-digital-divide-Afrobarometer-17dec25.pdf)).</details> | No change, four fifths of the population offline with no state measurement to set against the presidential target |
| Mauritius | Internet usage is [reported annually](https://statsmauritius.govmu.org/Documents/Statistics/ESI/2026/EI1939/ICT_Yr25_100726.pdf) alongside household penetration indicators, with no earlier figure held here. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2025 ICT statistics carry usage and household penetration together ([release](https://statsmauritius.govmu.org/Documents/Statistics/ESI/2026/EI1939/ICT_Yr25_100726.pdf)), on the household indicator series the promotion agency publishes ([indicators](https://indicators.govmu.org/ictindicators/?page_id=3218)).<br><br>Spectrum for mobile broadband was released in June 2026 ([decision](https://www.icta.mu/documents/2026/06/decision_imt_040626.pdf)).<br><br>A first observation with no earlier one on this ledger. What the base does not hold is a rural-urban split, a gender split, or usage by income.</details> | No change |
| Morocco | [5G reached 3.2m subscribers](https://www.ookla.com/articles/5g-north-africa-2026) by March 2026, 8.4 per cent of mobile-internet users, from 2.63m at the end of 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's figures, as reported by a measurement firm, give 3.2m 5G subscribers at March 2026 and 8.4 per cent of mobile-internet users, up from 2.63m and 38 per cent outdoor population coverage at the fourth quarter of 2025 ([figures](https://www.ookla.com/articles/5g-north-africa-2026)).<br><br>Morocco is the only market in that comparison publishing a 5G subscriber count, which is itself the finding: the number exists here and nowhere else in the set.</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | A [public broadband coverage portal](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/telecom/mozambique-un-portail-national-du-haut-debit-pour-etendre-la-couverture-telecoms) went live in July 2026 letting anyone query operator coverage and speeds by location, with a public gap-reporting channel. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The geospatial platform was built with commercial mapping software and publishes operator coverage and speeds queryable by location, with a channel for the public to report gaps ([portal](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/telecom/mozambique-un-portail-national-du-haut-debit-pour-etendre-la-couverture-telecoms)).<br><br>No usage or gap-report figures have been published. The portal maps what is available rather than what is used, and no national internet penetration figure is published alongside it.</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | Internet penetration was [62.2 per cent at January 2024](https://mict.gov.na/documents/869282/6089169/MICT_2025_2026_Budget_Speech+Full+Version.pdf/9b15c163-e790-96b8-f1af-ebd9573367b5?download=true&t=1746624578199), and the share of subscriber modules used for internet rose from 61 to 63 per cent in the third quarter of 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ICT ministry's budget motivation puts penetration at 62.2 per cent and about 1.63 million users at January 2024, with 4G population coverage at 88.4 per cent against 85 per cent in 2022 — 98 and 99 per cent in the strongest urban regions against 49 per cent in Kunene ([motivation](https://mict.gov.na/documents/869282/6089169/MICT_2025_2026_Budget_Speech+Full+Version.pdf/9b15c163-e790-96b8-f1af-ebd9573367b5?download=true&t=1746624578199)).<br><br>In the third quarter of 2025 active modules rose 3 per cent, mobile broadband usage 6 per cent and dongle and router subscriptions 30 per cent, while fixed-line internet subscriptions fell 2 per cent ([quarterly](https://nbcnews.na/node/114190)).<br><br>The universal service fund awarded the state fixed operator N$9,796,650 in April 2026 to deploy infrastructure at sites in underserved rural areas ([fund](https://www.telecom.na/media-centre/894-telecom-namibia-and-cran-formally-sign-universal-service-fund-agreement-to-enhance-rural-connectivity)).<br><br>Rural households remain the gap: 16.2 per cent had mobile internet at November 2025 ([rural](https://cadeproject.org/updates/apc-report-highlights-digital-rights-challenges-in-namibia-ahead-of-upr-review/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | [Fibre overtook fixed wireless](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/25/fibre-is-changing-nigerias-internet-now-comes-the-hard-part/) for the first time with median fixed download at 34.47 Mbps, and [rural medians rose](https://www.ncc.gov.ng/market-data-reports/network-performance-report) on the regulator's crowdsourced measurement. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Fibre-to-the-premises connections reached 265,000 by June 2026 from 84,141 at the end of 2025, the median fixed download speed rose 155 per cent to 34.47 Mbps, uploads nearly doubled and latency fell ([mix](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/25/fibre-is-changing-nigerias-internet-now-comes-the-hard-part/)); the gain sits at the bottom of the distribution, the slowest decile improving from 2.24 to 6.04 Mbps while the fastest moved only from 74.37 to 90.04 ([mix](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/25/fibre-is-changing-nigerias-internet-now-comes-the-hard-part/)).<br><br>Rural median download rose from 15.0 to 16.4 Mbps and rural latency fell, on a test base growing from 266,970 to 543,123 ([measurement](https://www.ncc.gov.ng/market-data-reports/network-performance-report)); only 31.4 per cent of 5G-capable devices in the capital territory and 27.5 in Lagos actually use 5G, unchanged over six months, with a negative promoter score for every carrier ([measurement](https://www.ncc.gov.ng/market-data-reports/network-performance-report), [geospatial](https://www.ncc.gov.ng/sites/default/files/2026-01/Urban-vs-Rural-Analysis.pdf)).<br><br>One operator is live in five cities with six further states named for launch before the end of 2026 ([footprint](https://businessday.ng/technology/article/fiberone-expands-to-seven-nigerian-states-steps-up-community-investment/)), and the largest operator is building toward a target of 8m fibre homes by 2028 ([homes](https://techafricanews.com/2025/10/10/mtn-nigeria-unveils-plan-to-connect-8-million-homes-with-fibre-network-by-2028/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | An international body put [mid-2025 internet penetration at 38 per cent](https://techcabal.com/2025/06/12/rwanda-internet-penetration-rate/) with end-2025 projected at 42.6, and the most recent figure the base holds is a projection rather than a measurement. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Penetration was measured at 38 per cent in mid-2025 and 42.6 per cent for end-2025 is a projection ([penetration](https://techcabal.com/2025/06/12/rwanda-internet-penetration-rate/), [masterclass](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/reports-and-coverage/13149-rwandas-masterclass-in-digital-penetration-a-use-case-for-african-counterparts/)).<br><br>A household survey reported in June 2025 records the urban and rural internet use gap, and no successor survey is held ([gap](https://techcabal.com/2025/06/12/rwanda-internet-penetration-rate/)).<br><br>Cost is the constraint both sources name, and neither carries a tariff series against which affordability could be tracked.</details> | Advanced |
| Senegal | The incumbent operator [committed to one million new fibre sockets](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/22/sonatel-expands-fiber-and-mobile-data-base-as-ftth-subscribers-top-640000/) between 2026 and 2028, and a [mobile latency measurement](https://www.seneweb.com/fr/news/Technologie/connectivite-le-senegal-2e-en-afrique-subsaharienne-pour-la-latence-mobile-avec-354-ms_n_498911.html) covering January to July 2026 is the first the base holds. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The fibre commitment was set out with full-year 2025 results in April 2026 ([results](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/22/sonatel-expands-fiber-and-mobile-data-base-as-ftth-subscribers-top-640000/)).<br><br>The latency measurement covers January to July 2026 and no prior figure for the country is held, so it establishes a baseline rather than a direction ([latency](https://www.seneweb.com/fr/news/Technologie/connectivite-le-senegal-2e-en-afrique-subsaharienne-pour-la-latence-mobile-avec-354-ms_n_498911.html)).<br><br>Both figures are the operator's and a measurement firm's; no regulator series on quality of service is held for this unit.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | [Internet use and price](https://www.hiiraan.com/news4/2025/Oct/203323/somalia_s_internet_users_soar_to_10_7_million_as_starlink_and_digital_strategy_fuel_expansion.aspx) were reported in October 2025, and the same account ranks the country 191st on online government services. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The figures establish a baseline the base did not previously hold ([use](https://www.hiiraan.com/news4/2025/Oct/203323/somalia_s_internet_users_soar_to_10_7_million_as_starlink_and_digital_strategy_fuel_expansion.aspx)).<br><br>The ranking on online government services sits alongside them in the same account, which is the useful pairing: connectivity and price are one problem, and what a connected person can actually do with government is another.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | The regulator [puts household internet access at 82.1% and fixed internet at home at 17.4%](https://www.icasa.org.za/uploads/files/The-State-of-the-ICT-Sector-Report-of-South-Africa-31-March-2026.pdf), with fourth-generation coverage at 99.5%. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-03-31 — the sector report [puts fourth-generation population coverage at 99.5% and fifth-generation at 58.0%, household internet access at 82.1% and fixed internet at home at 17.4%, with 4,377 of 21,878 government facilities connected as at October 2025](https://www.icasa.org.za/uploads/files/The-State-of-the-ICT-Sector-Report-of-South-Africa-31-March-2026.pdf), against [the prior year's edition](https://www.icasa.org.za/uploads/files/The-State-of-the-ICT-Sector-Report-of-South-Africa-2025.pdf).<br><br>Two of those figures are the finding. The gap between household access and fixed access at home says almost all use is mobile, which sets what a household can do with it. And a fifth of government facilities connected, in a country with near-universal mobile coverage, locates the problem in the state's own estate rather than in the network.<br><br>It is the only regulator-published usage series on this ledger.</details> | Advanced |
| South Sudan | [Internet users and penetration](https://www.eyeradio.org/nca-reports-huge-internet-growth-in-south-sudan/) were reported by the regulator in July 2025, and no later national measurement is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The figures are the regulator's own ([figures](https://www.eyeradio.org/nca-reports-huge-internet-growth-in-south-sudan/)).<br><br>Thirteen months without a further measurement, in a period covering two tariff adjustments and a licence award, means the direction of use since then is unknown.</details> | Advanced |
| Sudan | [14.9 million users at 28.7 per cent penetration](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-sudan) in October 2025, up 407,000 year on year; the official series has [no observation since 2017](https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/SDN/indicator/IT.NET.USER.ZS?format=json&per_page=100). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The commercial estimate for October 2025 puts users at 14.9 million, up 407,000 or 2.8 per cent year on year, with penetration unchanged at 28.7 per cent ([estimate](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-sudan)).<br><br>The official indicator series for Sudan was last updated on 13 July 2026 but its most recent non-null observation is 18.6 per cent for 2017, with every year since null ([series](https://api.worldbank.org/v2/country/SDN/indicator/IT.NET.USER.ZS?format=json&per_page=100)).<br><br>Usage is also being displaced rather than simply grown: during the April 2026 exam shutdowns one satellite provider reached 27 per cent of the internet-service market ([report](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/blog/2026/04/its-exam-season-in-sudan-and-that-means-internet-shutdowns/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Tanzania | The state operator [reset its fibre-to-the-home target to 2028](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/0605-55286-tanzania-s-state-telecom-targets-1-million-fiber-subscribers-by-2028) with about 200,000 of 280,000 financial-year connections delivered, and a [second operator launched fibre service](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/20/yas-tanzania-brings-fibre-connectivity-to-homes-businesses-in-dodoma/) in the capital. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The target slipped to 2028 with roughly seven-tenths of the year's connections delivered; the event date is not stated so the publication date stands, and subscriber ranks are regulator data to end-March 2026 ([target](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/0605-55286-tanzania-s-state-telecom-targets-1-million-fiber-subscribers-by-2028)).<br><br>A second operator launched fibre-to-the-home and business service in the capital in August 2026 ([launch](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/20/yas-tanzania-brings-fibre-connectivity-to-homes-businesses-in-dodoma/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | [Internet adoption has been flat across two annual readings](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-zambia), and [mobile connections](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-zambia) count connections rather than people. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The adoption reading is cited rather than absorbed, and has been flat across two annual compilations ([users](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-zambia), [prior](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2025-zambia)).<br><br>Mobile connections are connections, not people, and the regulator's separate administrative series counts subscriptions per hundred inhabitants ([connections](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2026-zambia)).<br><br>A median fixed broadband download reading is likewise cited and not absorbed ([speed](https://datareportal.com/reports/digital-2025-zambia)). Every usage figure in this unit comes from a foreign compiler rather than the national regulator.</details> | No change |
| Zimbabwe | [Internet penetration](https://technomag.co.zw/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Q4-2025-Abridged-Sector-performance-report-HMed-final-1.pdf) is active subscriptions divided by population rather than users, against a private tracker measuring 38.4 per cent of individuals, and [wireless became the majority fixed technology](https://www.newsday.co.zw/theindependent/business-digest/article/200055625/over-50-of-zims-fixed-broadband-now-wireless). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's rate divides active subscriptions by population and is not a measure of individual use ([penetration](https://technomag.co.zw/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Q4-2025-Abridged-Sector-performance-report-HMed-final-1.pdf), [roadmap](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/17/zimbabwe-unveils-digital-roadmap-to-accelerate-msme-growth/)).<br><br>Wireless became the majority fixed-broadband technology during the third quarter of 2025 ([mix](https://www.newsday.co.zw/theindependent/business-digest/article/200055625/over-50-of-zims-fixed-broadband-now-wireless)), and internet and data traffic rose on first-quarter 2026 figures given at a post-Cabinet briefing ([traffic](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200058335/internet-traffic-surges-57)).<br><br>A preferential buyer's credit for fixed broadband expansion ended in 2019; its title states US$98.6m and its deal record carries US$110,675,991, with no conversion basis on either ([credit](https://www.facebook.com/permalink.php?story_fbid=2059012574323134&id=2049750908582634&substory_index=0#)).</details> | Advanced |

## Mobile affordability

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | The regulator published [a decision on the interconnection catalogue](https://www.arpce.dz/fr/file/f5s5z5); no retail tariff or basket figure is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The wholesale side is regulated and on the record: [the regulator's bulletin of October 2025 carries a decision on the interconnection catalogue among at least a dozen decisions taken between June and October 2025](https://www.arpce.dz/fr/file/f5s5z5), and [the incumbent's fixed-network licence was renewed by executive decree with its annexed specification](https://www.arpce.dz/fr/file/g1v2e1).<br><br>The retail side is not. No mobile tariff, data-basket price, price index or affordability measure against income appears anywhere on this ledger, so what a subscriber pays cannot be stated.</details> | No change, a first stated position with no earlier account behind it |
| Benin | Data prices [fell from 4,000 to 995 FCFA a gigabyte between 2016 and 2023](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/28/benin-enters-the-5g-era-as-moov-africa-benin-launches-high-speed-network-at-novotel-orisha-cotonou/), and no measurement since is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Mobile data fell from 4,000 FCFA a gigabyte in 2016 to 995 in 2023](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/28/benin-enters-the-5g-era-as-moov-africa-benin-launches-high-speed-network-at-novotel-orisha-cotonou/). The series ends three years before the window opens, so the direction of prices across the window is unmeasured.<br><br>No tariff schedule, basket measure or regulator price return appears anywhere on this ledger, and the second fifth-generation launch reported above came with no published pricing. [The regional declaration Benin signed commits to 90% affordable broadband by 2030](https://lanation.bj/actualites/transformation-numerique-en-afrique-de-louest-et-du-centre-les-dirigeants-sengagent-a-oeuvrer-pour-des-progres-tangibles) without defining affordable, and no baseline against that commitment exists here.</details> | No change |
| Botswana | The incumbent's prepaid data tariffs [were cut with regulator approval on a pricing study](https://www.mmegi.bw/business/btc-reduces-data-prices-following-regulator-approval/news), and [a device-financing facility has published no uptake in fifteen months](https://www.sundaystandard.info/mascom-launches-myzaka-flexi-botswanas-first-mobile-device-financing-facilty/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-02-18 — [the regulator approved revised prepaid tariffs with immediate effect on a May 2025 pricing study: 1.5GB a day for P10, 4GB a month for P65, 30GB over 30 days for P199 and 100GB over 30 days for P349](https://www.mmegi.bw/business/btc-reduces-data-prices-following-regulator-approval/news). The other two operators revised earlier under the same study and their schedules are not held, so the market-wide effect cannot be stated.<br><br>The device side has not moved. [The country's first mobile device financing facility launched in May 2025](https://www.sundaystandard.info/mascom-launches-myzaka-flexi-botswanas-first-mobile-device-financing-facilty/) and has published no uptake, portfolio or default figure in the fifteen months since.<br><br>A regulator-driven price cut with a published schedule is unusual in this report; what is missing is any measure of what it changed.</details> | Advanced |
| Burkina Faso | Consumer rules [became enforceable after a court ruling, extending balance validity to 180 days](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/telecom/burkina-faso-tightens-telecom-regulations-but-affordability-concerns-persist), with the regulator reporting standard mobile-internet prices down 28 to 45%. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-12-02 — [rules adopted in 2023 but suspended and unenforceable became enforceable after a court ruling: main-balance validity extended from 90 to 180 days, promotional credit usable across networks and a minimum one-month validity on data bundles, with the regulator reporting standard mobile-internet prices down 28 to 45%](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/telecom/burkina-faso-tightens-telecom-regulations-but-affordability-concerns-persist). What consumers had asked for was lower prices, which is not what the rules address; the price movement is reported alongside them rather than attributed to them.<br><br>2026 — [an interconnection catalogue for 2026 to 2028 is in force with call-termination tariffs revised](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f), known only through an operator's half-year filing. The catalogue itself is not held, so the wholesale terms underneath retail prices reach this base through the accounts of a party to them.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | Internet package tariffs [have more than doubled since January 2025, in some cases tripling](https://www.sosmediasburundi.org/2026/07/31/telecoms-en-crise-au-burundi-labuco-denonce-linaction-des-autorites-face-a-la-degradation-des-services/), on the consumer association's account. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-31 — [the consumer association reported that internet package tariffs have more than doubled since January 2025, in some cases doubling or tripling, alongside degradation of service](https://www.sosmediasburundi.org/2026/07/31/telecoms-en-crise-au-burundi-labuco-denonce-linaction-des-autorites-face-a-la-degradation-des-services/).<br><br>The account is the association's own. No regulator tariff series, operator price schedule or basket measure appears anywhere on this ledger, so the direction is reported by the party least likely to understate it and there is nothing to test it against.<br><br>Read beside it, the satellite alternative is also out of reach on cost, reported under satellite broadband, and the network people are paying more for is the one the regulator itself describes as degraded.</details> | Regressed |
| Central African Republic | Satellite service [costs about 136% of monthly income per head, at FCFA 33,000 a month plus tax with a kit at FCFA 240,000](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/1703-136713-starlink-en-centrafrique-entre-couverture-nationale-et-defis-d-accessibilite), against a 2% international threshold. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-03-16 — [commercial service opened at FCFA 33,000 a month plus tax with a standard kit at FCFA 240,000, a monthly price of about 136% of monthly income per head against an international affordability threshold of 2%](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/1703-136713-starlink-en-centrafrique-entre-couverture-nationale-et-defis-d-accessibilite). The object advanced and affordability did not, and [the state's response was to suspend non-homologated kits](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/28/central-african-republic-suspends-starlink-kits-citing-security-and-equipment-traceability-requirements/) rather than to address price.<br><br>That is the only published price of any kind on this ledger. No mobile tariff, data basket, handset price or regulator price schedule appears for the country at any date, so a service costing more than a month's income per head is the country's one affordability datapoint.</details> | No change |
| Comoros | The entry fibre offer moved to [KMF 30,000 a month at 850 GB](https://alwatwan.net/societe/télécommunications-i-comores-télécom-lance-de-nouvelles-offres.html), from KMF 32,500 at 800 GB. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The basic fibre offer stood at KMF 32,500 a month for 800 GB, and is now [KMF 30,000 a month at 850 GB with unlimited fixed-line calls, plus embedded subscriber-identity migration at KMF 5,000 and a new one at KMF 10,000](https://alwatwan.net/societe/télécommunications-i-comores-télécom-lance-de-nouvelles-offres.html).<br><br>It is operator-announced, the source's own dateline is internally inconsistent and the date precision is flagged uncertain. No mobile tariff, basket or affordability measure exists anywhere on this ledger, so one incumbent's fixed offer is the whole of the price evidence for the country.</details> | Advanced |
| Congo | Backhaul spectrum fees run at [about 5% of operators' total revenue against a sub-Saharan median of 0.5%](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/16/why-connecting-rural-congo-costs-three-times-more-than-it-should/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>An industry country report puts [mobile backhaul spectrum fees at about 5% of operators' total revenue against a sub-Saharan median of 0.5%, alongside delayed spectrum assignments, cross-border interference and technology-neutrality restrictions](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/16/why-connecting-rural-congo-costs-three-times-more-than-it-should/).<br><br>Backhaul is the default rural link where fibre does not reach, so the premium falls hardest where coverage is weakest, and no regulatory response is on file. The only retail price the base holds is a [weighted FCFA 0.70 a megabyte in 2024](https://arpce.org/api/publications/rapport-du-marche-linternet-mobile-2024/download), with nothing later to compare it against.</details> | No change, a first published price position with nothing earlier behind it |
| Cote d'Ivoire | Observed mean prices [fell 39.9% for voice, 28.9% for SMS and 58.8% for mobile internet](https://www.economie-ivoirienne.ci/activites-sectorielles/telecommunication.html) between 2020 and early 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The official series distinguishes wholesale interconnection caps, displayed retail tariffs and prices drawn from actual consumption including bonuses and promotions. On that last basis it [runs from FCFA 10.06 a voice minute, 1.32 an SMS and 0.80 a megabyte in 2020 to FCFA 6.04, 0.94 and 0.33 by the first quarter of 2025 - falls of 39.9%, 28.9% and 58.8% - with wholesale interconnection rates down 77% for voice and 87% for SMS over the same period](https://www.economie-ivoirienne.ci/activites-sectorielles/telecommunication.html).<br><br>The same page [gives 2024 mobile penetration as 172.2% on 53,601,479 active SIM cards and mobile internet penetration as 93.7%](https://www.economie-ivoirienne.ci/activites-sectorielles/telecommunication.html).<br><br>What is missing is the denominator affordability turns on: no basket cost as a share of income, and no distributional figure, appears anywhere on this ledger.</details> | Advanced |
| Djibouti | [No national price figure is held](https://www.itu.int/dms_pub/itu-d/opb/ind/d-ind-ict_mdd-2025-3-pdf-e.pdf); the [first authorisation to a competing operator was granted in March 2026](https://www.armd.dj/en/blog/armd-grants-authorization-to-netcatonline-73). <details><summary>Full record</summary>This is a searched absence. The telecommunications union's [annual reference publication introduces a data-only mobile broadband basket and reports a global median of 1.4% of income per head, with 130 of 205 economies inside the 2% benchmark](https://www.itu.int/dms_pub/itu-d/opb/ind/d-ind-ict_mdd-2025-3-pdf-e.pdf); the country's own basket figure sits in the union's price database rather than in the report, and is not held here.<br><br>What did move is the market. 2026-03-22 - the regulator [authorised a new entrant to build and operate a national wired electronic-communications network, market internet access and data transmission, and access the incumbent's landing stations](https://www.armd.dj/en/blog/armd-grants-authorization-to-netcatonline-73), the first such authorisation against a [statutory monopoly](https://www.lanation.dj/du-hub-regional-au-catalyseur-continental-la-nouvelle-ere-de-djibouti-telecom/). No tariff effect is on record.</details> | Advanced, on market opening rather than on any published price |
| DR Congo | Bundle validity was cut without notice: [1GB at 1,500 francs fell from 48 hours to 24](https://laprunellerdc.cd/bukavu-des-usagers-denoncent-la-modification-des-tarifs-des-forfaits-internet/), and the 2GB bundle was replaced. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Subscribers in Bukavu and the eastern provinces report that [a 1GB bundle at 1,500 Congolese francs, formerly valid 48 hours, is now valid 24 hours, and a 2GB bundle at 2,000 francs valid 48 hours has been replaced by 1GB valid 72 hours at the same price](https://laprunellerdc.cd/bukavu-des-usagers-denoncent-la-modification-des-tarifs-des-forfaits-internet/).<br><br>Both ends are [subscribers' and resellers' accounts](https://radiomaendeleo.org/forfaits-internet-les-abonnes-denoncent-une-hausse-des-prix-et-une-reduction-de-la-validite/); no operator or regulator statement is on record and the changes are reported as unannounced. It is the only affordability evidence on this ledger, and it comes from the same eastern provinces where service has been cut outright for months at a time.</details> | Regressed |
| Egypt | A [new mobile package at EGP 5 replaces one at about EGP 13](https://www.tra.gov.eg/en/ntra-approves-new-packages-for-digital-inclusion-and-price-adjustment-of-some-services-2/) while other packages rise 9 to 15%. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05-06 - [a new mobile package at EGP 5 replaces a lowest package of about EGP 13, and a fixed package at EGP 150 replaces one at EGP 210, while other packages rise 9 to 15 per cent including taxes](https://www.tra.gov.eg/en/ntra-approves-new-packages-for-digital-inclusion-and-price-adjustment-of-some-services-2/).<br><br>The cheapest entry point falls and the rest of the ladder goes up, which is a distributional change rather than a price cut, and the regulator frames it as digital inclusion.<br><br>No data-basket price, no price index and no affordability measure against income appears anywhere on this ledger, so what the adjustment means for a typical subscriber cannot be stated. The regulator's [consultation framework](https://www.tra.gov.eg/wp-content/uploads/2021/06/Regulatory-Frame-on-the-Rules-and-Measures-of-Public-Consultations-003.pdf) is the process behind decisions of this kind.</details> | Mixed, the cheapest entry package fell while the rest of the ladder rose |
| Equatorial Guinea | The state operator [raised prepaid data volumes 50 per cent at held prices](https://ahoraeg.com/economia/empresa/2026/08/14/getesa-aumenta-un-50-el-volumen-de-datos-en-todos-sus-paquetes-prepago-durante-el-verano/) in August 2026, its top tier reaching 24GB for 15,000 CFA. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Price last moved by agreement rather than regulation: [the government and operators halved internet tariffs from 1 September 2022](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/gobierno_y_empresas_telefonicas_acuerdan_reducir_a_la_mitad_el_precio_de_internet).<br><br>In the window the movement was volume: a [summer promotion raising data 50 per cent across all prepaid packages at held prices, the top tier at 24GB for 15,000 CFA](https://ahoraeg.com/economia/empresa/2026/08/14/getesa-aumenta-un-50-el-volumen-de-datos-en-todos-sus-paquetes-prepago-durante-el-verano/). Upstream, the regulator [updated the primary and secondary broadband capacity market regime by ministerial order in February 2026](https://ortelge.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Orden-Ministerial-Numero-12026-de-fecha-13-de-1.pdf). No affordability ratio against income is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Eritrea | An entry-level mobile broadband basket costs 85% of monthly income per head, against a 2% affordability benchmark ([country tables](https://www.itu.int/dms_pub/itu-d/opb/ind/D-IND-ICT_MDD-2023-PDF-E.pdf)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Country-table data on 2022 pricing records Eritrea's entry-level mobile broadband basket at 85% of monthly gross national income per head, against the international affordability benchmark of 2% ([country tables](https://www.itu.int/dms_pub/itu-d/opb/ind/D-IND-ICT_MDD-2023-PDF-E.pdf)).<br><br>That is more than forty times the benchmark, and no later Eritrea-specific figure was located at the probe date, so the base cannot say whether it has moved since 2022.<br><br>The level is stated and the direction is withheld, because there is nothing to compare it against.</details> | No change, a first measurement with no earlier figure behind it |
| Eswatini | The [cost of data as a share of income per head](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/04/eswatini-ramps-up-tech-reform-with-new-legislation-5g-rollout-and-coding-training-for-all/) sits above the international affordability threshold. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The finding is stated in an August 2025 account and nothing has moved it since ([affordability](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/04/eswatini-ramps-up-tech-reform-with-new-legislation-5g-rollout-and-coding-training-for-all/)).<br><br>It is the constraint under the usage gap recorded separately — mobile coverage substantially exceeds mobile-internet use — and no tariff instrument, subsidy or device-financing scheme appears anywhere in this ledger to address it.</details> | No change |
| Ethiopia | The second operator raised data prices by 20 to 82 percent from December 2025 ([report](https://capitalethiopia.com/2025/12/28/safaricom-ethiopia-faces-backlash-over-sharp-data-price-hike/)) while a US$40 smartphone pilot named Ethiopia ([pilot](https://www.gsma.com/newsroom/press-release/pioneering-affordable-access-in-africa-gsma-and-handset-affordability-coalition-members-identify-six-african-countries-to-pilot-affordable-40-smartphones/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Safaricom Ethiopia revised its data packages from 23 December 2025, raising prices by between 20 and 82 percent across most plans while cutting data volumes on several: the weekly 10GB bundle at 250 birr was reduced to 5.5GB, and the 100 birr weekly package to 2GB from 3GB ([report](https://capitalethiopia.com/2025/12/28/safaricom-ethiopia-faces-backlash-over-sharp-data-price-hike/)). The operator cited operational and investment costs, the birr devaluation and prior below-cost pricing.<br><br>Ethiopia was named one of six African countries for a 2026 pilot of entry-level 4G smartphones in the US$30-40 range by the handset affordability coalition ([pilot](https://www.gsma.com/newsroom/press-release/pioneering-affordable-access-in-africa-gsma-and-handset-affordability-coalition-members-identify-six-african-countries-to-pilot-affordable-40-smartphones/)).<br><br>The most recent market assessment is from July 2025 ([assessment](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099821508252526233/txt/IDU-a026d983-6657-497a-8b99-5b873649b929.txt)); its affordability figures are not in the captured text, so the base holds no price-to-income measure against which to size the increase.</details> | Mixed, service prices rose sharply at the second operator while a device-affordability pilot was extended to Ethiopia |
| Gambia | [D75 per gigabyte from 1 January 2026](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/headlines/pura-sets-mobile-data-at-d75-per-gigabyte-in-latest-telecom-pricing-decision), the regulated floor price ended and data rollover introduced. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [final tariff order](https://thepoint.gm/africa/gambia/headlines/pura-sets-mobile-data-at-d75-per-gigabyte-in-latest-telecom-pricing-decision) sets mobile data at D75 per gigabyte with effect from 1 January 2026, ends the price floor and introduces rollover of unused data.<br><br>The ministerial [directive to lift the floor](https://standard.gm/govt-says-pura-directed-to-remove-floor-price-on-mobile-data/) was issued on 16 October 2025 with a cost-of-service study still pending, so the price was set before the cost basis for it was established. Roaming costs move separately: caps between The Gambia and Senegal with thirty days of free incoming calls [start on 1 March 2026](https://pura.gm/pura-and-artp-sign-mou-to-implement-ecowas-roaming-regulation/).</details> | Advanced |
| Ghana | An independent index puts the market [among the ten cheapest in Africa for a one-gigabyte basket](https://researchictafrica.net/2026/02/28/ramp-index-insights-quarter-4-2025/), and the regulator has [opened a consultation on roaming wholesale rates](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Framework-for-National-Roaming-Wholesale-Rates09032026.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-02-28 - an independent index for the fourth quarter of 2025 [places the country among the ten cheapest African markets for a one-gigabyte monthly mobile data basket, attributing it to state-supported infrastructure rollout, infrastructure sharing and stronger competition, while cautioning that the results are converted to dollars and sensitive to exchange-rate movement](https://researchictafrica.net/2026/02/28/ramp-index-insights-quarter-4-2025/).<br><br>2026-03-11 - the regulator [opened a four-week consultation proposing a regulator-led wholesale-rate framework for national roaming to replace bilateral operator arrangements, following its significant-market-power declaration](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Framework-for-National-Roaming-Wholesale-Rates09032026.pdf).<br><br>The regulator's own quarterly bulletin is the primary domestic series, but the held capture stops before its average-tariff section, so no national tariff figure is on this ledger and the affordability position rests on the independent index.</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea | Mobile internet cost was [reported down 25 per cent over 2025](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/guinea-reviews-2025-digital-sector-gains-targets-faster-rollout-in-2026) in the ministry's sector review, with no baseline price published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The reduction is ministry-reported and carries no starting price, tariff basket or basis of comparison ([sector review](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/tech/guinea-reviews-2025-digital-sector-gains-targets-faster-rollout-in-2026)).<br><br>No regulator tariff series, affordability index or income-share measure exists against which to read it, so the direction is stated and the level is not.</details> | Advanced, on the ministry's own figure |
| Guinea-Bissau | No tariff or affordability figure is published; the [universal access fund plan](https://wardip.gw/plano-estrategico-do-fundo-de-acesso-e-servicos-universais-2026-2031-consultoria-salience-2025/) is the instrument aimed at the gap. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base holds no Guinean retail tariff, price basket or affordability ratio.<br><br>The instrument that would address affordability is the universal access fund, which a [legal review recommended restructuring](https://wardip.gw/revisao-do-quadro-legal-para-o-acesso-servico-universal-consultoria-de-saliencia/) in October 2025 and for which a [2026-2031 strategic and financing plan](https://wardip.gw/plano-estrategico-do-fundo-de-acesso-e-servicos-universais-2026-2031-consultoria-salience-2025/) was delivered in May 2026. Neither publishes a price target.</details> | No change |
| Kenya | A [bill proposing consumption-based internet billing](https://www.businessdailyafrica.com/bd/economy/firms-warn-of-costly-internet-surveillance-in-proposed-law-5569724) drew warnings from two providers that per-megabyte metering would force deep packet inspection into their networks. <details><summary>Full record</summary>They told Parliament it would hand subscriber-level usage to the regulator, raise consumer prices, break fixed-speed broadband pricing and conflict with data minimisation, and a jurists' commission warned the central store could be used to monitor activists, journalists and political opponents; the reporting establishes no precedent for the regime in any national market ([submissions](https://www.businessdailyafrica.com/bd/economy/firms-warn-of-costly-internet-surveillance-in-proposed-law-5569724), [assessment](https://nation.africa/kenya/blogs-opinion/opinion/kenya-information-and-communication-bill-a-solution-looking-for-a-problem-5565386), [warning](https://peopledaily.digital/business/faiba-warns-parliament-new-internet-billing-rules-could-raise-costs-and-threaten-privacy)).<br><br>The Bill's own gazetted text runs to four clauses, of which one brings internet service providers inside the definition of telecommunication operator and another preserves existing licences until expiry; the only verification duty in it is the user's verification of an invoice, and the subscriber-verification duty reported from the commentaries is not in the text ([the Bill](https://new.kenyalaw.org/akn/ke/bill/na/2025-03-07/the-kenya-information-and-communication-amendment-bill-2025/eng@2025-03-07/source)).</details> | Regressed |
| Lesotho | The regulator [found against an operator over unnotified airtime restrictions and ordered them reversed](https://groundup.news/article/lesotho-regulator-finds-vodacom-guilty-of-unfair-market-practices/); no price series is published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2020-02 — [a World Bank diagnostic is the base’s fullest account of digital infrastructure and affordability](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/196401591179805910/pdf/Lesotho-Digital-Economy-Diagnostic.pdf).<br><br>2025-10-01 — [an operator published promotional bundle terms](https://www.etl.co.ls/files/Hoa%20Khonahala%20Terms%20and%20Conditions.pdf).<br><br>2025-12-05 — [the regulator found against an operator over airtime restrictions applied without notice and ordered them reversed](https://groundup.news/article/lesotho-regulator-finds-vodacom-guilty-of-unfair-market-practices/).<br><br>No basket price, income share or affordability index for Lesotho is held, which is what this indicator turns on.</details> | Advanced, on enforcement rather than price |
| Liberia | The [regulator's own series](https://lta.gov.lr/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Sector-Data-Snapshot-25.pdf) puts a 5GB basket at 12.1 per cent of monthly income in 2025, down from 13.6 per cent in 2023. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [sector series](https://lta.gov.lr/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Sector-Data-Snapshot-25.pdf) holds the 5GB monthly basket flat at US$8 across 2023, 2024 and 2025 while average monthly income rose from US$59 to US$63 to US$66, giving affordability ratios of 13.6, 12.7 and 12.1 per cent against a 2 per cent benchmark. The whole of the improvement is income, not price.<br><br>Tax works the other way: the [April 2026 revenue notice](https://revenue.lra.gov.lr/important-revenue-noticeadjustment-to-goods-and-services-tax-gst-rates/) raises the general services rate to 13 per cent from 1 May 2026 while holding telecommunications at 15 per cent. Roaming charges among Liberia, Ghana and Sierra Leone were [removed in February 2026](https://lta.gov.lr/liberia-ghana-sierra-leone-move-toward-borderless-mobile-communication/), with full implementation still to follow.</details> | Advanced, on income growth alone |
| Libya | Operator tariffs run [from 0.5 LYD for 50MB to 80 LYD for 80GB](https://almadar.ly/ar/Pages/Tawasul/Services/Netpackages.aspx); no regulated price basket is published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base holds no regulated tariff order and no affordability basket for Libya. What it holds is the retail cards: an [operator's live tariffs from 0.5 LYD for 50MB over a day to 80 LYD for 80GB over thirty days](https://almadar.ly/ar/Pages/Tawasul/Services/Netpackages.aspx).<br><br>A competing [unlimited daily plan caps heavy use at 10 dinars through the operator's app](https://www.libyaakhbar.com/business-news/2711916.html). Neither is set against income, and no regulator series measures the basket as a share of earnings.</details> | No change |
| Madagascar | Operators and the state [agreed in December 2025 to cut mobile internet prices gradually](https://www.lexpress.mg/2025/12/connectivite-baisse-annoncee-des-tarifs.html), with excise relief conditioned on reinvestment in quality. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The agreement ended a standoff between the state and the operators ([agreement](https://www.lexpress.mg/2025/12/connectivite-baisse-annoncee-des-tarifs.html)).<br><br>No price schedule, reduction figure or implementation date was published with it, and no tariff basket or affordability measure for the country is held, so the base records that prices were agreed to fall and cannot say from what or to what.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | The minister called for [scrapping the 10 per cent internet surtax and reviewing the 17.5 per cent smartphone value-added tax](https://www.itweb.africa/article/malawi-targets-smartphone-tax-relief/mQwkoq6Y9DpM3r9A), targeting 30 per cent mobile internet usage by 2030. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The call was made at a Lilongwe summit in August 2026, setting a target of raising mobile internet usage from 12.5 per cent to at least 30 per cent by 2030 ([call](https://www.itweb.africa/article/malawi-targets-smartphone-tax-relief/mQwkoq6Y9DpM3r9A)).<br><br>No budget measure, bill or instrument is on record: the treasury has published nothing and no timetable is given. It is a ministerial statement at a summit, and it is the only affordability position the base holds for the country.</details> | Advanced |
| Mali | About [1.5GB of mobile data in Bamako costs what buys 25GB in Dakar](https://theafricantribune.info/malis-telecom-costs-surge-while-senegal-thrives-in-data-affordability/), and the market has [one surviving independent provider](https://www.maliweb.net/contributions/deviations-institutionnelles-et-oligopole-du-numerique-en-afrique-de-louest-la-manne-sequestree-aux-origines-de-la-captation-du-fonds-dacces-universel-au-mali-3118719.html). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The comparison, published in August 2026, puts the ratio at roughly twenty-five to one and attributes it to weak market oversight, the duopoly of the two incumbent operators and the years since a new operator licence was issued ([comparison](https://theafricantribune.info/malis-telecom-costs-surge-while-senegal-thrives-in-data-affordability/)). It is a press comparison rather than a tariff series, and neither the basket nor the date of the prices compared is stated.<br><br>One independent internet service provider is reported surviving, buying wholesale transit at near-retail prices, which is the wholesale structure behind the retail figure ([provider](https://www.maliweb.net/contributions/deviations-institutionnelles-et-oligopole-du-numerique-en-afrique-de-louest-la-manne-sequestree-aux-origines-de-la-captation-du-fonds-dacces-universel-au-mali-3118719.html)).</details> | No change, a first published comparison with no earlier price series behind it |
| Mauritius | A [free monthly mobile data package](https://www.mra.mu/eservices1/financial-assistance/free-mobile-data-package) for young people was added in June 2026 to the regulator's promotional tariff framework. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator issued a promotional tariff framework in August 2025 ([directive](https://www.icta.mu/documents/2025/08/TD_3_2025.pdf)).<br><br>A free monthly mobile data package for young people is administered through the revenue authority ([package](https://www.mra.mu/eservices1/financial-assistance/free-mobile-data-package)).<br><br>Usage and penetration are reported annually ([statistics](https://statsmauritius.govmu.org/Documents/Statistics/ESI/2026/EI1939/ICT_Yr25_100726.pdf)). Both instruments are interventions rather than measures: no price index, basket cost or affordability figure is held, so whether tariffs have moved cannot be read from the base.</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | [Mobile interconnection tariffs](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f) entered a transitional reduction in July 2026 and reach full symmetry in February 2027. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The transitional phase runs from 1 July 2026 to 31 January 2027, with full symmetry from 1 February 2027 ([phase](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f)).<br><br>The record of it is the operator's own half-year filing; the regulator's decision text is not held, and no retail price, basket or affordability measure for the country is held at all.</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | A standard data package fell from [9.4 per cent of monthly income per head in 2022 to 2.67 per cent in March 2026](https://www.oeconomico.com/mocambique-cria-mapa-nacional-da-banda-larga-para-orientar-investimento-e-reduzir-assimetrias-digitais/), on the funder's own figures. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The figures were released with the national coverage map and are the World Bank project's own; the basket behind the standard package is not defined in the record held ([figures](https://www.oeconomico.com/mocambique-cria-mapa-nacional-da-banda-larga-para-orientar-investimento-e-reduzir-assimetrias-digitais/)).<br><br>A fall of that size in four years is the largest single improvement recorded for the country in this base, and it is the one measure here that a household would feel directly.</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | The regulator's chief executive [proposed tax relief on fourth-generation devices](https://itweb.africa/article/namibian-regulator-proposes-tax-breaks-on-4g-devices/), against operator-reported tower utilisation close to half in one region. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The proposal was made at a tower launch in Kunene; no fiscal instrument, budget line or ministerial response has been published, and the operator reported utilisation close to 50 per cent across about 74 enabled towers in the region ([proposal](https://itweb.africa/article/namibian-regulator-proposes-tax-breaks-on-4g-devices/)).<br><br>Device cost, not coverage, is what the proposal treats as the constraint, and the incumbent made the same argument a year later in asking for smartphone subsidies before the 2G and 3G switch-off.</details> | No change, a regulator proposal with no fiscal instrument or ministerial response behind it |
| Nigeria | [Sub-US$100 smartphone shipments fell 34 per cent](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/20/ai-is-making-africas-cheapest-smartphones-harder-to-afford/) in a quarter with the African average price up US$41, and [consumer cloud storage rose more than 50 per cent](https://www.africaninsider.com/business/google-one-gets-more-expensive-in-africa-nigerian-users-face-52-hike/) on some plans. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A global memory-chip shortage turns device cost into a digital-divide variable in a market that imports almost every computer it sells: sub-US$100 shipments fell 34 per cent year on year in the second quarter of 2026, the continental average selling price rose US$41 to US$202, and the Nigerian market fell 11 per cent in the quarter as buyers delayed purchases ([devices](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/20/ai-is-making-africas-cheapest-smartphones-harder-to-afford/)); the analyst's stated cause is artificial-intelligence data-centre demand for memory making US$75 devices unprofitable to build ([devices](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/20/ai-is-making-africas-cheapest-smartphones-harder-to-afford/), [laptops](https://www.vanguardngr.com/2026/07/why-laptops-may-become-more-expensive-in-nigeria/)).<br><br>A single provider raised consumer cloud storage prices by more than 50 per cent on some plans, with Nigerian users facing increases of up to 52 per cent ([storage](https://www.africaninsider.com/business/google-one-gets-more-expensive-in-africa-nigerian-users-face-52-hike/)); it is the only dated consumer cloud price the base holds ([storage](https://www.africaninsider.com/business/google-one-gets-more-expensive-in-africa-nigerian-users-face-52-hike/)).</details> | Regressed |
| Rwanda | An [infrastructure-sharing regulation](https://www.rura.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RURA/Documents/Sectors/ICT/Regulatory_Instruments/ICT_Regulations_and_Guidelines/Regulatio_Governing_Telecommunication__Infrastructure_Sharing_and_Service_provision_in_Rwanda_.pdf) was made in January 2026, while [excise on mobile services stepped from 12 to 14 per cent](https://www.gsma.com/solutions-and-impact/connectivity-for-good/public-policy/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/Mobile-Taxation-Rwanda.pdf) in June. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulation made on 27 January 2026 requires cost-based, non-discriminatory infrastructure sharing — a structural route to lower retail prices through less duplicated network investment ([regulation](https://www.rura.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RURA/Documents/Sectors/ICT/Regulatory_Instruments/ICT_Regulations_and_Guidelines/Regulatio_Governing_Telecommunication__Infrastructure_Sharing_and_Service_provision_in_Rwanda_.pdf)).<br><br>Against that, excise duty on mobile services stepped from 12 per cent in June 2025 to 14 per cent in June 2026, within an effective average tax rate on sector pre-tax profits put at 74 to 83 per cent ([study](https://www.gsma.com/solutions-and-impact/connectivity-for-good/public-policy/wp-content/uploads/2026/06/Mobile-Taxation-Rwanda.pdf)) — an operators' trade association's own commissioned analysis.<br><br>The regulator's published tariffs stand at RWF 40 a minute and RWF 10 or 5 per megabyte ([report](https://www.rura.rw/fileadmin/user_upload/RURA/Documents/Sectors/ICT/Statistics/Quarterly_publication/Quarterly_publication_ICT_Sector_Statistics_Report_as_of_Quarter_Four_of_2025.pdf)).</details> | Mixed, sharing mandated while excise rose |
| Sierra Leone | Sierra Leone [entered the regional free roaming agreement](https://lta.gov.lr/liberia-ghana-sierra-leone-move-toward-borderless-mobile-communication/) in February 2026, removing charges with Liberia and Ghana. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [agreement entered in February 2026](https://lta.gov.lr/liberia-ghana-sierra-leone-move-toward-borderless-mobile-communication/) removes roaming charges among Sierra Leone, Liberia and Ghana, with the regulator stressing that full implementation still had to follow.<br><br>It is a cross-border price, not a domestic one: nothing in the base publishes a Sierra Leonean retail tariff or an affordability ratio against income.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | Average mobile data cost [fell from USD 5.75 to USD 5.10](https://nca.gov.so/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/NCA%20Annual%20Report%202024-2025.pdf) between 2023 and 2024, and an operator [launched cheaper tiered bundles](https://shabellemedia.com/fursad-cusub-somnet-telecom-oo-soo-kordhisay-adeegga-mudnaan-oo-aad-kaga-shuban-karto-xirmooyin-qiimo-jaban-oo-ku-habboon-baahidaada-2/) in April 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's price basket, built on the international methodology, records average mobile data cost falling 11.3 per cent from USD 5.75 in 2023 to USD 5.10 in 2024, with one gigabyte of residential mobile broadband around USD 5.65 at purchasing-power parity ([report](https://nca.gov.so/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/NCA%20Annual%20Report%202024-2025.pdf)).<br><br>An operator expanded tiered low-cost bundles — internet-only, calls-only and combined — reachable by short code in April 2026 ([launch](https://shabellemedia.com/fursad-cusub-somnet-telecom-oo-soo-kordhisay-adeegga-mudnaan-oo-aad-kaga-shuban-karto-xirmooyin-qiimo-jaban-oo-ku-habboon-baahidaada-2/)).<br><br>Affordability has two halves and the handset is the other: a financing scheme launched in May 2026 to put devices in hands without a bank account or credit record ([programme](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/hormuudtelecom_somalia-digitalinclusion-smartphones-activity-7457442289615863808-8GfU)).</details> | Advanced |
