| South Africa | Unused bundles [must roll over from January 2027](https://www.icasa.org.za/uploads/files/End-user-and-subscriber-Service-Charter-Amendment-Regulations-2025.pdf), a rule [now under review application by two operators](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/malatsi-backs-consumer-sentiment-on-data-expiry-debate/RgeVDMPRkPJvKJN3); an excise cut is [offset by rising device prices](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/20/ai-is-making-africas-cheapest-smartphones-harder-to-afford/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-01-23 — amendment regulations were [gazetted requiring unused data bundles to roll over automatically and free of charge at least once](https://www.icasa.org.za/uploads/files/End-user-and-subscriber-Service-Charter-Amendment-Regulations-2025.pdf), excluding bundles of seven days or less and uncapped, free and promotional bundles, with depletion alerts at 50%, 80% and 100%. They come into force on 23 January 2027. [Two operators have filed for review](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/malatsi-backs-consumer-sentiment-on-data-expiry-debate/RgeVDMPRkPJvKJN3), and the filing date and case number are unestablished.<br><br>On devices the two forces run opposite ways. The minister [credits the April 2025 excise removal with 1.1m additional entry-level units](https://www.sowetan.co.za/opinion/columnists/2026-07-10-solly-malatsi-removing-smartphone-tax-means-access-to-opportunity/); against that, [African sub-$100 smartphone shipments fell 34% year on year in the second quarter of 2026 and the continental average selling price rose $41 to $202](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/20/ai-is-making-africas-cheapest-smartphones-harder-to-afford/), attributed to memory and semiconductor demand. A tax cut and a component-cost rise are working on the same price, and the base holds no South-Africa-specific unit or price series to net them against.</details> | Mixed, a data-rollover regulation gazetted and under legal challenge while device prices rose against an excise cut |
| South Sudan | [Three tariff adjustments](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/parliament-questions-unchecked-telecom-tariff-hikes) on exchange-rate grounds brought Parliament to summon the information minister, after an [operator cut tariffs 25 per cent](https://techafricanews.com/2025/12/12/south-sudans-ict-ministry-commends-mtn-for-25-tariff-reduction-urges-further-cuts/) in December 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The adjustments ran through 2026 in three phases and no regulator determination, tariff schedule or operator justification is published; Parliament moved to summon the minister on 21 August 2026 ([summons](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/parliament-questions-unchecked-telecom-tariff-hikes), [phase three](https://www.onecitizendaily.com/index.php/2026/08/20/network-telecommunication-companies-raise-call-and-data-tariffs/), [phase two](https://www.onecitizendaily.com/index.php/2026/07/27/network-telecom-companies-declare-another-increment/), [second](https://www.eyeradio.org/you-will-pay-more-for-calls-data-from-tonight-as-second-tariff-adjustment-takes-effect/), [operator](https://ss.zain.com/2026/06/26/zain-south-sudan-reassures-customers-on-tariff-exchange-rate-adjustment/), [first](https://www.radiotamazuj.org/en/news/article/nca-adjusts-telecom-tariff-exchange-rate-to-avert-network-collapse)).<br><br>An operator cut tariffs by 25 per cent in December 2025 with the ministry pressing for more ([cut](https://techafricanews.com/2025/12/12/south-sudans-ict-ministry-commends-mtn-for-25-tariff-reduction-urges-further-cuts/)), and the revised rates after the adjustments are unpublished ([price](https://www.onecitizendaily.com/index.php/2026/07/27/network-telecom-companies-declare-another-increment/), [editorial](https://www.onecitizendaily.com/index.php/2025/11/27/41522/)).<br><br>So the direction is a cut followed by three rises, none of which can be quantified from anything published.</details> | Regressed |
| Sudan | One operator [raised bundle and call prices](https://almashhadalsudani.com/economic-news/139290/) in September 2025 and the other [reset its schedule](https://almashhadalsudani.com/services/148435/) in January 2026; prices have moved again since. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The first increase took effect on 6 September 2025, covering daily, weekly and monthly internet bundles and local call minutes, on grounds of higher operating costs ([announcement](https://almashhadalsudani.com/economic-news/139290/)).<br><br>The second operator's schedule as at 8 January 2026 ran from SDG 1,100 for 100MB a day to SDG 110,000 for 500GB a month ([schedule](https://almashhadalsudani.com/services/148435/)). Its own current price list differs again, so the schedule moved after that report ([price list](https://www.mtn.sd/bundle-type/20)).<br><br>No affordability measure against income is held, so the direction is clear and the burden is not.</details> | Regressed |
| Tunisia | The regulator published an [affordability report on international data](https://www.lapresse.tn/2026/07/18/internet-et-mobile-la-tunisie-parmi-les-pays-arabes-ou-les-services-numeriques-coutent-le-moins-cher/) in July 2026, cited as a reference measurement rather than absorbed. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It is cited as a reference measurement, not absorbed as country state ([report](https://www.lapresse.tn/2026/07/18/internet-et-mobile-la-tunisie-parmi-les-pays-arabes-ou-les-services-numeriques-coutent-le-moins-cher/)).<br><br>A regulator publishing an affordability comparison is the kind of measurement most units in this frame lack entirely, and this base holds the fact of it rather than its figures.</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | A [device-tax paper was stated ready for cabinet](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/business/ict-ministry-communication-stakeholders-call-NV_238288_082026), and [average monthly consumer data spend rose](https://www.ceo.co.ug/ugandans-spend-on-data-telecom-budget-shift/) on volumes that moved the implied per-gigabyte cost the other way. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator proposed major telecom tax cuts and a device-tax paper was stated ready for cabinet in August 2026; the study itself is not held ([paper](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/business/ict-ministry-communication-stakeholders-call-NV_238288_082026), [cuts](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/22/ucc-proposes-major-telecom-tax-cuts-to-boost-ugandas-digital-economy/)).<br><br>The two ends of the spend series are on different consumption volumes, so the implied per-gigabyte cost moved the other way; the reporting outlet's own per-gigabyte derivation is not the regulator's figure ([spend](https://www.ceo.co.ug/ugandans-spend-on-data-telecom-budget-shift/)).<br><br>A regional dataset states a mobile data price printed in dollars with no exchange-rate or conversion basis ([price](https://admin.eaco.int/assets/e774142a-21fd-48e2-bdfd-3309d7d559d7?download=EACO%20Regional%20Communications%20Sector%20Statistics%20Report%202025.pdf)), and buy-now-pay-later device financing is reported by a trade analysis with no default, repossession or total-cost-of-credit figure published ([financing](https://pctechmag.com/2026/08/can-device-financing-solve-the-problem-of-smartphone-penetration/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | The regulator's [2025 sector report carries its own subscription and affordability figures](https://www.zambiamonitor.com/techbytes-zicta-reports-strong-growth-in-mobile-internet-usage-in-2025-ict-sector-review/), and the [mobile money levy schedule was revised](https://zambialii.org/akn/zm/act/2025/22/eng@2025-12-30/source.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[An external country diagnostic published Zambia-specific prepaid one-gigabyte and voice basket prices](https://www.digitaldevelopment.org/countries/zambia/) — the base's earlier affordability position, from a foreign tool rather than a domestic series.<br><br>2026-03 — [the regulator's 2025 ICT and postal sector market performance report gave its own current mobile subscription and affordability figures](https://www.zambiamonitor.com/techbytes-zicta-reports-strong-growth-in-mobile-internet-usage-in-2025-ict-sector-review/), which is the more durable source.<br><br>2025-12-30 — [an Act revised the person-to-person mobile money transfer levy schedule, effective 1 January 2026](https://zambialii.org/akn/zm/act/2025/22/eng@2025-12-30/source.pdf). A transfer levy is an affordability question for the poorest users of the payment system, and no assessment of its incidence accompanies it.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | An [uncapped 500 Mbps fibre package at US$99](https://www.newsday.co.zw/opinion-analysis/article/200055001/the-empire-strikes-back-why-liquids-fibre-price-war-is-threatening-starlinks-urban-reign) came out of a price war, while [satellite subscriptions grew](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200055470/govt-defends-starlink-rollout) against a rural population 76 per cent of whom live below the poverty line. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The package prices are the operators' own, quoted in United States dollars ([package](https://www.newsday.co.zw/opinion-analysis/article/200055001/the-empire-strikes-back-why-liquids-fibre-price-war-is-threatening-starlinks-urban-reign), [wireless](https://www.newsday.co.zw/theindependent/business-digest/article/200055625/over-50-of-zims-fixed-broadband-now-wireless)).<br><br>Satellite subscriptions grew in scale only; affordability is unchanged against a rural population three-quarters of whom live below the poverty line ([affordability](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200055470/govt-defends-starlink-rollout), [silence](https://techcabal.com/2025/06/26/starlink-in-zimbabwe/)).<br><br>A media institute's review of internet affordability against income was published in August 2026 on regulator data; it is a review rather than a household survey, and the penetration figure it uses counts active subscriptions ([review](https://zimbabwe.misa.org/2026/08/16/internet-affordability-and-access-in-zimbabwe/)).</details> | Advanced |

## National fibre backbone

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | [3.4m of over 7m fixed-internet subscribers are on fibre](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%D8%B2%D8%B1%D9%88%D9%82%D9%8A-%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D9%83%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%84-%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%85%D9%8A%D9%85-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%84%D9%8A%D8%A7%D9%81-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A8%D8%B5%D8%B1%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D9%85), with copper replacement targeted for end-2026 or early 2027. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [copper phase-out was announced in May 2025 on a 2027 target](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/telecom/high-speed-networks-algeria-to-phase-out-copper-switch-to-fiber-by-2027). By May 2026 there were [over 7m fixed-internet subscribers of which over 3.4m on fibre, and over 51m mobile subscribers, with copper replacement targeted for end-2026 or early 2027](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%D8%B2%D8%B1%D9%88%D9%82%D9%8A-%D8%A7%D8%B3%D8%AA%D9%83%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%84-%D8%AA%D8%B9%D9%85%D9%8A%D9%85-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%84%D9%8A%D8%A7%D9%81-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A8%D8%B5%D8%B1%D9%8A%D8%A9-%D9%85), after [3m fibre-to-the-home households in February](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/20/algeria-reaches-milestone-as-3-million-households-connect-to-fibre-broadband/) and a [claim of continental leadership on that measure](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D8%B2%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%B1-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%88%D9%84%D9%89-%D8%A5%D9%81%D8%B1%D9%8A%D9%82%D9%8A%D8%A7-%D9%81%D9%8A-%D9%85%D8%AC%D8%A7%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%B1%D8%A8%D8%B7-%D8%A8).<br><br>The [400G optical backbone was commissioned in February 2025 and no capacity update has followed](https://techafricanews.com/2025/02/25/algeria-telecom-and-huawei-ignite-a-new-era-of-high-speed-connectivity/), and an [Italy-Algeria submarine cable is at memorandum stage only, with no build contract, route or landing date](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/alg-rie-t-l-com-sparkle-plan-subsea-cable-to-link-italy-to-africa).</details> | Advanced |
| Angola | Five official statements of fibre length [run non-monotonic between 20,000 km and 30,000 km](https://pti.ao/unesco-conclui-que-angola-esta-preparada-para-adopcao-etica-e-inclusiva-da-ia/), and the base asserts no current national figure. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The measurement is the finding. Official statements put the terrestrial network at [22,000 km in 2023](https://minttics.gov.ao/ao/noticias/matias-borges-dois-novos-projectos-vao-garantir-a-expansao-dos-servicos-de-comunicacoes-nos-municipios-e-nas-comunas/), 20,000 in October 2025, [30,000 in November 2025](https://angop.ao/en/noticias/economia/angola-presente-na-conferencia-mundial-das-telecomunicacoes/), 22,000 in April 2026 and [28,000 in June 2026](https://pti.ao/unesco-conclui-que-angola-esta-preparada-para-adopcao-etica-e-inclusiva-da-ia/). The series is not monotonic and no definition accompanies any of the figures, so the base asserts no current national length. The regulator's annual statistical report that would settle it was dropped as unobtainable.<br><br>The build behind the figures is equally opaque. [The national broadband network was financed by a specific loan agreement of about US$249m signed in October 2023](https://www.bu.edu/gdp/files/2024/08/GCI-PB-23-CLA-2024-FIN.pdf) and remains active with no delivery milestone published at either end. [A domestic submarine cable to interlink the coastal cities and connect a transport corridor to an international system was stated as in preparation in March 2026](https://angop.ao/noticias/sociedade/minttics-implementa-projectos-estruturantes-para-a-redefinicao-da-base-tecnologica-do-pais/), with no route, cost, financier or date.</details> | No change |
| Benin | More than 3,000 km of fibre [underpins the service estate](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/benin-sets-2031-horizon-for-new-digital-transformation-roadmap), cited as a foundation of the 2031 strategy and unchanged in the window. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[More than 3,000 km of fibre is cited as underpinning the service estate and as a foundation of the 2031 strategy](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/benin-sets-2031-horizon-for-new-digital-transformation-roadmap). The figure is a strategy citation rather than an operator or regulator return, and the same figure stood at the start of the window.<br><br>No route map, capacity, utilisation or extension programme is published, and no second measurement exists to read it against. The regional financing reported under development-partner project financing carries a broadband pillar, and nothing yet states what of it lands on this backbone.</details> | No change |
| Botswana | About 16,000 km of fibre [is stated laid, linking four neighbours](https://dailynews.gov.bw/news-detail/90463), and [a P43m local-access project connected three villages and 191 customers](https://na.co.bw/bofinet-completes-p43-million-fibre-project/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-03-09 — [the minister put the network at about 16,000 km of fibre laid, linking South Africa, Namibia, Zimbabwe and Zambia](https://dailynews.gov.bw/news-detail/90463), a statement with no published register behind it.<br><br>2026-08-16 — [a P43m local-access programme completed, extending fibre to three villages and connecting 191 customers with 210 construction jobs created](https://na.co.bw/bofinet-completes-p43-million-fibre-project/), [by an operator reporting P376m revenue and 99.7% availability across the backbone](https://na.co.bw/bofinet-hosts-agm-showcasing-enhanced-accountability-and-value-creation/), [confirmed in a third account](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/14/bofinet-completes-p43m-fibre-project-connects-three-botswana-villages/).<br><br>Against those figures, [a Village Connectivity Project is stated as running with no site count, target, budget or completion date published](https://itweb.africa/article/botswana-taps-huawei-for-ai-rural-connectivity/GxwQDq1DOk9MlPVo): the state names the programme and publishes no figure against it, while the wholesale operator publishes down to the customer.</details> | Advanced |
| Burkina Faso | More than 11,292 km [is reported deployed with 270 km more programmed](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/burkina-faso-earmarks-109-4mln-for-digital-projects-in-2026), and the wholesale price of a 10 Gb service [fell from about 90m to about 35m FCFA a month](https://digitalmagazine.bf/2026/08/18/interview-avec-sagadibwaoga-michel-ouedraogo-dg-de-bfs-le-backbone-national-a-tenu-ses-promesses-trois-ans-dexploitation-de-la-fibre-optique-publique-burkinabe/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-12-30 — [more than 11,292 km is reported deployed with 270 km more programmed for 2026](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/burkina-faso-earmarks-109-4mln-for-digital-projects-in-2026). No earlier cumulative figure is on file, so the increment is not derivable, and the figure does not reconcile with the other lengths the base carries.<br><br>2026-08-18 — [the operating company's director general puts the wholesale price of a 10 Gb service at about 35 million FCFA a month against about 90 million in 2023, on three years running the state-built backbone](https://digitalmagazine.bf/2026/08/18/interview-avec-sagadibwaoga-michel-ouedraogo-dg-de-bfs-le-backbone-national-a-tenu-ses-promesses-trois-ans-dexploitation-de-la-fibre-optique-publique-burkinabe/). The figures are his own in an interview, no published tariff schedule is held, and the 3,000 km he describes does not reconcile with [the 307 km first phase in service at 1,600 Gbit/s, itself the first stage of a stated EUR 175m and 5,740 km programme with no phase-two status on file](https://www.jeuneafrique.com/593265/politique/ambassade-de-chine-au-burkina-pekin-resserre-sa-cooperation-avec-ouagadougou/).<br><br>The other completed build is dual-purpose: [650 km of backbone plus 150 km of city links, 900 surveillance cameras and 300 video-surveillance sites on a Chinese concessional loan of RMB 560m](https://www.bu.edu/gdp/files/2022/11/GCI_PB_012_EN_FIN.pdf), [reported complete in August 2025](https://www.imf.org/en/Publications/CR/Issues/2019/01/22/Burkina-Faso-2018-Article-IV-Consultation-First-Review-Under-the-Extended-Credit-Facility-46533) on two records that disagree about completion.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | [1,750 km on the national backbone covers all provinces](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/RAPPORT-DES-INDICATEURS-DU-SECTEUR-DES-TIC-EDITION-2024-VALIDE-ET-PUBLIE-1.pdf), with the regulator stating the national territory is now covered by fibre. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Regulator statistics as at 31 December 2024 give fibre deployment by infrastructure operator: 1,750 km on the national backbone covering all provinces, 350 km of metropolitan network in the capital and 3,400 km on a third operator's network, and state that the national territory is now covered by fibre optic](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2025/08/RAPPORT-DES-INDICATEURS-DU-SECTEUR-DES-TIC-EDITION-2024-VALIDE-ET-PUBLIE-1.pdf).<br><br>2026-04 — [a presidential decree appointed a new director general of the mixed-economy company holding the backbone concession](https://presidence.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/DECRET-PORTANT-NOMINATION-DU-DG-BBS.pdf), and [a second decree four days later named the administrators representing the state on its board](https://presidence.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/DECRET-PORTANT-NOMINATION-DES-ADMINISTRATEURS-REPRESENTANT-LETAT-DU-BURUNDI-AU-CABBS.pdf). Governance moved; no traffic, utilisation or wholesale-price figure for the backbone is published, so what the fibre carries cannot be read from the base.</details> | Advanced |
| Cameroon | The backbone is [reported at over 15,000 km](https://newswatchcameroon.com/cameroon-starlink-licensing-stalls-over-security-sovereignty-concerns/), and a [fourth phase of about FCFA 108.4bn was authorised by decree](https://leconomie.info/extension-du-reseau-de-fibre-optique-le-cameroun-sendette-a-hauteur-de-108-milliards-de-fcfa-aupres-de-la-chine/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base held [about 12,000 km terrestrial plus about 812 km overhead on 2024 reference-year figures](https://techafricanews.com/2025/12/09/cameroons-telecom-sector-grows-18-in-2024-driven-by-mobile-and-digital-services/), and the backbone is now [reported at over 15,000 km](https://newswatchcameroon.com/cameroon-starlink-licensing-stalls-over-security-sovereignty-concerns/). The two figures are not reconciled to a single primary and may rest on different measurement bases.<br><br>2026-03-06 - a decree [authorised signature of CNY 1,350,963,161, about FCFA 108.4bn, for a further 4,000 km, a national data centre and regional links to Chad, the Central African Republic and Nigeria](https://leconomie.info/extension-du-reseau-de-fibre-optique-le-cameroun-sendette-a-hauteur-de-108-milliards-de-fcfa-aupres-de-la-chine/). The [loan is not confirmed signed](https://www.prc.cm/files/c3/68/8c/dc79325e0fb9f1c5354d4abbe5c4a2b7.pdf https://news.broadcastmediaafrica.com/2026/03/10/cameroon-secures-us195-million-to-enhance-fibre-optic-network/) and the executing contractor is not confirmed.</details> | Advanced |
| Central African Republic | The backbone [holds 935 km in service and gained nothing through the window](https://www.agenceecofin.com/infrastructures/0802-105283-inauguration-de-la-dorsale-optique-nationale-de-la-republique-centrafricaine-et-de-l-interconnexion-avec-le-congo), against [a 12,000 km commitment stated by the minister](https://oubanguimedias.com/2026/02/02/presentation-de-voeux-le-ministere-de-leconomie-numerique-devoile-ses-grandes-orientations-pour-lannee-2026/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[935 km of terrestrial and sub-fluvial fibre is in service on the Bangui-Douala corridor with spurs to Cameroon and Congo](https://www.agenceecofin.com/infrastructures/0802-105283-inauguration-de-la-dorsale-optique-nationale-de-la-republique-centrafricaine-et-de-l-interconnexion-avec-le-congo), and no new kilometrage, commissioning or route was added through the window. [The minister's claim of about 1,200 km in July 2025 is unverified](https://africa24tv.com/republique-centrafricaine-le-taux-de-penetration-dinternet-atteint-30/) and the development bank is the announcer of record for the 935 km. [The 2026 estimates appropriate FCFA 2,000,000 to connect prefecture capitals to Bangui over it](http://www.finances.gouv.cf/sites/default/files/2026-03/Cadre%20des%20D%C3%A9penses%20%C3%A0%20Moyen%20Terme%202026.pdf).<br><br>2026-02-02 — against that, [the minister stated a 12,000 km fibre commitment at the ministry's orientations ceremony](https://oubanguimedias.com/2026/02/02/presentation-de-voeux-le-ministere-de-leconomie-numerique-devoile-ses-grandes-orientations-pour-lannee-2026/), with no contract, financing, route, phasing or timetable held and no progress in the six months since. The figure is roughly ten times the backbone built to date and reaches this base through a speech.<br><br>A two-million-franc line to connect the prefectures and a twelve-thousand-kilometre announcement are the two ends of the same year's policy.</details> | No change |
| Chad | A [seven-day ultimatum to the two mobile operators](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/chad-pushes-telcos-for-network-upgrades) to connect to the national backbone was not complied with and no sanction was applied, and the [trans-Saharan section](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1302-52879-chad-plans-50-telecom-sites-50-km-of-fiber-in-n-djamena) is 60 per cent complete. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ultimatum followed a ministerial meeting; the state cited 1,275 km of fibre deployed, a figure no source reconciles with the project's own 1,200 km target, and no sanction was ever recorded as applied ([ultimatum](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/chad-pushes-telcos-for-network-upgrades), [order](https://afriqueitnews.com/tech-media/tchad-ordonne-operateurs-connecter-reseau-national-fibre-optique/), [link](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fiber-networking/chad-tells-operators-to-fast-track-fiber-link-to-cameroon)).<br><br>The state connectivity programme's first phase was inaugurated at Sarh in January 2026; its 2020 specification of 2,000 km of fibre and 200 masts across twelve regions has never been restated and no delivery is reported against any figure ([programme](https://lendjampost.com/inauguration-de-la-fibre-optique-au-moyen-chari-un-pas-vers-la-modernisation-numerique/), [phase](https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/chad-invests-175m-in-national-data-center-and-networks)).<br><br>The trans-Saharan section was reported 60 per cent complete on EUR 29m of European financing, with no completion date, operator or route detail on file ([section](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1302-52879-chad-plans-50-telecom-sites-50-km-of-fiber-in-n-djamena), [delays](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/chad-urges-speed-on-delayed-trans-saharan-fiber-optic-project)). A twenty-year border-to-border concession has made no progress ([concession](https://russellsouthwood.substack.com/p/waking-the-dead-slow-road-to-extinction)).</details> | Stalled |
| Comoros | A [connectivity project reached readiness-stage funding talks in August 2026](https://alwatwan.net/economie/connectivit%C3%A9-num%C3%A9rique-i-la-bad-et-le-mcdf-pr%C3%AAts-%C3%A0-soutenir-le-projet-comorien.html); the 2018 supplier loan has no delivered site count. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-10 - two lenders [told the finance ministry they are ready to fund feasibility studies as a grant for a project carried by the digital agency covering data-centre studies and inter-island connectivity](https://alwatwan.net/economie/connectivit%C3%A9-num%C3%A9rique-i-la-bad-et-le-mcdf-pr%C3%AAts-%C3%A0-soutenir-le-projet-comorien.html). No commitment amount, study contract or timetable is stated, so it is readiness-stage discussion.<br><br>The standing position is a [CNY 567,000,000 concessional loan signed in December 2018 at thirty-year maturity funding a US$81m supplier contract for fibre extension to fifty-seven sites](https://www.aiddata.org/data/global-chinese-development-finance-dataset-version-3), with no site count delivered, no completion status and no repayment position held at either end. It is still the largest tracked deal in the base for this country.</details> | No change |
| Congo | The backbone's first three phases were [contributed in kind to the state operator at FCFA 143,878,429,945](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6002/congo-telecom-reiterates-ambition-to-lead-digital-transit-and-technology-hub-in-central-africa), staged to prepare a listing. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Earlier phases were held by the operator as usufruct with no formal contribution and no capital entry. A [council of ministers decree contributed assets valued at FCFA 143,878,429,945 in kind against a capital increase, raising share capital to FCFA 157,297,453,418 and absorbing accumulated losses of just over FCFA 20bn, staged explicitly to prepare a listing](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6002/congo-telecom-reiterates-ambition-to-lead-digital-transit-and-technology-hub-in-central-africa), [recorded in the council of 6 May 2026](https://sgg.cg/ccm/compte-rendu-conseil-des-ministres1-du-06-mai-2026-bis-1.pdf).<br><br>The backbone whose outages the regulator's own enforcement file blames for northern coverage failures is the asset being readied for market. A [co-regulation accord on shared fibre and pylon infrastructure, announced as imminent in March 2025, has no signature, text or implementation sixteen months on](https://www.adiac-congo.com/content/deploiement-de-la-fibre-optique-vers-une-urbanisation-maitrisee-des-reseaux-163787).</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | Deployed fibre is [put at about 38,000 km, of which over 6,600 km is state backbone](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6286/cote-divoire-positions-digital-technology-as-key-driver-of-economic-transformation), against 30,303 km earlier in the window. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base held [30,303 km deployed nationally](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2636184/economie/africa-bb-maps-connectivite-haut-debit-la-cote-divoire-dessine-sa-cartographie-nationale-des-reseaux-de-telecommunications) at the window's opening.<br><br>2026-07-13 - the ministry [put deployment at about 38,000 km with connectivity reaching nearly one million homes](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/6286/cote-divoire-positions-digital-technology-as-key-driver-of-economic-transformation), and the [state broadband backbone at over 6,600 km](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2642807/societe/forum-intech-sante-2026-lansut-presente-sa-feuille-de-route-pour-connecter-les-zones-rurales-et-moderniser-lacces-aux-soins). A [third figure of over 33,000 km for 2024 is in tension with the 2025 count](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2636184/economie/africa-bb-maps-connectivite-haut-debit-la-cote-divoire-dessine-sa-cartographie-nationale-des-reseaux-de-telecommunications), and all of them are the ministry's or the agency's own.<br><br>2025-12-17 - the first national assises on protecting network infrastructure convened operators, infrastructure managers and the regulator [after 665 acts of theft and vandalism nationally between January and October 2025](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2638877/economie/infrastructures-des-reseaux-de-communication-electronique-les-acteurs-du-secteur-a-la-recherche-dune-protection-durable); no protection instrument has been published since. 2026-08-13 - an operator and its vendor [completed a proof of concept in Abidjan detecting vibrations around fibre ducts and classifying them to flag third-party excavation before a cut happens](https://itweb.africa/article/mtn-is-promoting-ai-driven-fibre-cut-sensing-to-reshape-the-resilience-of-digital-infrastructure-in-africa/VgZeyqJlBLV7djX9), on a vendor-issued account with no commercial deployment stated.</details> | Advanced, on ministry figures that do not reconcile with the earlier count |
| Djibouti | A [binding agreement for a 2,800 km terrestrial corridor to Addis Ababa and Port Sudan was signed on 4 February 2026](https://www.lanation.dj/djibouti-telecom-ethio-telecomet-sudatel-telecom-group-signent-un-accord-strategique-pour-linitiative-horizon-fiber/), from a memorandum only. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The corridor stood at a [memorandum of understanding in December 2024](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/04/ethio-telecom-djibouti-telecom-and-sudatel-seal-horizon-fiber-cross-border-connectivity-deal/). 2026-02-04 - a [binding agreement was signed in Djibouti covering about 2,800 km, 144 fibre pairs and about 14 ms latency](https://www.lanation.dj/djibouti-telecom-ethio-telecomet-sudatel-telecom-group-signent-un-accord-strategique-pour-linitiative-horizon-fiber/), [reported across the trade press](https://itweb.africa/article/east-african-telcos-seal-cross-border-fibre-corridor-deal/xA9PO7NE16Yvo4J8). No service date is published, and the route and latency figures are the three operators' own.<br><br>Domestically, the fibre programme moved from the [Ali-Sabieh regional hospital connected and over 200 km deployed in June 2025](https://www.lanation.dj/ali-sabieh-le-dfn-propulse-lhopital-regional-dans-une-nouvelle-ere-numerique/) to [a school, sub-prefecture and clinic at Damerjog connected in October 2025](https://www.lanation.dj/damerjog-entre-dans-lere-du-numerique-grace-au-projet-djibouti-fondement-du-numerique-dfn/), with a [stakeholder and grievance workshop covering the capital, Balbala and the five interior regions](https://www.lanation.dj/transition-numerique-les-parties-prenantes-mobilisees-autour-des-projets-dfn-et-eardip/).</details> | Advanced |
| DR Congo | [7,000 km of backbone is deployed against a target of more than 18,500 km](https://deskeco.com/2026/08/03/transformation-numerique-en-rdc-encore-11500-km-de-fibre-optique-deployer-pour-atteindre-un-total-de), leaving 11,500 km outstanding to 2030. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-03 - the 95th ordinary Council meeting was told [7,000 km had been deployed against a target of more than 18,500 km, leaving 11,500 km outstanding, tied to connecting 145 territories by 2030](https://deskeco.com/2026/08/03/transformation-numerique-en-rdc-encore-11500-km-de-fibre-optique-deployer-pour-atteindre-un-total-de). The [minister cites budget constraints and a low disbursement rate on external financing, and the remaining distance over the 2030 horizon implies about 2,900 km a year against about 7,000 km delivered in the programme's first year](https://deskeco.com/2026/08/03/transformation-numerique-en-rdc-encore-11500-km-de-fibre-optique-deployer-pour-atteindre-un-total-de).<br><br>2026-07 - a [memorandum was signed with a Chinese supplier for about 2,100 km along and beside the Congo River at a stated US$1.5bn, more than US$400m of it required for the first phase](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fiber-networking/drc-plans-1-5b-fiber-optic-project-along-congo-river), with no financing close, contract or works. Both figures are company-reported.</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | The incumbent [launched in-home fibre-to-the-room commercially with a vendor](https://gate.ahram.org.eg/News/5660818.aspx), stated as a first for the country. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The service did not exist at the window's opening. 2026-05-24 - the incumbent's consumer brand [launched in-home fibre-to-the-room commercially with a vendor, presented as a first for the country](https://gate.ahram.org.eg/News/5660818.aspx).<br><br>No coverage, subscriber, tariff or contract-value figure is stated. It is the only fixed-network product launch on this ledger, against a rural fibre programme that has reached about five million lines and a fixed base of about 12.6 million subscriptions - so what share of the network it touches is not established.</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | The [four-ring backbone](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/imgdb/2019/file/5cdr_DISCURSOMINISTROKIGALI.pdf) may be joined by the electricity utility's [2,000km grid fibre network](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/segesa_estudia_la_posibilidad_de_comercializar_su_red_de_fibra_optica). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The minister's own account puts the backbone at [four rings and an international gateway with over US$30m invested](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/imgdb/2019/file/5cdr_DISCURSOMINISTROKIGALI.pdf).<br><br>A second national network exists and is unused for telecommunications: the electricity utility is [studying whether to lease its 2,000km grid fibre network to operators, on a vendor study](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/segesa_estudia_la_posibilidad_de_comercializar_su_red_de_fibra_optica). International capacity may also grow — a [study backs a Medusa cable landfall at Bome near Bata at EUR 20-60m, with service targeted for 2029-2030](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/guinea_ecuatorial_estudia_la_posibilidad_de_la_conexion_de_un_nuevo_enlace_de_fibra_optica_al_cable_submarino_internacional).</details> | Advanced |
| Eritrea | The ministry reports [about 73km of new cable laid and landline plant renovated in six towns](https://shabait.com/2026/05/19/substantial-investment-in-expansion-of-communication-service/); no independent measure of the backbone exists. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05-19 — the minister responsible for communications [reported 184 towers installed, about 73km of new cable laid, and landline plant renovated in six named towns and at the Naval Force Base](https://shabait.com/2026/05/19/substantial-investment-in-expansion-of-communication-service/), against a claimed cumulative investment above 35 billion Nakfa.<br><br>The figures are the ministry's own, carried by state media, and nothing on the ledger tests them: no operator disclosure, no regulator return and no third-party measurement of the network appears for Eritrea in the window. EriTel remains the sole operator.</details> | Advanced, on the ministry's own figures |
| Eswatini | [International capacity rose from 47 to 72 Gbps](https://times.co.sz/business/readmore.php?bhsadjgfoh=Optical+fibre+rollout+strengthens+business+ecosystem&bvhdgsj=Business+and+Economy&yiphi=2892), above target, and [1,031 public institutions](https://independentnews.co.sz/46514/business/aviation/digital-eswatini-project-hinges-on-leadership-ps-warns/) are targeted for connection under the digital project. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The capacity increase was reported in February 2026 as above target ([capacity](https://times.co.sz/business/readmore.php?bhsadjgfoh=Optical+fibre+rollout+strengthens+business+ecosystem&bvhdgsj=Business+and+Economy&yiphi=2892), [rollout](https://techafricanews.com/2025/08/04/eswatini-ramps-up-tech-reform-with-new-legislation-5g-rollout-and-coding-training-for-all/)).<br><br>The institution target was stated at the project's change-management workshop ([institutions](https://independentnews.co.sz/46514/business/aviation/digital-eswatini-project-hinges-on-leadership-ps-warns/)).<br><br>A landlocked country raising international capacity by half in a reporting period is a real gain; it is also why the satellite gateway recorded separately is framed as reducing dependence on cross-border fibre.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | The second operator [began building fibre](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/46234/) from Afdera to Mekelle in August 2025, the only backbone construction the base holds. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Construction on that route started in August 2025 and is the only record of national fibre being built by anyone other than the incumbent ([fibre deployment](https://www.thereporterethiopia.com/46234/)).<br><br>No route length, capacity, cost or completion date is published, and the base holds no figure at all for the national backbone's route kilometres, its utilisation or its redundancy.</details> | Advanced |
| Gabon | [2,000 km of backbone](https://agpgabon.ga/gabon-seminaire-sur-levolution-des-communications-spatiales-11-recommandations-adoptees/) is deployed, with 88 per cent forest cover cited as the physical limit on extending it further. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The figure is ministry-stated at a satellite seminar in July 2026 rather than a regulator or operator return, and extension works are said to be under way ([backbone](https://agpgabon.ga/gabon-seminaire-sur-levolution-des-communications-spatiales-11-recommandations-adoptees/)).<br><br>On access, the incumbent announced nine new fibre-to-the-home localities for 2025 on top of eight opened in 2024, and the base holds a plan and never a completion ([access programme](https://www.gabonreview.com/moov-africa-gabon-telecom-extension-de-la-fibre-optique-ftth-en-2025/)).<br><br>The first technology-neutral fixed authorisation was signed with the regulator in January 2025 carrying no build obligation or coverage target, and nothing is on record of what has been built under it since ([fixed authorisation](https://www.gabonreview.com/numerique-airtel-gabon-sengage-a-revolutionner-les-services-fixes/)).</details> | No change |
| Gambia | A [US$50m partnership](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/05/gamtel-and-sysroad-sign-50-million-ppp-to-modernize-the-gambias-broadband-network/) signed in January 2026 would take the backbone from 40 Gbps to 800 Gbps, on a network still carrying a 2017 concessional loan. <details><summary>Full record</summary>No completion date or milestone schedule is published for the upgrade ([partnership](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/05/gamtel-and-sysroad-sign-50-million-ppp-to-modernize-the-gambias-broadband-network/)).<br><br>The existing network was built under a US$25.9m concessional loan repayable by the state operator, with a servicing history of arrears cleared in June 2020 and the loan collateralised against a minimum escrow balance accessible to the lender ([loan](https://www.dropbox.com/s/9yvhtd9i4i0vrs0/cr18197.pdf?dl=0)); no statement of network condition, utilisation or debt position has followed since ([loan](https://www.dropbox.com/s/9yvhtd9i4i0vrs0/cr18197.pdf?dl=0)).</details> | Advanced |
| Ghana | Fibre cuts ran to [more than 8,000 in 2025 at about US$20m in repairs](https://www.myjoyonline.com/fibre-repairs-drain-millions-ghana-needs-for-digital-expansion/), against about 3,900 costing US$7.5m in 2021. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2022 peak was 10,034 cuts costing US$17.4m, and the operators logged 4,289 cuts in the first half of 2026, the only period-stated figure held ([repairs](https://www.myjoyonline.com/fibre-repairs-drain-millions-ghana-needs-for-digital-expansion/), [backbone under strain](https://www.graphic.com.gh/business/business-news/ghanas-digital-backbone-comes-under-strain.html)).<br><br>One operator recorded sabotage affecting 157 sites ([sabotage](https://www.myjoyonline.com/157-sites-disrupted-mtn-ceo-reveals-massive-scale-of-fibre-cable-sabotage/)).<br><br>A dig-once policy drafted with the roads ministry is finalised and awaiting cabinet approval, targeted for implementation in the third quarter of 2026 and claimed to cut fibre rollout cost by up to 60 per cent ([dig once](https://www.myjoyonline.com/government-says-dig-once-policy-could-cut-fibre-rollout-costs-by-up-to-60/)); no cabinet decision, instrument or commencement is on record ([dig once](https://www.myjoyonline.com/government-says-dig-once-policy-could-cut-fibre-rollout-costs-by-up-to-60/)).</details> | Regressed |
| Guinea | The backbone's first loop [went from 200 to 400 Gb/s on 20 May 2026, with 600 Gb/s targeted by year end](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/2205-138703-la-guinee-augmente-la-capacite-de-son-reseau-internet-de-700-en-un-an-et-demi). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[SOGEB announced on 20 May 2026 that the first loop of the national fibre backbone had been raised from 200 Gb/s to 400 Gb/s with Huawei's technical support, the second upgrade in eighteen months after December 2024's step from 50 Gb/s to 200 Gb/s — a cumulative 700% increase — with both partners targeting 600 Gb/s across the whole infrastructure by the end of the year](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/2205-138703-la-guinee-augmente-la-capacite-de-son-reseau-internet-de-700-en-un-an-et-demi).<br><br>The same account carries the regulator's traffic series, mobile internet traffic rising from 23.98 million GB in the second quarter of 2021 to 118.86 million GB in the second quarter of 2025. No utilisation figure against the upgraded capacity is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea-Bissau | The WARDIP-GB backbone [reached its environmental and social safeguards study in May 2026](https://www.odemocratagb.com/?p=57347), on a [2,877 km, 56-node design](https://wardip.gw/guine-bissau-acelera-transformacao-digital-com-nova-rede-nacional-de-fibra-optica/) not yet built. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The country has no national fibre backbone. The design for one [specifies 2,877 km over 56 nodes in four phases, targeting coverage of more than 99% of the population and operation by a private wholesale operator under exclusive licence](https://wardip.gw/guine-bissau-acelera-transformacao-digital-com-nova-rede-nacional-de-fibra-optica/); that specification is itself undated and carried at year precision.<br><br>2026-05-21 — the [environmental and social impact study was published](https://www.odemocratagb.com/?p=57347), which is the movement in the window: the project passes from design toward implementation procurement.<br><br>The starting position was thinner. In [August 2025 the country was seeking deployment experience from Chad with no fixed design published](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/2908-131059-deploiement-de-la-fibre-optique-la-guinee-bissau-veut-apprendre-du-tchad), after [the programme's launch in April 2025](https://ang.gw/guine-bissau-nao-pode-perder-o-comboio-da-modernizacao-tecnologica-diz-presidente-da-republica/). No construction contract, no selected wholesale operator and no built phase appears on the ledger.</details> | Advanced |
| Kenya | A [cross-border terrestrial link with Tanzania](https://www.kenyanews.go.ke/kenya-and-tanzania-launch-terrestrial-fiber-link-connection/) was launched in July 2026, integrating the neighbour's backbone with the Mombasa landings. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It was delivered by the information technology authority with the Tanzanian state operator at two border points; no capacity, cost or route length is stated ([link](https://www.kenyanews.go.ke/kenya-and-tanzania-launch-terrestrial-fiber-link-connection/)).<br><br>It is the only completed backbone build in the base with a date on it, against a national target of 100,000km measured at about 2,193km delivered.</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | A [metropolitan fibre network](https://www.rsl.org.ls/sites/default/files/2025-02/Budget%20Speech%202025%2026%20Final%2019-02-2025.pdf) has a completed feasibility study and no construction reported; a [96km Roma to Thaba-Tseka stretch](https://www.rsl.org.ls/sites/default/files/2025-02/Budget%20Speech%202025%2026%20Final%2019-02-2025.pdf) was under way in 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The budget speech of February 2025 recorded the feasibility study for the Lesotho Metropolitan Fibre Distribution Network as completed, laying groundwork for high-speed broadband across the major towns, with construction not reported ([feasibility](https://www.rsl.org.ls/sites/default/files/2025-02/Budget%20Speech%202025%2026%20Final%2019-02-2025.pdf)).<br><br>The same speech recorded a 96km fibre stretch from Roma to Thaba-Tseka under way and 17 base tower stations operational ([budget speech](https://www.rsl.org.ls/sites/default/files/2025-02/Budget%20Speech%202025%2026%20Final%2019-02-2025.pdf)). Eighteen months on, no completion, route map or capacity figure has been published for either.</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | The [domestic backbone is reported extending south-east](https://elbcnews.com/2026/07/23/commissioner-honnah-reaffirms-liberia-telecom-authoritys-commitment-to-expanding-digital-access/) from one coastal city toward five counties, with no route map or kilometre figure. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The line is reported reaching a border town from the capital, with private firms extending it ([extension](https://elbcnews.com/2026/07/23/commissioner-honnah-reaffirms-liberia-telecom-authoritys-commitment-to-expanding-digital-access/), [earlier account](https://www.liberianobserver.com/news/liberia-on-the-verge-of-5g-revolution/article_992b027e-4c8a-4578-bf70-baf2e2eabeeb.html)).<br><br>No completion date is published, and the base holds no national kilometre figure for the backbone at any date.</details> | Advanced |
| Libya | A flood severed the [Ghat to Tahala fibre link](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/519926) on 27 May 2026; 11 of 12 sites were back within days, most on VSAT backup. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The link was cut by flooding on 27 May 2026 and restored to 11 of the 12 sites it serves, 91.7 per cent, with most running on VSAT backup rather than on fibre ([restoration](https://alwasat.ly/news/libya/519926)).<br><br>The base holds no route map, no capacity figure and no statement of when full fibre service returned, so the condition of the backbone after the cut cannot be established beyond the count of sites.</details> | Regressed, restored to eleven of twelve sites after a flood cut, most of them on satellite backup |
| Madagascar | A [558 km Antananarivo to Mahajanga backbone](https://2424.mg/telecommunications-mahajanga-desservie-par-un-reseau-fibre-optique-dorange-madagascar/) entered service in December 2025, built in eight months by the country's third fibre operator. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The link was inaugurated in December 2025 after eight months' construction ([backbone](https://2424.mg/telecommunications-mahajanga-desservie-par-un-reseau-fibre-optique-dorange-madagascar/)).<br><br>It is the base's only national fibre construction record. No route map, capacity figure, wholesale price or measure of the pre-existing backbone is held, so the addition can be recorded and the total cannot.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | Two [concessional loans](http://africa.chinadaily.com.cn/weekly/2018-07/13/content_36567490.htm) financed the fibre backbone with no route length or operating status published, and [procurement for more than 90 towers](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/26/malawi-moves-to-expand-rural-connectivity-with-90-new-telecom-towers/) is advancing against a requirement above 1,000. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Phase one was financed by a concessional loan of CNY 155,432,330 committed in 2016 ([phase one](https://www.ecofinagency.com/telecom/1811-35880-malawi-to-secure-23-mln-loan-from-china-for-fiber-optic-backbone-project)) and phase two by CNY 693,000,000 at 1 per cent over twenty years, on-lent to the electricity utility with CNY 299,286,770 drawn as at June 2023 ([phase two](http://africa.chinadaily.com.cn/weekly/2018-07/13/content_36567490.htm)). The base holds the two loan records and no statement of route kilometres, completion, operator or current operating status.<br><br>Tower procurement for more than 90 towers is being advanced against a project commitment of around 100, while a study cited at the same meeting puts the requirement for nationwide coverage above 1,000 towers ([towers](https://techafricanews.com/2026/03/26/malawi-moves-to-expand-rural-connectivity-with-90-new-telecom-towers/), [project](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099050524041520240/txt/P5050951dae66d0e1bc631717e7e7a4e70.txt)).<br><br>A separate commercial loan covering 136 tower installations and last-mile fibre has no progress report, disbursement figure or completion statement on file ([loan](http://africa.chinadaily.com.cn/weekly/2018-07/13/content_36567490.htm)).</details> | Advanced |
| Mali | Retail [fibre speeds doubled to 100 Mbps across six cities](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1506-56460-moov-africa-upgrades-fiber-service-amid-rising-internet-use-in-mali), [ADSL is to be retired](https://www.maliweb.net/communique-391/moov-africa-malitel-tourne-la-page-de-ladsl-la-migration-vers-la-fibre-optique-ftth-lancee-dici-fin-2026-3118639.html) by end-2026, and a [single national transport operator](https://www.maliweb.net/technologie/4e-edition-de-la-semaine-du-numerique-des-recettes-variees-pour-la-souverainete-numerique-3118872.html) was recommended. <details><summary>Full record</summary>An operator doubled fibre speeds at no extra cost in June 2026, reaching up to 100 Mbps across six cities ([speeds](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1506-56460-moov-africa-upgrades-fiber-service-amid-rising-internet-use-in-mali)).<br><br>Retirement of copper and migration to fibre to the home was announced for the end of 2026, with no subscriber or coverage figure published ([migration](https://www.maliweb.net/communique-391/moov-africa-malitel-tourne-la-page-de-ladsl-la-migration-vers-la-fibre-optique-ftth-lancee-dici-fin-2026-3118639.html)).<br><br>The national digital week recommended making the state transport-infrastructure company the sole national operator of transport infrastructure, as a law; nothing has been tabled ([recommendation](https://www.maliweb.net/technologie/4e-edition-de-la-semaine-du-numerique-des-recettes-variees-pour-la-souverainete-numerique-3118872.html)). No route map or backbone length for the country is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | The backbone is in service under the [2030 strategic plan's](https://mdpa.govmu.org/mdpa/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/DigitalMauritius2030.pdf) connectivity pillar; no route length or capacity figure is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The 2030 strategic plan is the instrument the backbone sits under ([plan](https://mdpa.govmu.org/mdpa/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/DigitalMauritius2030.pdf)).<br><br>The incumbent used its December 2025 summit to position the island as the natural connector between Africa and Asia ([summit](https://mtconnect.telecom.mu/)).<br><br>What the base does not hold on this indicator is any physical figure — kilometres laid, lit capacity, or the number of exchanges reached. The connector claim rests on landings and geography rather than on a published domestic network.</details> | No change |
| Morocco | [Fibre to the home reached 1.5m lines](https://www.anrt.ma/observatoires/donnees-du-secteur/mobile), about half of wireline internet, with [passive sharing](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f) opening commercially and [joint ventures](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/maroc-telecom-inwi-partner-to-deploy-fiber-and-5g) holding shared assets. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator's first-quarter 2026 observatory gives fixed lines at 3.262m, up 5.05 per cent, and fibre to the home at 1.5m, about 52 per cent of wireline internet ([observatory](https://www.anrt.ma/observatoires/donnees-du-secteur/mobile)).<br><br>Technical prerequisites for passive infrastructure sharing were completed in April 2026 with commercial opening scheduled for 27 April over a 40,000-line lot ([sharing](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f)); the date is the operator's own filing and no source confirms the opening happened ([sharing](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f)).<br><br>Two operators formed joint ventures on shared fibre and mobile-site assets in April 2025, with no further reporting on file; the regulator flagged uneven low-density coverage as a risk of the shared model ([joint ventures](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/maroc-telecom-inwi-partner-to-deploy-fiber-and-5g)).</details> | Advanced |
| Mozambique | The incumbent's modernisation facility covers [7,600 km of fibre and capacity from 10 to 400 Gbit/s](https://www.bu.edu/gdp/files/2022/11/GCI_PB_012_EN_FIN.pdf), last reported 57 per cent complete in 2023. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The scope is 1,350 antennas, 7,600 km of fibre and capacity raised from 10 to 400 Gbit/s, at 57 per cent complete in September 2023 with completion expected in May 2024 ([facility](https://www.bu.edu/gdp/files/2022/11/GCI_PB_012_EN_FIN.pdf)).<br><br>No completion or delivery report has entered the base since. It is the only national backbone build on record, and its last measured position is three years old.</details> | No change |
| Namibia | The backbone runs to [18,719.5 km](https://mict.gov.na/documents/869282/6089169/MICT_2025_2026_Budget_Speech+Full+Version.pdf/9b15c163-e790-96b8-f1af-ebd9573367b5?download=true&t=1746624578199), and the state operator is laying redundant long-distance routes after network failures. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ministry's budget motivation gives the network length as 18,719.5 km, longest in //Karas at 3,340.48 km, Otjozondjupa at 2,624.34 km and Erongo at 2,547.72 km ([motivation](https://mict.gov.na/documents/869282/6089169/MICT_2025_2026_Budget_Speech+Full+Version.pdf/9b15c163-e790-96b8-f1af-ebd9573367b5?download=true&t=1746624578199)).<br><br>The state fixed operator said in May 2026 that it is laying new long-distance routes and creating redundant paths between key cities and regions, acknowledging network problems customers had experienced ([modernisation](https://www.telecom.na/media-centre/902-telecom-namibia-affirms-commitment-to-network-modernisation-amid-ongoing-service-improvements)), and signed a second open-access fibre partnership in June 2026, live in five named localities ([partnership](https://www.telecom.na/media-centre/907-telecom-namibia-strengthens-fibre-expansion-through-strategic-open-access-partnership-with-lightstruck)).<br><br>That modernisation is financed by a seven-year N$405 million social loan from a commercial bank ([loan](https://www.nampa.org/text/22896359)).<br><br>No later backbone length is published, so the redundancy work cannot be read as added kilometres.</details> | Advanced |
| Niger | [1,031 km on five cross-border axes](https://www.lesahel.org/projet-de-la-dorsale-transsaharienne-a-fibre-optique-un-levier-essentiel-pour-le-desenclavement-numerique-du-niger/) were provisionally accepted in November 2025, above a [2,275 km domestic backbone](https://china.aiddata.org/projects/58683/) completed in 2017. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The trans-Sahara acceptance covers five axes to Algeria, Chad, Nigeria, Benin and Burkina Faso plus an 88 km loop to the national data centre, under a project launched in 2017 with works from 2021 and financed at about EUR 43m through a concessional window and national counterpart funds ([acceptance](https://www.lesahel.org/projet-de-la-dorsale-transsaharienne-a-fibre-optique-un-levier-essentiel-pour-le-desenclavement-numerique-du-niger/), [reception](https://anp.ne/niger-reception-provisoire-des-troncons-du-projet-de-la-dorsale-transsaharienne-a-fibre-optique/), [financing](https://www.afdb.org/fr/news-and-events/press-releases/le-niger-franchit-une-etape-majeure-vers-la-connectivite-haut-debit-avec-la-reception-de-plus-de-1-000-kilometres-de-fibre-optique-88765)).<br><br>The domestic backbone runs 2,275 km across three sections, built under a concessional loan of RMB 623.7m signed in January 2014 and completed in the first quarter of 2017, aimed at raising national broadband coverage from 54 to 72 per cent ([loan](https://china.aiddata.org/projects/58683/), [backbone](https://china.aiddata.org/projects/58683/)). Repayment responsibility was assigned to the incumbent, whose legal relationship to the loan is unresolved in the source.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | [Project BRIDGE](https://thesun.ng/will-90000km-fibre-move-nigeria-from-buffering-to-better-browsing/) has a US$1.6bn appraised envelope and an October 2026 start, against [about 101,000 km already built](https://businessday.ng/technology/article/how-nigeria-built-101000km-of-fibre-that-bypasses-130m-nigerians/) that bypasses some 130m Nigerians. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The programme's special purpose vehicle is incorporated with private investors at 51 to 75 per cent and the state at 25 to 49 ([execution](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/10/project-bridge-enters-execution-phase-with-bridge-oa-incorporation/)); the minister stated in August 2026 that the 90,000 km rollout begins in October and that funding is raised, against the 2027 start for physical deployment the project documents carry ([execution](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/10/project-bridge-enters-execution-phase-with-bridge-oa-incorporation/), [rollout](https://punchng.com/fg-to-begin-90000km-fibre-rollout-in-october-minister/), [envelope](https://techcabal.com/2026/08/04/nigerias-project-bridge-targets-90000km-fibre-network-by-2030/)).<br><br>The financier's own two figures are five weeks apart and on different bases: an appraisal counting 30,000 km of terrestrial fibre deployed against the 120,000 km the national blueprint requires and stating flatly that there is no national backbone network, and a board statement framing the programme as extending the backbone from 35,000 km to 125,000 ([appraisal](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099091125121538069/txt/BOSIB-f3e6411b-ac7d-490f-a0ef-af1e4a6d1a28.txt), [approval](https://www.worldbank.org/en/news/press-release/2025/10/07/world-bank-approves-new-financing-to-enhance-nigeria-digital-infrastructure-and-health-security)).<br><br>About 101,000 km of fibre has been built and the finding is that it bypasses some 130m Nigerians, a utilisation and last-mile problem rather than a backbone one ([utilisation](https://businessday.ng/technology/article/how-nigeria-built-101000km-of-fibre-that-bypasses-130m-nigerians/)); an operator lit a 400G and 800G optical backbone in October 2025 ([optical](https://techafricanews.com/2025/10/22/mtn-nigeria-and-huawei-launch-nations-first-400g-800g-hybrid-optical-network-in-lagos/)).</details> | Mixed, a 90,000 km open-access programme moving into execution while the fibre already built bypasses 130m people |
| Rwanda | A [government network](https://www.newtimes.co.rw/article/27947/news/technology/inside-rwandas-plan-to-fast-track-digital-transformation-by-2026) carrying US$33.5m is named as a project subcomponent and has not moved in the base in twelve months. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It is named as a subcomponent of the acceleration project with a stated allocation and nothing else: no route, capacity, coverage or completion figure ([network](https://www.newtimes.co.rw/article/27947/news/technology/inside-rwandas-plan-to-fast-track-digital-transformation-by-2026)).<br><br>A named national system carrying that much money and generating no reporting for a year is the clearest instance in this unit of the reporting problem the acceleration project has generally.</details> | No change |
| Sierra Leone | The [national fibre backbone reaches 14 of 16 districts](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/sierra-leone-invests-400m-in-telecoms-60-usage-gap-remains), a marginal advance with the last two districts unstated as to timing. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Backbone coverage was reported at 14 of 16 districts in May 2026 ([backbone](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/sierra-leone-invests-400m-in-telecoms-60-usage-gap-remains), [centre](https://www.slena.gov.sl/post/sierra-leone-s-president-julius-maada-bio-commissions-natca-hq-reinforces-commitment-to-digital-inn), [ambition](https://techcabal.com/2025/07/31/sierra-leone-tech-ambitions/)).<br><br>No date, cost or route is stated for the remaining two, and no utilisation or last-mile figure accompanies the coverage count. The evidence recorded separately is that the gap in this country is usage rather than reach.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | The regulator convened the [first submarine cable technical working group](https://sonna.so/en/article/NCA-Holds-First-Somalia-Submarine-Cable-Technical-Working-Group-Workshop) in June 2026; the terrestrial backbone is still being designed under a [feasibility consultancy](https://moct.gov.so/en/moct-launches-technical-feasibility-study-and-security-risk-management-consultancy-for-eardip-somalia-2/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The working group brought cable operators, internet service providers and technical experts together on outage management, competition, infrastructure development and implementation of the submarine cable regulatory framework ([workshop](https://sonna.so/en/article/NCA-Holds-First-Somalia-Submarine-Cable-Technical-Working-Group-Workshop)).<br><br>The consultancy launched in October 2025 is mapping existing broadband infrastructure and designing backbone and cross-border routes ([launch](https://moct.gov.so/en/moct-launches-technical-feasibility-study-and-security-risk-management-consultancy-for-eardip-somalia-2/)), funded by the regional project's grant agreement ([agreement](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099062123164542123/pdf/P17618108dc2460600945a0f83353acb3fb.pdf)).<br><br>No route kilometre figure for a national terrestrial backbone appears anywhere in the base.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | The flagship broadband programme was [declared ended](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/budget-2026-sa-connect-phase-two-comes-to-an-end/Pero3MZ3zXXqQb6m) and the state network merger [pushed to FY2028/29](https://techcentral.co.za/state-broadband-merger-limps-into-a-second-decade/280723/), while an [amendment Bill on rapid deployment was tabled](https://www.parliament.gov.za/storage/app/media/Bills/2026/B12-2026_Electronic_Communications_AB.pdf) and a [private wholesale 5G network opened](https://www.techcentral.co.za/south-africa-to-get-a-new-national-5g-broadband-network/285080/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-02 — the flagship broadband programme [was declared ended in the Budget Review rather than completed](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/budget-2026-sa-connect-phase-two-comes-to-an-end/Pero3MZ3zXXqQb6m), against a 2022 cabinet target of universal access in 36 months.<br><br>2026-04-28 — the state network merger went backwards: the deliverable for the year was a finalised road map, and [the plan tabled in March 2026 starts again with a preliminary report and implementation in FY2028/29](https://techcentral.co.za/state-broadband-merger-limps-into-a-second-decade/280723/).<br><br>2026-04-20 — an [Electronic Communications Amendment Bill was tabled](https://www.parliament.gov.za/storage/app/media/Bills/2026/B12-2026_Electronic_Communications_AB.pdf) that would make not-for-profit network services licence-exempt and require a national standard by-law providing a uniform wayleave process; [the committee extended written submissions to 21 September 2026](https://www.citizen.co.za/news/south-africa/parliament-extends-deadline-public-input-on-electronic-communications-bill/). The regulator separately [moved to compel every licensed operator to submit geo-referenced data on all passive infrastructure twice a year](https://techcentral.co.za/icasa-moves-to-mandate-national-infrastructure-database/280056/), with fines up to R1m.<br><br>2026-08 — a [privately financed wholesale 5G home-broadband network opened wholesale sales](https://www.techcentral.co.za/south-africa-to-get-a-new-national-5g-broadband-network/285080/), with the first retail provider due live in September 2026; its chief executive accepted the description that this is the open-access network the state spent a decade trying to licence. No coverage footprint, site count or wholesale price is published, so what entered service cannot be sized.</details> | Mixed, a programme closed and a merger deferred while a rapid-deployment Bill and a wholesale network moved |
| South Sudan | A [commercial fibre project moved to execution](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/4204/nct-south-sudan-and-bayobab-collaborate-to-roll-out-national-backbone-fiber-network-project) in early 2026, and the [state backbone programme](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099063026190082191/txt/P176181-84d9770b-853c-4bb8-8e5a-996eac681382.txt) states fibre tendering starting in the second half of 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The commercial project holds a fifteen-year licence and moved to execution with a local partner in early 2026 ([execution](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/4204/nct-south-sudan-and-bayobab-collaborate-to-roll-out-national-backbone-fiber-network-project), [licence](https://techafricanews.com/2025/11/25/mtn-digital-infrastructure-secures-15-year-license-to-expand-fibre-network-in-south-sudan/)).<br><br>The state programme runs about 2,400 km through a neighbour ([tendering](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099063026190082191/txt/P176181-84d9770b-853c-4bb8-8e5a-996eac681382.txt)); the design budget and route length are the government's own figures, and the lender's implementation report puts tendering in the second half of 2026 ([tendering](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099063026190082191/txt/P176181-84d9770b-853c-4bb8-8e5a-996eac681382.txt), [network](https://techafricanews.com/2025/10/09/south-sudan-announces-2400-km-fiber-optic-network-to-strengthen-digital-infrastructure/), [design](https://www.onecitizendaily.com/index.php/2025/06/30/govt-approves-9-million-for-fiber-optic-design/), [route](https://itweb.africa/article/south-sudan-to-lay-fibre-from-kenya/lwrKx73Y94lqmg1o)).<br><br>Neither has laid cable on any record held.</details> | Advanced |
| Sudan | The incumbent added [1,586 km of backbone and 94 sites in 2025](https://sudatel.sd/en/fy2025-results/), taking group-wide terrestrial fibre past 25,000 km. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The operator's own results announcement for the year to 31 December 2025 reports over 25,000 km of terrestrial fibre group-wide, 1,586 km of new backbone added and 94 new sites built, alongside stakes in three submarine systems ([results](https://sudatel.sd/en/fy2025-results/)).<br><br>These are group figures across the operator's footprint, not a Sudan-only count, and they are the company's own.<br><br>The audited statements for the same year carry a qualified opinion, because war-related restrictions on access to Khartoum, Darfur and Kordofan sites prevented verification of property, plant and equipment ([statements](https://sudatel.sd/files/Q4_2025_EN.pdf)).</details> | Advanced |
| Tanzania | The prime minister committed to further national backbone investment and higher rural fund allocations ([statement](https://www.ippmedia.co.tz/nipashe/habari/kitaifa/read/serikali-kuimarisha-huduma-mawasiliano-mkongo-wa-taifa-2026-08-27-114208)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The prime minister committed to continued investment in the communications sector, including strengthening the national backbone and raising funding for the rural communications fund ([statement](https://www.ippmedia.co.tz/nipashe/habari/kitaifa/read/serikali-kuimarisha-huduma-mawasiliano-mkongo-wa-taifa-2026-08-27-114208)). No amount or route length is stated.<br><br>A contradiction between three figures is resolved: 16,280 km is the planned total, 13,820 km the length laid at April 2025, and 15,167 km the length at December 2025 ([backbone](https://www.tcra.go.tz/uploads/text-editor/files/MINISTERS%20STATEMENT%20WITSD%202026_1779118337.pdf)); the "over 15,000 km" quoted at an international summit in July 2026 is that December figure, correctly stated ([backbone](https://www.tcra.go.tz/uploads/text-editor/files/MINISTERS%20STATEMENT%20WITSD%202026_1779118337.pdf), [budget](https://www.mawasiliano.go.tz/uploads/documents/sw-1747383343-SUMMARY_HOTUBA_YA_BAJETI_2025-26_FINAL%5B1%5D.pdf), [zanzibar](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/24/tanzania-president-mwinyi-reviews-ttcl-digital-connectivity-zanzibar/), [sector](https://dailynews.co.tz/communications-sector-surges-400pc/)).<br><br>It is built as a state network: the regional manager states phase one connected government offices and public institutions, with individual citizens only next. A backbone user figure for one region was given to the minister and no national connected-user total is held ([users](https://www.mwananchi.co.tz/mw/habari/kitaifa/waziri-kairuki-watanzania-wengi-hawajanufaika-na-mkongo-wa-taifa-5536718)).<br><br>A cross-border terrestrial link was launched in July 2026 with no capacity, cost or financing stated, and its relation to a 2025 connection is unreconciled ([link](https://www.kenyanews.go.ke/kenya-and-tanzania-launch-terrestrial-fiber-link-connection/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | Parliament approved an [RMB 1.05bn loan for backbone phase five](https://www.parliament.go.ug/documents/3743/hansards-2023-december) in December 2023, with no newer figure, and service-centre throughput was made contingent on it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The loan approval is the last figure on file for the backbone programme ([loan](https://www.parliament.go.ug/documents/3743/hansards-2023-december)).<br><br>Making the service centres' throughput contingent on a backbone phase whose latest reported position is a 2023 loan approval is the dependency worth stating.<br><br>A 260 km commercial route carrying over 1 Tbit/s was lit before the window opened ([route](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/business/kampala-malaba-get-new-fibre-optic-route-NV_210273_052026)), and a gigabit fixed broadband product launched in May 2026 with no subscriber, price or footprint figure published ([product](https://itweb.africa/article/savanna-fibre-uganda-unveils-1-gbps-fixed-broadband/xnklOqz1mAzM4Ymz)).</details> | No change |
| Zambia | The [backbone extension financing stood at 0 per cent disbursement](https://dailyrevelationzambia.com/chitala-says-lungu-told-him-against-proceeding-on-392-million-huawei-deal-as-it-was-more-scandalous-than-the-purchase-of-42-fire-tenders/) as of July 2022 — the largest single undrawn digital-infrastructure commitment the base holds — and the two [national fibre-length figures](https://techafricanews.com/2026/05/15/zambia-approves-k255-million-to-upgrade-digital-infrastructure-and-network-quality/) are irreconcilable. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Corruption allegations from a former utility board chairman are on the record against the undrawn commitment ([extension](https://dailyrevelationzambia.com/chitala-says-lungu-told-him-against-proceeding-on-392-million-huawei-deal-as-it-was-more-scandalous-than-the-purchase-of-42-fire-tenders/)).<br><br>Neither fibre-length figure states whether it counts backbone only or backbone plus metropolitan and access network, which is why they cannot be reconciled ([length](https://techafricanews.com/2026/05/15/zambia-approves-k255-million-to-upgrade-digital-infrastructure-and-network-quality/), [appraisal](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099012025023033676/txt/P505094-630a5ef9-d23a-4221-b7c8-397c94a90b4d.txt)).<br><br>A second communication towers programme reached 87.8 per cent disbursement on ministry reporting through a third-party database ([towers](https://www.lusakatimes.com/2017/06/28/government-gets-280-million-loan-china-bank-finance-communication-towers/)). One programme nearly drawn and one not drawn at all, both Chinese-financed, is the backbone record.</details> | Mixed, a backbone extension financed and 0 per cent drawn while the two national fibre-length figures cannot be reconciled |
| Zimbabwe | Two [ministerial fibre-length figures four months apart](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200058335/internet-traffic-surges-57) reconcile neither with each other nor with the regulator's series, and a [cross-border corridor](https://itweb.africa/article/powertel-paratus-zimbabwe-switch-on-new-digital-highway/Gb3Bw7WadXZq2k6V) carries traffic on phase one. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Two ministerial figures four months apart and a regulator series that matches neither is the measurement position ([length](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200058335/internet-traffic-surges-57), [report](https://technomag.co.zw/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Q4-2025-Abridged-Sector-performance-report-HMed-final-1.pdf)).<br><br>The corridor's first phase runs on an indefeasible right-of-use model with the state operator retaining operational control ([corridor](https://itweb.africa/article/powertel-paratus-zimbabwe-switch-on-new-digital-highway/Gb3Bw7WadXZq2k6V), [switch-on](https://www.newsday.co.zw/local-news/article/200057978/powertel-join-paratus-zimbabwe-to-launch-new-digital-highway)).<br><br>Government arrears owed to the state fixed operator doubled on the chief executive's statement at an annual general meeting; the two currency figures are the company's own ([arrears](https://www.newsday.co.zw/business/article/200057743/govt-debt-to-telone-doubles-to-us42m)) — the state is the largest debtor of the operator carrying its backbone.</details> | Mixed, a cross-border corridor switched on while ministerial fibre-length figures do not reconcile with each other or the regulator |

## International internet bandwidth

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | Equipped international capacity [exceeds 10.2 terabits per second](http://news.radioalgerie.dz/fr/node/77708), up from 1.5 in early 2020. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-01-15 - the minister told parliament that [equipped international bandwidth capacity now exceeds 10.2 terabits per second, against 1.5 terabits in early 2020](http://news.radioalgerie.dz/fr/node/77708) - close to a sevenfold rise over six years, on the minister's own figures.<br><br>2025-10-23 - the minister [gave the go-ahead for a submarine fibre-optic cable project departing from the capital's port, intended to expand the routes available](https://www.aps.dz/fr/algerie/education-et-technologie/mh3smuck-l-algerie-renforce-ses-infrastructures-numeriques-avec-le-lancement-du-projet-du-cable-sous-marin-medusa).<br><br>What is not published is utilisation: no figure for how much of the equipped capacity carries traffic, and no wholesale price, appears on this ledger.</details> | Advanced |
| Angola | A new cable branch [entered service with 23.6 Tbps allocated to Angola](https://angop.ao/noticias/economia/unitel-invete-mais-de-40-milhoes-de-dolares-americano-em-fibra-optica/), and [1.0 Tbps was contracted through a neighbouring landing](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/15/telecom-namibia-angola-telecom-and-satcab-sign-sarssy-deal-to-deliver-1-tbps-connectivity-across-southern-africa/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-12-13 — the 2Africa branch and its landing station [were inaugurated and switched on, built by the incumbent for US$43m, with 23.6 Tbps of the cable's capacity allocated to Angola, replacing an end-of-life system and one already at capacity](https://angop.ao/noticias/economia/unitel-invete-mais-de-40-milhoes-de-dolares-americano-em-fibra-optica/). The incumbent is the only Angolan member of the consortium.<br><br>2026-06-15 — the state operator [signed a master service agreement for an initial 1.0 Tbps of dedicated capacity through a Namibian landing of the Equiano cable, with an option to assume ownership](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/15/telecom-namibia-angola-telecom-and-satcab-sign-sarssy-deal-to-deliver-1-tbps-connectivity-across-southern-africa/); design, testing, commissioning and handover are still to run, and the country reaches that cable only indirectly.<br><br>[Further submarine expansion is stated as ministry policy](https://angop.ao/noticias/tecnologia/minttics-trabalha-para-expandir-rede-de-fibra-optica-submarina/). No price, utilisation or wholesale capacity figure is published for any of it, so the base can state what has been added and not what it costs or carries.</details> | Advanced |
| Botswana | A first direct cross-border fibre link with Namibia [was completed and interconnected](https://observer24.com.na/trans-kalahari-4g-rollout-requires-n52m/), while the only national traffic figure held [remains just over 100Gbps from February 2025](https://www.mmegi.bw/business/bofinet-invests-p100m-in-upgrading-botswanas-internet-gateway/news). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-10 — [the Namibian operator completed the Gobabis-Buitepos link, now interconnecting at Charles Hill, which the two sides describe as the first direct cross-border fibre link between them](https://observer24.com.na/trans-kalahari-4g-rollout-requires-n52m/), against [a memorandum only in February 2026](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/18/mtc-namibia-and-bofinet-sign-mou-to-strengthen-cross-border-connectivity/).
