No capacity, commissioning date or traffic figure is published for the link and the corridor beyond it is not reported delivered.<br><br>2026-07-06 — [a second corridor's first phase is live at 800Gbps on equipment rated over 10Tbps, with phase two due September 2026](https://itweb.africa/article/powertel-paratus-zimbabwe-switch-on-new-digital-highway/Gb3Bw7WadXZq2k6V); it is described as a three-country corridor and the live section is inside a neighbour, with no Botswana segment dated.<br><br>Measurement has not moved with the build. [International traffic was just over 100Gbps in February 2025, up from under 8Gbps in 2015, after a P100m gateway upgrade](https://www.mmegi.bw/business/bofinet-invests-p100m-in-upgrading-botswanas-internet-gateway/news), and no later figure has been published — the only national capacity figure the base holds, eighteen months old at the window's close.</details> | Advanced |
| Burkina Faso | Nigeria [proposed connecting its planned 90,000 km fibre network to Burkina Faso's borders](https://burkina24.com/2026/08/12/cooperation-numerique-sous-regionale-le-burkina-faso-et-le-nigeria-sallient-pour-accelerer-leur-transformation-digitale/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[At a bilateral meeting of the two digital-transformation ministers on 12 August 2026, Nigeria proposed connecting its planned 90,000 km open-access national fibre network to Burkina Faso's borders to improve internet resilience, quality and affordability, alongside cooperation on artificial intelligence, digital public infrastructure and talent](https://burkina24.com/2026/08/12/cooperation-numerique-sous-regionale-le-burkina-faso-et-le-nigeria-sallient-pour-accelerer-leur-transformation-digitale/).<br><br>It is a proposed corridor and not a live link: no agreement, route, capacity or date is held, and the Nigerian network it would connect to is itself planned rather than built.</details> | Advanced |
| Burundi | International capacity [runs through neighbouring countries' submarine landings](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/RAPPORT-SUR-LES-INDICATEURS-TIC-2020-VERSION-PUBLIE.pdf); no capacity figure is published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The regulator's indicators report records submarine cable access to EASSy, SEACOM and TEAMS through neighbouring countries, cross-border fibre interconnection points with Rwanda, the Democratic Republic of the Congo and Tanzania, and regional roaming](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/RAPPORT-SUR-LES-INDICATEURS-TIC-2020-VERSION-PUBLIE.pdf).<br><br>For a landlocked country the international bandwidth position is its neighbours' landings and the transit bought over them. What the base does not hold is any capacity figure in Gbps, any wholesale transit price, or any statement of redundancy — which is the part that matters, since a single cross-border route is a single point of failure for the whole country's international traffic.</details> | No change |
| Cameroon | A [memorandum to join the Medusa Africa submarine cable was finalised in December 2025](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1012-51258-cameroon-finalizes-mou-to-connect-to-medusa-africa-submarine-cable-system). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Cameroon finalised a memorandum of understanding to join the Medusa Africa submarine cable project, stated to be operational by 2028 and to improve connectivity with Europe, Africa and Asia](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1012-51258-cameroon-finalizes-mou-to-connect-to-medusa-africa-submarine-cable-system).<br><br>It is a memorandum and not a landing: no landing station, capacity, cost or in-service date for Cameroon is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Cape Verde | [EllaLink came ashore at Praia on 18 February 2021 with a stated 30 TB capacity](https://www.mf.gov.cv/web/mf/-/cabo-submarino-ellalink-j%C3%A1-est%C3%A1-em-cabo-verde). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The EllaLink submarine cable came ashore at Portinho beach, Achada Grande Tras, in Praia on 18 February 2021; the Secretary of State for Innovation called it a historic moment for the country's telecommunications, and the government's record gives the low-latency fibre system a capacity of 30 TB, more than all existing cables combined](https://www.mf.gov.cv/web/mf/-/cabo-submarino-ellalink-j%C3%A1-est%C3%A1-em-cabo-verde). [A second ECOWAS regional submarine cable reached feasibility-study validation at a Monrovia workshop in October 2025](https://www.ecowas.int/liberia-hosts-workshop-to-validate-ecowass-second-submarine-cable/).<br><br>No inventory naming every system serving Cabo Verde, its landing station and its in-service date is held, and no lit or used capacity figure is on file for any of them.</details> | No change, a standing position the base holds for the first time |
| Central African Republic | A published assessment set out the options for reaching submarine cable capacity other than through Cameroon ([assessment](https://www.camerounlink.com/news/2026/9/1/acces-aux-cables-sous-marins-quelles-options-pour-la-centrafrique-au-dela-du-cameroun-156491/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A published assessment set out the options for reaching submarine cable capacity other than through Cameroon ([assessment](https://www.camerounlink.com/news/2026/9/1/acces-aux-cables-sous-marins-quelles-options-pour-la-centrafrique-au-dela-du-cameroun-156491/)). It is an analysis, not a route secured.</details> | Advanced |
| Chad | The [fibre corridor memorandum to a neighbour](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fiber-networking/chad-tells-operators-to-fast-track-fiber-link-to-cameroon) was suspended pending revision, and that route is the country's only entry point for international submarine-cable capacity. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The ministry ordered the two operators to correct the clauses and sign; the date of the suspension is not established in any source held ([corridor](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fiber-networking/chad-tells-operators-to-fast-track-fiber-link-to-cameroon), [analysis](https://russellsouthwood.substack.com/p/waking-the-dead-slow-road-to-extinction), [sites](https://www.ecofinagency.com/news-digital/1302-52879-chad-plans-50-telecom-sites-50-km-of-fiber-in-n-djamena)).<br><br>For a landlocked country this route is the only entry point for international submarine-cable capacity on the record. Suspending the instrument that would build it is the single most consequential regression in this unit.</details> | Regressed |
| Comoros | The regulator [approved the state cable company's 2025-2026 access catalogue](https://anrtic.km/media/media/avis_decisions/IHJnhQCoMBHNgK4z8b3XJtnOout2856oxqVkrc0B.pdf), setting wholesale international connectivity prices. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-07-17 - a regulator decision [approved the access catalogue, setting the wholesale prices at which a state-owned company sells the country's international connectivity](https://anrtic.km/media/media/avis_decisions/IHJnhQCoMBHNgK4z8b3XJtnOout2856oxqVkrc0B.pdf).<br><br>The tariff schedule itself is a separate document and is not held, the regulator publishes no decision series so no prior catalogue exists in the base, and the held decision is an excerpt of a two-page scan. What the country pays for international capacity, and whether the approved prices moved, cannot be stated from anything on file.</details> | Advanced |
| Congo | A switch to an alternative submarine cable was [stated to be three weeks away in July 2026](https://www.adiac-congo.com/content/numeriquegouvernance-frederic-malik-nguema-nze-fait-de-larpce-son-fer-de-lance-170823); no source confirms any new cable carrying traffic. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The country has [relied on one cable since 2012, and after weeks of outages sought a new subsea connection in January 2026](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/congo-wants-to-connect-to-new-cable-amidst-wacs-failures).<br><br>2026-07-14 - the regulator [stated a connection to another cable could be operational within three weeks, with one alternative cable delayed](https://www.adiac-congo.com/content/numeriquegouvernance-frederic-malik-nguema-nze-fait-de-larpce-son-fer-de-lance-170823). No source since confirms any new cable carrying traffic.<br><br>Single-cable dependence is the structural fact under every connectivity row here, and no capacity figure, landing station or redundancy arrangement is published for the cable actually in service.</details> | Stalled |
| Cote d'Ivoire | A [consortium memorandum was signed for an Atlantic cable with an Ivorian landing](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2641940/societe/publi-reportage/via-africa-un-nouveau-projet-de-cable-sous-marin-pour-renforcer-la-connectivite-entre-leurope-et-lafrique), and an existing branch was repaired. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05-15 - a [Europe-to-South Africa Atlantic system with an Ivorian landing had its consortium memorandum signed, its route survey funded and supplier selection being prepared](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2641940/societe/publi-reportage/via-africa-un-nouveau-projet-de-cable-sous-marin-pour-renforcer-la-connectivite-entre-leurope-et-lafrique). No landing date, capacity or cost is stated, and the held Ivorian account is a reprint of the consortium release.<br><br>2026-08-02 - the [2Africa Ivory Coast branch was restored to service after an outage, with WACS repairs expected the following week](https://subseacables.blogspot.com/2026/08/2africa-ivory-coast-branch-live-again.html). The outage's start date, cause and duration are not stated, and no capacity figure for any landing is on file.</details> | Advanced |
| Djibouti | [Twelve submarine systems land in the country, carrying about 18% of international submarine cable data](https://www.med-or.org/news/il-ministro-bernini-a-gibuti-e-il-progetto-promosso-da-med-or-sullunderwater), and a southern extension is under build. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Twelve operational systems were reached by the two carrier-neutral facilities in June 2025](https://www.connectingafrica.com/data-centers/wingu-africa-secures-60m-to-fuel-east-african-expansion), and [twelve now land, six of them connected to Italy, carrying about 18% of international submarine cable data](https://www.med-or.org/news/il-ministro-bernini-a-gibuti-e-il-progetto-promosso-da-med-or-sullunderwater). Both counts are the reporting parties' own; no landing register is held.<br><br>2025-09 - a [southern extension of the regional cable was announced at 36 Tbit/s over about 3,200 to 3,500 km, ready for service in 2028](https://techafricanews.com/2025/09/01/djibouti-telecom-expands-dare1-cable-to-connect-east-and-southern-africa/), [reported across three outlets](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/dare1-subsea-cable-to-link-kenya-to-south-africa).<br><br>2026-08-03 - an [Italian cable inspection and protection project at the landing station was reported during a ministerial mission, under a memorandum with the finance ministry and with no amount, term or delivery date](https://www.med-or.org/news/il-ministro-bernini-a-gibuti-e-il-progetto-promosso-da-med-or-sullunderwater).</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | [About fifteen active international submarine systems land in the country, the largest concentration in Africa](https://english.ahram.org.eg/NewsContentP/3/562063/Business/Africa-cable-boosts-Egypt-digital-exports-amid-ris.aspx). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The base held [thirteen systems landing on the eastern coast with a stated plan to expand to eighteen](https://ir.te.eg/en/CorporateNews/PressRelease/211/Telecom-Egypt-s-Regional-Data-Hub-2-Awarded-Tier-III-Design-Certification), and now [about fifteen active systems, the largest concentration in Africa](https://english.ahram.org.eg/NewsContentP/3/562063/Business/Africa-cable-boosts-Egypt-digital-exports-amid-ris.aspx). Both figures are operator or ministry-reported and no independent register is held.<br><br>The largest system carries [Mediterranean capacity in service since January 2025 on the Port Said to Genoa and Marseille route, with the country a transit hub rather than a terminal landing, the whole stated at about 45,000 km across thirty-three or more countries and designed for up to 180 Tbps on main routes](https://gate.ahram.org.eg/News/5081510.aspx). [No later source updates the vendor partnership behind that activation](https://gate.ahram.org.eg/News/5081510.aspx).<br><br>[An agreement was signed on 3 September 2026 to construct a multiple-petabit Sharm El Sheikh to Taba subsea cable, framed by the incumbent as extending the country's eastern digital corridor](https://ir.te.eg/en/CorporateNews/PressRelease/10246/Telecom-Egypt-to-Advance-Egypt-s-Eastern-Digital-Corridor-with-a-Multiple-Petabit-Sharm-El-Sheikh-Taba-Subsea-Cable-in-Accordance-with-its-New-Strategy). It is a domestic route rather than an international landing, and no cost or in-service date is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Equatorial Guinea | The country lands [three submarine systems](https://afr-ix.com/equatorial-guinea-and-cameroon-are-now-more-connected/), has two more under study or agreement, and since November 2025 [exchanges traffic overland with Gabon](https://spin.ga/alliance-historique-numerique-gabon-guinee-equatoriale/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>One regional system was inaugurated in 2018 on a 400 km route between the two main cities and a neighbour, alongside an earlier landing; no later capacity or utilisation figure is held ([regional cables](https://afr-ix.com/equatorial-guinea-and-cameroon-are-now-more-connected/)).<br><br>The state infrastructure operator is landing party for the continental system and hosted its maintenance committee in May 2026, with capacity unchanged on the record ([landing party](https://realequatorialguinea.com/tecnologia/gitge-anfitrion-del-36o-encuentro-del-consorcio-ace-sobre-operacion-y-mantenimiento-de-cable-submarino/)).<br><br>A Mediterranean-anchored system is under study at a landing in one of two towns, put at EUR 20-60m and 2029 to 2030 with an alternative route also named, the existing cable reported to degrade quality which is the stated reason for diversifying ([study](https://realequatorialguinea.com/destacado/politica/guinea-ecuatorial-estudia-conectarse-a-un-nuevo-cable-submarino-para-mejorar-el-internet/), [routes](https://www.larazon.es/sociedad/cables-submarinos-que-conectan-africa-resto-mundo_202606056a227fb59c159d030fd0e65c.html)).<br><br>A subsea fibre agreement with Nigeria was signed in February 2026 bundling a data centre, with no route, capacity or delivery date published ([agreement](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fiber-networking/nigeria-equatorial-guinea-subsea-cable-to-strengthen-regional-connectivity), [signature](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/reports-and-coverage/28289-nigeria-and-equatorial-guinea-sign-landmark-subsea-fiber-optic-agreement/)).<br><br>2025-11-10 - the two countries' national backbones were [interconnected and inaugurated at Ebibeyin, exchanging traffic on the Bitam-Ebibeyin and Libreville-Bata routes](https://spin.ga/alliance-historique-numerique-gabon-guinee-equatoriale/), the first terrestrial international route on this ledger; no traffic or capacity figure is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Eritrea | Bandwidth is [reported at about 1,536 Mbps after a decade of growth](https://shabait.com/2026/05/19/substantial-investment-in-expansion-of-communication-service/), and it is carried by satellite: [no submarine cable lands in Eritrea](https://russellsouthwood.substack.com/p/the-long-view-what-ict4d-did-for). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05-19 — the ministry [put international bandwidth at about 1,536 Mbps, risen over the past decade](https://shabait.com/2026/05/19/substantial-investment-in-expansion-of-communication-service/).<br><br>2026-04-16 — the industry position is unchanged: Eritrea [is still the only one of Africa's coastal states with no cable landing](https://russellsouthwood.substack.com/p/the-long-view-what-ict4d-did-for), and cable firms describe a country that does not answer landing requests. [An earlier account of the same position](https://www.middleeasteye.net/news/cable-firms-eye-eritrea-yemen-houthi-attacks-threaten-internet-traffic) dates it to June 2024.<br><br>EriTel's international transit therefore runs on geostationary satellite, which is what a bandwidth figure of this size describes. The growth is real and the ceiling is the technology carrying it.</details> | Advanced, on a ministry figure, with no cable landing behind it |
| Eswatini | International capacity on the national backbone stands at [72 Gbps](https://times.co.sz/business/readmore.php?bhsadjgfoh=Optical+fibre+rollout+strengthens+business+ecosystem&bvhdgsj=Business+and+Economy&yiphi=2892), and a June 2026 ruling forced the backbone open after a provider built its own cross-border link. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The national fibre backbone carries 72 Gbps of international capacity with rollout continuing ([backbone](https://times.co.sz/business/readmore.php?bhsadjgfoh=Optical+fibre+rollout+strengthens+business+ecosystem&bvhdgsj=Business+and+Economy&yiphi=2892)).<br><br>The national operator complained that a private provider had built its own cross-border fibre link from a border post, bypassing the backbone. The regulator fined the provider E150,000 and directed the operator to give reliable, non-discriminatory and transparent backbone access and to share its existing connections and fibre topology with internet service providers, effective 1 July 2026 ([ruling](https://esccom.org.sz/publications/notices/docs/EPTC%20AND%20JENNY%20FINAL%20%20DECISION%20Notice%205.pdf)).<br><br>The alternative route out of the country runs to Maputo over powerline fibre and onward to undersea cable at Cape Town ([programme](https://uasf.esccom.org.sz/programmes/connectivity-programmes/)).<br><br>No per-operator bandwidth table, utilisation figure or price is published, so the 72 Gbps cannot be read against demand.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | A multi-terabit corridor to [Jeddah through Djibouti and Sudan](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/20/ethio-telecom-active-deployment-africa-middle-east-fibre-corridor/) entered deployment, the first new international route the base holds. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The corridor is described as multi-terabit and now runs to Saudi Arabia, which would give a landlocked country a second path to international capacity alongside its existing transit ([corridor deployment](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/20/ethio-telecom-active-deployment-africa-middle-east-fibre-corridor/)).<br><br>No capacity figure, in-service date or price is published, and the base holds no measurement of international bandwidth in use, per person or in total, against which to read it.</details> | Advanced |
| Gambia | [15 Gbps through the state incumbent](https://gambia.gov.gm/gambia-app/gambia-telecommunication-company-gamtel/), with a partnership to lift the backbone [from 40 to 800 Gbps](https://standard.gm/gamtel-sysroad-sign-50m-ppp-to-modernise-gambias-broadband-network/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The state incumbent's [own profile](https://gambia.gov.gm/gambia-app/gambia-telecommunication-company-gamtel/) puts its international capacity on the ACE cable at 15 Gbps and its share of the country's cable segment at 49 per cent.<br><br>The [US$50m partnership signed in December 2025](https://standard.gm/gamtel-sysroad-sign-50m-ppp-to-modernise-gambias-broadband-network/) is to take the national backbone from 40 to 800 Gbps. A second submarine cable is further off: [five states evaluated bids in Abuja between 29 June and 10 July 2026](https://www.ecowas.int/ecowas-commission-hosts-evaluation-committee-meeting-for-west-african-submarine-cable-capacity-under-wardip/), and the programme coordinator [flagged the deal as still unsigned](https://standard.gm/two-week-certified-rbm-ict-data-analytics-training-programme-opens/) later that month.</details> | Advanced |
| Ghana | The regulator names [five submarine cable providers](https://nca.org.gh/submarine-cable-landing/) on an undated page, against an analyst count of six cables and about 2.5 Tbps in use. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The landing licence rests on section 3(8) of the 2008 Act and the regulator's list carries no date, so it predates later landings; the six-cable count and the capacity estimate are an analyst's, not the regulator's ([landing page](https://nca.org.gh/submarine-cable-landing/), [market brief](https://cms.d4dhub.eu/assets/Initiatives/Data-Governance-in-Africa/Digital-Investment-Facility/2507_Country-Market-Briefs/Data-Center-Market-Brief-Ghana.pdf)).<br><br>A separate five-year renewable authorisation lets consortium members sell capacity directly to users, charging 1 per cent of revenue annually, lapsing if not operated within two years and requiring three years' notice of non-renewal ([carrier authorisation](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/Electronic-Communication-Submarine-Carrier-Services-Authorisation_November-2024-V5.pdf)); the document held is the template with the grantee left blank ([carrier authorisation](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/Electronic-Communication-Submarine-Carrier-Services-Authorisation_November-2024-V5.pdf)).<br><br>The 2008 Act is the statute under which both landings and 5G licences are granted, and it sources the universal-access fund from contributions stipulated in each licence rather than from any rate set in the Act ([the Act](https://nca.org.gh/wp-content/uploads/2023/04/NCA-Electronic-Communications-Act-775.pdf)).</details> | No change |
| Guinea | The [single 2012 landing is saturated](https://mediaguinee.com/2025/07/atterrissement-dun-second-cable-sous-marin-en-guinee-la-guilab-et-ses-partenaires-se-concertent-a-conakry/), and agreements are now signed or agreed for a second and a third cable. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The cable has been the country's only landing for about thirteen to fifteen years and is named saturated ([sole landing](https://mediaguinee.com/2025/07/atterrissement-dun-second-cable-sous-marin-en-guinee-la-guilab-et-ses-partenaires-se-concertent-a-conakry/)).<br><br>A construction and maintenance agreement was signed in August 2026 to land a Mediterranean-anchored system at Conakry, with the state-majority landing party alongside a mobile operator; no timeline is given for the 8,700km, 19-landing-site system ([second landing](https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/medusa-subsea-cable-to-land-in-guinea/), [agreement](https://mediaguinee.com/2026/05/2e-cable-sous-marin-la-guinee-propulse-sa-souverainete-numerique-avec-la-signature-de-laccord-medusa-africa/)).<br><br>Co-investment in a third system was agreed in Paris in June 2026 with no landing date published ([third landing](https://mediaguinee.com/2026/06/saturation-du-cable-sous-marin-ahmed-karifa-diawara-dg-de-la-guilab-devoile-la-riposte-de-la-guinee/)).<br><br>No capacity figure, price or utilisation is published for the existing link or the two planned, so saturation is asserted and not measured.</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea-Bissau | The ACE submarine cable [remains the country's only international fibre link](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/2908-131059-deploiement-de-la-fibre-optique-la-guinee-bissau-veut-apprendre-du-tchad), with a second, ECOWAS-led cable [approved at feasibility stage in October 2025](https://www.ecowas.int/liberia-hosts-workshop-to-validate-ecowass-second-submarine-cable/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>International capacity rests on a single submarine cable. The ACE landing [is the country's first and only international fibre-optic connection, live since March 2023](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/2908-131059-deploiement-de-la-fibre-optique-la-guinee-bissau-veut-apprendre-du-tchad), and it is held only as background in a secondary record: no primary on the landing itself is on the base.<br><br>2025-10-22 - member-state experts with their technical and financial partners [reviewed and approved the feasibility study for a second submarine cable serving Cabo Verde, Liberia, Sierra Leone and Guinea-Bissau](https://www.ecowas.int/liberia-hosts-workshop-to-validate-ecowass-second-submarine-cable/), at a workshop the ECOWAS Project Preparation and Development Unit convened in Monrovia.<br><br>That cable is at feasibility and nothing further: no route, cost, landing site or construction date is published. Nor does any capacity, price or utilisation figure for the existing link appear on the ledger, so the base can state what carries the traffic and not how much it carries.<br><br>The national backbone that would distribute that capacity inland is still in design.</details> | No change, a second cable approved at feasibility but not built |
| Kenya | [Two new submarine systems](https://www.businessdailyafrica.com/bd/corporate/technology/kenya-to-get-two-new-undersea-fibre-optic-cables-in-ai-race-5547996) are expected, taking the country from seven landings to nine. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A 10,000km France to Middle East system routed through Africa is expected by a research firm to land in 2027, which is a forecast rather than an operator or landing-party statement ([forecast](https://www.businessdailyafrica.com/bd/corporate/technology/kenya-to-get-two-new-undersea-fibre-optic-cables-in-ai-race-5547996)).<br><br>A 4,108km system with 24 fibre pairs from Salalah to Mombasa at US$23m was ready for service in 2026 on its announcement, with the incumbent named landing and operating partner ([cable](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/reports-and-coverage/13219-meta-selects-safaricom-as-its-landing-partner-for-daraja-subsea-cable-project/)); nothing held records landing, testing or service readiness ([cable](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/reports-and-coverage/13219-meta-selects-safaricom-as-its-landing-partner-for-daraja-subsea-cable-project/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | A [managed satellite enterprise service launched in August 2026](https://www.thereporter.co.ls/2026/08/26/vodacom-launches-satellite-broadband-for-enterprises/), the first international path not transiting a neighbour. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2018-02-13 — [the regulator’s sector review describes the international gateways and bandwidth pricing structure](https://lca.org.ls/wp-content/uploads/filr/2334/Final%20Integrated%20Report%2013022018.pdf).<br><br>2026-07-01 — [the regulator’s strategic plan makes universal and meaningful connectivity its top theme](https://lca.org.ls/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/STRATEGIC-PLAN-2026-2029.pdf).<br><br>2026-08-26 — [the incumbent operator launched managed satellite enterprise connectivity](https://www.thereporter.co.ls/2026/08/26/vodacom-launches-satellite-broadband-for-enterprises/).<br><br>Lesotho is landlocked and every terrestrial route crosses South Africa. No capacity figure in gigabits, and no wholesale price, is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | Two routes to a second landing are open: [one at Buchanan](https://elbcnews.com/2026/07/23/commissioner-honnah-reaffirms-liberia-telecom-authoritys-commitment-to-expanding-digital-access/) sought with partial funding, and [an ECOWAS four-country cable approved at feasibility](https://www.ecowas.int/liberia-hosts-workshop-to-validate-ecowass-second-submarine-cable/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-10-22 - member-state experts with their technical and financial partners [reviewed and approved the feasibility study for an ECOWAS second submarine cable serving Cabo Verde, Liberia, Sierra Leone and Guinea-Bissau](https://www.ecowas.int/liberia-hosts-workshop-to-validate-ecowass-second-submarine-cable/) at a workshop the ECOWAS Project Preparation and Development Unit convened in Monrovia; no route, cost, landing site or construction date is published.<br><br>2026-07-23 - the minister announced [a second submarine landing at Buchanan, with partial funding stated as sourced and investors still being sought](https://elbcnews.com/2026/07/23/commissioner-honnah-reaffirms-liberia-telecom-authoritys-commitment-to-expanding-digital-access/) at a technology summit; no landing partner, cost or date is published. Neither record says whether the two are the same project.<br><br>The country has run on one landing in the capital, which is why the agreement to commercialise spare fibre on the regional power grid matters as much as the cable does: both would end sole reliance on a single route.</details> | Advanced |
| Libya | The [Medusa cable](https://libyaherald.com/2025/05/medusa-submarine-cable-project-linking-libya-with-europe-inaugurated) has been live at Misrata since May 2025 and no capacity or bandwidth figure has been published for it since. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The cable was inaugurated at Misrata in May 2025 ([inauguration](https://libyaherald.com/2025/05/medusa-submarine-cable-project-linking-libya-with-europe-inaugurated)).<br><br>No capacity figure, utilisation share or price effect has been published in the fifteen months since, and the base holds no measure of international bandwidth per user for the country at all.</details> | No change |
| Madagascar | A [subsea cable extension](https://2424.mg/infrastructures-le-cable-sous-marin-djiboutien-dare1-setend-a-madagascar/) to Mahajanga and Toliara was announced in September 2025, with work from 2026 and service from 2028. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The extension runs on a leg of 3,200 to 3,500 km via Tanzania and Mozambique and is ready for service in 2028 ([extension](https://2424.mg/infrastructures-le-cable-sous-marin-djiboutien-dare1-setend-a-madagascar/), [route](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/dare1-subsea-cable-to-link-kenya-to-south-africa)).<br><br>No capacity, landing-station or price figure is published, and the base holds no measure of the country's existing international bandwidth against which to set the addition.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | Wholesale bandwidth [fell from about US$460 per Mbit/s a month in 2017 to below US$10 at 2024](https://www.worldbank.org/en/results/2025/06/23/digitalizing-afe-malawi-to-improve-access-to-education-public-services-and-income-opportunities), and no price for 2025 or 2026 is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The fall was reported at the close of the foundations project, on the lender's own figures ([close](https://www.worldbank.org/en/results/2025/06/23/digitalizing-afe-malawi-to-improve-access-to-education-public-services-and-income-opportunities), [account](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202506/malawis-boosts-digital-economy-with-connectivity-plans-2m-digital-id-enrollments)).<br><br>No wholesale price for 2025 or 2026 is held, so whether the gain has held is not established, and a 2024 appraisal instead gives about US$35 per Mbit/s ([appraisal](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202506/malawis-boosts-digital-economy-with-connectivity-plans-2m-digital-id-enrollments)), which the base does not reconcile.</details> | No change |
| Mali | An [independent subsea route with automated rerouting](https://www.mobileeurope.co.uk/csquared-boosts-internet-resilence-integrating-with-2africa-west/) was added behind the inland backbone in July 2026, easing a landlocked country's exposure to three landing points. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The inland backbone integrated capacity on a west-coast subsea system in July 2026, giving an independent route with automated rerouting behind it ([integration](https://www.mobileeurope.co.uk/csquared-boosts-internet-resilence-integrating-with-2africa-west/)).<br><br>The country's inland routes had been exposed to the concentration of landings at Abidjan, Accra and Lagos. The account is the vendor's, and no capacity, price or utilisation figure is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritania | The [one international cable since 2011](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/0505-138141-internet-la-mauritanie-amorce-le-deploiement-de-son-deuxieme-cable-sous-marin) is to be joined by a [second](https://ami.mr/fr/archives/294524) commissioning in early 2027, with a [third financed and unlanded](https://www.eib.org/en/projects/all/20210166). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A single submarine cable has been the country's only international connection since 2011 ([cable](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/0505-138141-internet-la-mauritanie-amorce-le-deploiement-de-son-deuxieme-cable-sous-marin), [baseline](https://www.eib.org/en/press/all/2025-202-inauguration-du-centre-d-hebergement-de-donnees-numeriques-de-nouakchott)).<br><br>The second cable was launched at Nouadhibou in July 2025 at 669 km and 200 Gb/s rising to 12 Tbit/s, European-financed; phase-one coastal landing is complete, offshore laying was scheduled for August 2026 and commissioning is expected in the first quarter of 2027 ([landing](https://ami.mr/fr/archives/294524), [launch](https://ami.mr/fr/archives/276234)).<br><br>A third system's sub-Saharan component is financed with no landing, route or commissioning date on record ([financing](https://www.eib.org/en/projects/all/20210166)). A country dependent on one cable for fifteen years is the position the other two are meant to end.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | [Two ageing cables carry most international traffic](https://journaldudimanche.com/google-amazon-et-maurice-les-coulisses-dun-tournant-numerique-majeur/); a [new cable participation](https://journaldudimanche.com/google-amazon-et-maurice-les-coulisses-dun-tournant-numerique-majeur/) rests on a non-disclosure agreement, and [two further landings](https://lexpress.mu/node/553579) are named in an operator strategy. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Landings exist on four systems, two of which carry most international traffic, and the stated driver for new capacity is infrastructure ageing, with one cable due to retire around 2027 ([cables](https://journaldudimanche.com/google-amazon-et-maurice-les-coulisses-dun-tournant-numerique-majeur/), [estate](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/features/12397-sublime-seas-to-storage-sphere-mauritius-expands-hyperscale-data-hub/)).<br><br>The prime-ministerial commitment to join a new transoceanic cable sits behind a signed non-disclosure agreement and unfinished technical, legal and financial negotiation; no consortium confirmation, landing point, capacity, cost or service date is published, and the operator's own chief executive stayed non-specific on subsea at the same event ([commitment](https://journaldudimanche.com/google-amazon-et-maurice-les-coulisses-dun-tournant-numerique-majeur/), [summit](https://mitci.govmu.org/mitci/mauritius-to-join-america-india-connect-subsea-cable-programme-announces-pm-ramgoolam/), [report](https://subtelforum.com/mauritius-joins-america-india-connect-cable/)).<br><br>Two further landings are named as planned in the operator's strategy, one of them to replace the retiring cable, with no contract, cost or date published ([landings](https://lexpress.mu/node/553579)).</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | The [Medusa subsea cable](https://itweb.africa/article/nokia-powers-subsea-cable-linking-europe-north-africa/LPwQ5MlbjBjvNgkj) selected its optical supplier in July 2025 and no Moroccan landing date has been published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The supplier was selected in July 2025 and nothing further is on file; the system was announced in 2022 and landing agreements are held with licensed local operators ([supplier](https://itweb.africa/article/nokia-powers-subsea-cable-linking-europe-north-africa/LPwQ5MlbjBjvNgkj)).<br><br>No capacity, price or utilisation figure for international bandwidth is held for the country, so the base records one cable in progress and no measure of what the country already has.</details> | No change |
| Mozambique | A [subsea cable southern extension](https://techafricanews.com/2025/09/01/djibouti-telecom-expands-dare1-cable-to-connect-east-and-southern-africa/) announced in 2025 plans a Mozambican landing, with work from 2026 and service from 2028. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The extension plans landings in Tanzania, Mozambique, Madagascar and South Africa, interconnecting at Mombasa, with the Mombasa to Durban route estimated at 3,200 to 3,500 km ([extension](https://techafricanews.com/2025/09/01/djibouti-telecom-expands-dare1-cable-to-connect-east-and-southern-africa/)); no construction start was confirmed by August 2026 ([extension](https://techafricanews.com/2025/09/01/djibouti-telecom-expands-dare1-cable-to-connect-east-and-southern-africa/)).<br><br>No capacity, price or utilisation figure for the country's existing international bandwidth is held, so the addition is on record and the base it adds to is not.</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | A [subsea cable station](https://www.telecom.na/media-centre/797-telecom-namibia-celebrates-32-years-with-historic-inauguration-of-the-%E2%80%9Ctelecom-namibia-equiano-cable-station%E2%80%9D-and-activation-of-capacity-on-equiano-subsea-cable) at Swakopmund carries a 100G link to Johannesburg, and a 2026 agreement commits an initial 1.0 Tbps on a regional system. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The state fixed operator inaugurated its cable station and activated a 100G link between Swakopmund and Johannesburg in July 2024 ([station](https://www.telecom.na/media-centre/797-telecom-namibia-celebrates-32-years-with-historic-inauguration-of-the-%E2%80%9Ctelecom-namibia-equiano-cable-station%E2%80%9D-and-activation-of-capacity-on-equiano-subsea-cable)).<br><br>In June 2026 it signed a master service agreement with two Angolan counterparts for a regional submarine system integrating the existing cable, committing an initial dedicated 1.0 Tbps ([agreement](https://telecom.na/media-centre/910-telecom-namibia,-angola-telecom,-and-satcab-sign-landmark-sarssy-and-equiano-submarine-cable-systems-master-service-agreement-in-angola)); the Namibian operator is the landing party hosting Angolan equipment.<br><br>A low earth orbit satellite broadband service launched commercially in June 2026 through a licensed local partner, a second international route ([satellite](https://www.qkon.com/2026/07/22/q-kon-and-partners-launch-fully-compliant-eutelsat-oneweb/)).<br><br>No lit capacity, utilisation or wholesale price figure is published, so the 1.0 Tbps cannot be read against what is actually carried.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | The last official national statement is the [2020 broadband plan: six submarine cables at Lagos, over 40 Tbps available and under 10 per cent used](https://ncc.gov.ng/sites/default/files/2024-11/Documents/Nigerian_National_Broadband_Plan_2020-2025.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [plan, whose term ran to 2025, carries the last clear official statement of international capacity, dated January 2020](https://ncc.gov.ng/sites/default/files/2024-11/Documents/Nigerian_National_Broadband_Plan_2020-2025.pdf). No successor plan and no later official figure is held, so the position at the end of the window is a six-year-old statement — which is itself the finding.</details> | No change |
| Rwanda | A [new subsea cable system](https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/seacom-plans-25000km-subsea-cable-from-france-to-singapore-and-south-africa/) was announced in July 2026 with no timeline, and for a landlocked country the exposure is the terrestrial leg rather than the cable. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The system was announced in early July 2026 and no timeline was given ([cable](https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/seacom-plans-25000km-subsea-cable-from-france-to-singapore-and-south-africa/)).<br><br>A landlocked country's exposure is backhaul, so this row moves only when the terrestrial leg is contracted. The cross-border terrestrial route recorded elsewhere in this report is the relevant instrument, and its live status rests on a vendor announcement.</details> | Advanced |
| Sao Tome and Principe | A [submarine cable to the second island](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099102025185177863/pdf/P177158-d0ebd79d-6646-4e92-b9ae-1183b632c15a.pdf) is listed as an active project objective, and no source states its build status or a completion date. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It is monitored as an objective of the lender's digital project ([cable](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099102025185177863/pdf/P177158-d0ebd79d-6646-4e92-b9ae-1183b632c15a.pdf)).<br><br>No build status or completion date is held. Connecting the smaller island is the single most consequential connectivity item for this country, and the base can say only that it remains an objective.</details> | No change |
| Senegal | A [subsea cable landing station was commissioned](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/23/sonatel-commissions-2africa-submarine-cable-africas-largest-connectivity-project/) in December 2025, and a [second cable landing](https://techcabal.com/2026/05/23/orange-backed-subsea-cable-to-connect-nigeria-19-other-countries/) was announced in May 2026 with no landing-site, financing or contract detail. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The landing station was commissioned in December 2025; the base holds a month-precision event date and no day ([station](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/23/sonatel-commissions-2africa-submarine-cable-africas-largest-connectivity-project/)).<br><br>The second cable was announced at a continental summit in May 2026 and no landing-site, financing or contract detail is held for the country's leg ([cable](https://techcabal.com/2026/05/23/orange-backed-subsea-cable-to-connect-nigeria-19-other-countries/)).<br><br>An internet exchange point reactivation was announced in a state and operator partnership in March 2025 and is the only source in the base naming the exchange ([exchange](https://www.wearetech.africa/en/fils-uk/news/public-management/senum-sonatel-announce-partnership-to-enhance-digital-services-and-connectivity-in-senegal)).</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | A wholesale operator [added new subsea capacity to its West Africa network](https://www.mobileeurope.co.uk/csquared-boosts-internet-resilence-integrating-with-2africa-west/) in July 2026, and an [ECOWAS four-country cable cleared feasibility](https://www.ecowas.int/liberia-hosts-workshop-to-validate-ecowass-second-submarine-cable/) in October 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The added capacity serves a wholesale route across several West African states and is not a national asset ([capacity](https://www.mobileeurope.co.uk/csquared-boosts-internet-resilence-integrating-with-2africa-west/)).<br><br>2025-07-11 - a ministry delegation [assessed feasibility and public-private models for a second landing routed via Guinea](https://techafricanews.com/2025/07/11/sierra-leone-explores-second-submarine-cable-landing-to-boost-connectivity/), and nothing further on that route is held.<br><br>2025-10-22 - separately, ECOWAS member-state experts with their technical and financial partners [reviewed and approved the feasibility study for a second submarine cable serving Cabo Verde, Liberia, Sierra Leone and Guinea-Bissau](https://www.ecowas.int/liberia-hosts-workshop-to-validate-ecowass-second-submarine-cable/) at a Monrovia workshop; no route, cost, landing site or construction date is published, and neither record says whether the two are the same project.<br><br>A single landing station is the most concentrated infrastructure risk in this ledger, and until one of these is built it stays that way.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | A [first submarine cable landing technical working group](https://www.hiiraan.com/news4/2026/Jun/205487/somalia_ifc_opens_first_technical_working_group_on_submarine_cable_infrastructure.aspx) was opened with an international financier in June 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The working group was opened in June 2026 to develop the landing and its regulatory framework ([group](https://www.hiiraan.com/news4/2026/Jun/205487/somalia_ifc_opens_first_technical_working_group_on_submarine_cable_infrastructure.aspx)).<br><br>That the regulatory framework is being developed alongside the first landing rather than after it is the point worth recording: the country is setting the rules for cable landings while it still has the option to.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | A new subsea route [opened for business in December 2025 with two South African landings](https://bayobab.africa/2africa-west-seamless-connectivity-at-scale/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-12-04 — [the 2Africa West route opened for business with landings at Yzerfontein and Duynefontein](https://bayobab.africa/2africa-west-seamless-connectivity-at-scale/), an in-window addition to international capacity where none had been recorded.<br><br>No capacity, price or utilisation figure is published for either landing, so the base can state that capacity was added and nothing about how much, at what cost, or to whom it is sold.<br><br>That is the standing weakness of this indicator for the country: cable landings are announced and the wholesale market they feed is not measured anywhere on this ledger.<br><br>A second route was launched in September 2026: a €93.1 million cable from Saint-Paul on Réunion to Durban, 16 fibre pairs at 20 Tbps each, to be built within three years and framed as route diversity ahead of an older cable's retirement from 2027 ([launch](https://la1ere.franceinfo.fr/reunion/reunion-bientot-un-nouveau-cable-sous-marin-entre-la-reunion-et-l-afrique-du-sud-1734668.html)). It is [French-side led and about 79 per cent publicly funded](https://la1ere.franceinfo.fr/reunion/reunion-bientot-un-nouveau-cable-sous-marin-entre-la-reunion-et-l-afrique-du-sud-1734668.html), so the South African end is a landing rather than an investment.</details> | Advanced |
| South Sudan | The [international gateway is operated by a Swiss company](https://www.eyeradio.org/nca-establishes-gateway-and-data-center-oversight-committee) which also holds the national data centre, under an oversight committee constituted with it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>One privately held foreign company holds both the country's international gateway and its national data centre ([gateway](https://www.eyeradio.org/nca-establishes-gateway-and-data-center-oversight-committee)).<br><br>No concession terms, duration, revenue share or performance condition is published for either. That concentration — international transit and national hosting in the same private hands — is stated in the record and nowhere explained.</details> | No change |
| Sudan | A [tripartite binding agreement](https://www.connectingafrica.com/partnerships/east-african-telcos-ink-cross-border-fiber-network-deal) was signed in February 2026 converting a 2024 memorandum into a three-country fibre corridor, and no source states a build or completion date. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The agreement was signed in Djibouti between the three states' operators, converting the December 2024 memorandum into a binding instrument ([agreement](https://www.connectingafrica.com/partnerships/east-african-telcos-ink-cross-border-fiber-network-deal), [deal](https://techafricanews.com/2026/02/04/ethio-telecom-djibouti-telecom-and-sudatel-seal-horizon-fiber-cross-border-connectivity-deal/), [corridor](https://itweb.africa/article/east-african-telcos-seal-cross-border-fibre-corridor-deal/xA9PO7NE16Yvo4J8)).<br><br>No physical build or completion date is stated in any source held ([review](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/reports-and-coverage/28249-ethio-telecom-djibouti-telecom-sudatel-seal-binding-deal-on-cross-border-fiber-network/), [operators](https://www.lanation.dj/djibouti-telecom-ethio-telecomet-sudatel-telecom-group-signent-un-accord-strategique-pour-linitiative-horizon-fiber/)).<br><br>For a country whose international capacity runs through neighbours, the corridor is the single most consequential infrastructure commitment in this ledger, and it is dated only by its signature.</details> | Advanced |
| Tanzania | A [submarine cable](https://dailynews.co.tz/tanzania-unveils-kilimanjaro-one-submarine-cable-system-to-connect-mauritius/) was announced in November 2025 with no construction start, contractor or cost confirmed since, and [cables and landing stations](https://dailynews.co.tz/communications-sector-surges-400pc/) are counted on two unreconciled bases. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The cable announcement has produced nothing further in the base ([cable](https://dailynews.co.tz/tanzania-unveils-kilimanjaro-one-submarine-cable-system-to-connect-mauritius/)).<br><br>Cables and landing stations are not the same unit and nothing in the base reconciles the two counts, one from the regulator to December 2025 and one from a ministerial statement at an international summit ([cables](https://dailynews.co.tz/communications-sector-surges-400pc/), [summit](https://www.mawasiliano.go.tz/news/tanzania-yaonesha-mafanikio-ya-utekelezaji-wa-akili-unde-ai-katika-mkutano-wa-kimataifa-wa-waico-2026)).</details> | Advanced |
| Togo | [Capacity on a second submarine system](https://www.mobileeurope.co.uk/csquared-boosts-internet-resilence-integrating-with-2africa-west/) was added for route diversity, on a pan-regional account with no country-specific figure. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The country is one of eleven places tagged in the account, and no national capacity, price or route figure is published ([capacity](https://www.mobileeurope.co.uk/csquared-boosts-internet-resilence-integrating-with-2africa-west/), [fibre pair](https://www.ecofinagency.com/telecom/3003-43499-), [ownership](https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/full/10.1080/01972243.2025.2547676)).<br><br>Route diversity on the subsea leg is real and unquantified here. It is the only international-capacity item in the ledger.</details> | Advanced |
| Tunisia | A [submarine cable landing became ready for service](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/26/orange-tunisia-launches-medusa-submarine-cable-to-boost-international-connectivity/) in June 2026, with the state operator holding a [dedicated fibre pair](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/medusa-s-viatunisa-subsea-cable-now-live) on it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The landing was ready for service on 3 June 2026. Both national operators' publicity claimed the landing; it is settled against the cable operator's own newsroom, which makes one the owner and the other a capacity customer ([operational](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/26/orange-tunisia-launches-medusa-submarine-cable-to-boost-international-connectivity/), [live](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/medusa-s-viatunisa-subsea-cable-now-live), [landing](https://medusascs.com/news/medusa-submarine-cable-system-lands-in-bizerte/), [hosting](https://medusascs.com/news/orange-tunisia-signs-an-agreement-with-medusa-submarine-cable-system-to-host-the-new-submarine-cable-in-bizerte/)).<br><br>The state operator's dedicated pair entered service with the cable; the cable operator's record gives it as 20 Tbps and trade press and a national re-report give 22 ([pair](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/medusa-s-viatunisa-subsea-cable-now-live), [joining](https://medusascs.com/news/tunisie_telecom_joins_medusa/)).<br><br>Two operators claiming one landing, and two capacity figures for one fibre pair, are both settled here against the operator of the cable.</details> | Advanced |
| Uganda | A [2,000 km protected route to the coast](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fiber-networking/paratus-lights-up-new-east-africa-fiber-route) was switched on in February 2026, and a [second route to the same coast](https://seacom.co.ke/news/seacom-deploys-a-new-high-capacity-nairobikampala-route-to-strengthen-east-africas-digital-backbone) launched with 1 Tbps activated. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The first route was switched on in February 2026 ([route](https://www.connectingafrica.com/fiber-networking/paratus-lights-up-new-east-africa-fiber-route), [highway](https://itweb.africa/article/paratus-lights-up-new-east-africa-fibre-highway-linking-goma-to-mombasa/wbrpOqg2aJQMDLZn)).<br><br>The second launched in June 2026 with 1 Tbps activated, and is absent from the compiled intersection page ([second](https://seacom.co.ke/news/seacom-deploys-a-new-high-capacity-nairobikampala-route-to-strengthen-east-africas-digital-backbone)).<br><br>Two protected routes to the coast inside five months is the most substantive infrastructure gain in this unit, and both are commercial builds with no state instrument attached.</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | A [cross-border fibre interconnector](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/zambia-and-mozambique-launch-fibre-optic-interconnector/) was commissioned in February 2026 with no capacity, cost or funder stated, and a [terrestrial cable segment](https://developingtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/data-centres-networks/19091-google-plans-to-build-four-subsea-cable-connectivity-hubs-for-africa.html) is stated ready for service in 2027. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The interconnector was commissioned in February 2026 ([interconnector](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/zambia-and-mozambique-launch-fibre-optic-interconnector/), [appraisal](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099012025023033676/txt/P505094-630a5ef9-d23a-4221-b7c8-397c94a90b4d.txt)).<br><br>The cable segment's 2027 date was first stated inside the window; the position itself did not move ([segment](https://developingtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/data-centres-networks/19091-google-plans-to-build-four-subsea-cable-connectivity-hubs-for-africa.html), [investment](https://www.mobileeurope.co.uk/google-invests-in-terrestrial-subsea-cable-linking-kenya-to-australia/)).<br><br>For a landlocked country these two routes are the whole of the international capacity record, and neither carries a capacity figure.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | [Equipped international bandwidth](https://technomag.co.zw/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Q4-2025-Abridged-Sector-performance-report-HMed-final-1.pdf) rose on the regulator's fourth-quarter 2025 report, and the apparent collapse in headline used bandwidth is a [reclassification](https://www.newsday.co.zw/theindependent/business-digest/article/200055624/measured-but-not-counted-how-starlink-broke-potrazs-bandwidth-framework) rather than a fall in traffic. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The measure excludes the satellite operator from the second quarter of 2025 ([bandwidth](https://technomag.co.zw/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Q4-2025-Abridged-Sector-performance-report-HMed-final-1.pdf)).<br><br>The satellite operator was excluded and the baseline restated from that quarter, so the apparent collapse in headline used bandwidth is a reclassification and not a fall in traffic ([framework](https://www.newsday.co.zw/theindependent/business-digest/article/200055624/measured-but-not-counted-how-starlink-broke-potrazs-bandwidth-framework), [report](https://technomag.co.zw/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Q4-2025-Abridged-Sector-performance-report-HMed-final-1.pdf)).<br><br>Naming a measurement change as a measurement change is what stops a series break being read as a collapse. A terrestrial segment of a trans-continental cable, built by a domestically owned group, is stated ready for service in 2027 ([segment](https://developingtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/data-centres-networks/19091-google-plans-to-build-four-subsea-cable-connectivity-hubs-for-africa.html)).</details> | Advanced |

## Internet Exchange Points

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | An independent measurement puts [local content at 13% of national traffic, most of it served from European servers](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/blog/2026/06/beyond-the-homepage-measuring-what-it-really-takes-to-keep-web-traffic-local/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The position is unchanged at both ends: an [independent measurement puts local content at 13% of national traffic in June 2026, most of it served from European servers, with no national exchange point or local-hosting policy held](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/blog/2026/06/beyond-the-homepage-measuring-what-it-really-takes-to-keep-web-traffic-local/).<br><br>It is the only traffic-localisation measure on this ledger and it is a third party's. A country with mandatory in-country data localisation, a new national data centre and a sovereign cloud has no published exchange point, and most of what its users reach is served from another continent.</details> | No change |
| Benin | [Benin IX is operating and tracked in the Internet Society's register](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/ixp-tracker/ixp/395/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Benin IX appears in the Internet Society's IXP tracker](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/ixp-tracker/ixp/395/). [The society's Benin chapter published a study in June 2023 on what its redynamisation, modernisation and long-term viability would require](https://isoc.bj/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/beninix.pdf), which is the only assessment of the exchange on the record.<br><br>No peering-member count, traffic figure or local-traffic share is held.</details> | No change |
| Botswana | [Digital Delta IX is registered and operating in Gaborone with one peer and 10G of capacity](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/5002), run by the state wholesale operator. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-04-21 — [the exchange’s registry entry records one peer, one connection and 10G of total capacity](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/5002), the only public description of its size.<br><br>2025-11-25 — [the Tier III data centre behind the exchange was launched](https://dailynews.gov.bw/news-detail/89235).<br><br>2026-02-18 — [Namibia’s MTC and BoFiNet agreed cross-border interconnection, IP transit and wholesale capacity exchange through the Buitepos-Ngoma corridor](https://thebrief.com.na/2026/02/mtc-bofinet-sign-agreement-to-strengthen-namibia-botswana-digital-connectivity/).<br><br>No traffic figure for the exchange is published.</details> | Advanced, an exchange with one peer |
| Burkina Faso | The exchange point [runs at a 6 Gbit/s peak with local latency of 15 to 20 milliseconds against over 300 before peering](https://www.internetsociety.org/blog/2026/05/local-infrastructure-lower-costs-how-peering-is-moving-the-needle-on-internet-affordability/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The exchange has operated since June 2015 at a 6 Gbit/s peak, keeping local traffic flowing through subsea cable cuts](https://www.internetsociety.org/blog/2025/04/local-businesses-benefit-from-burkina-fasos-internet-development), and 2026-05-25 — [local latency is reported at 15 to 20 milliseconds against over 300 before peering](https://www.internetsociety.org/blog/2026/05/local-infrastructure-lower-costs-how-peering-is-moving-the-needle-on-internet-affordability/).<br><br>The capacity figure has not moved and no update has been published since May 2026. For a landlocked country whose international transit runs through neighbours' coastlines, keeping domestic traffic domestic is the cheapest resilience available, and 6 Gbit/s is a small ceiling against the service estate this report describes.<br><br>No address-protocol migration plan exists to sit beside it: [operators and the regulator again called for a roadmap with a timetable in July 2026, with individual operators migrating on their own schedules](https://lefaso.net/spip.php?article147597).</details> | No change |
| Burundi | A national internet exchange point [was created by decree in May 2014](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/image0013.pdf), its first objective keeping local traffic off international links. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Decret n 100/122 du 13 mai 2014 creates the national internet exchange point as an exchange belonging to the Republic; article 2 sets its objectives, first among them keeping local traffic off international links, and article 3 places its management with an internet service provider association chosen and authorised by the regulator](https://arct.gov.bi/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/image0013.pdf).<br><br>The founding instrument is held and the operating record is not: no traffic figure, member count, uptime statement or annual report for the exchange was located, so the base can say the exchange exists in law and cannot say whether it carries anything.</details> | No change |
| Cameroon | CAMIX [opened a third point of presence at the ST Digital data centre in April 2026](https://www.camix.cm/actualite/3e-pop-camix). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[CAMIX opened a third point of presence at the ST Digital data centre in April 2026](https://www.camix.cm/actualite/3e-pop-camix). [Its Douala presence is tracked in the Internet Society's register](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/ixp-tracker/ixp/704/), and [the exchange's own site dates its current form to March 2026](https://www.camix.cm/).<br><br>No peering-member count, traffic figure or local-traffic share is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Central African Republic | A national internet exchange point [was created in January 2026](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f), known to the base only through a foreign operator's investor filing. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-01 — [an internet exchange point was created, described as reinforcing digital sovereignty](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f), where none existed. The date carries month precision and the sole source is an operator's half-year investor filing: no government instrument, membership list, peering agreement or traffic figure is held.<br><br>That an exchange point's creation reaches this base through a foreign shareholder's financial report rather than through the regulator is the sharpest illustration of the measurement problem in this country. The regulator publishes nothing, reported under quality standards, and its own quality-of-service site is financed and not live.</details> | Advanced |
| Chad | The [national internet exchange point](https://www.digitalbusiness.africa/tchad-ladetic-celebre-un-an-du-point-dechange-internet-tchadix-bilan-et-perspectives/) held its first-year general assembly and adopted statutes and internal rules. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The assembly was held in May 2026, institutionalising the exchange ([exchange](https://www.digitalbusiness.africa/tchad-ladetic-celebre-un-an-du-point-dechange-internet-tchadix-bilan-et-perspectives/)).<br><br>Its stated purpose is to stop traffic leaving the country and returning, and the cost and latency benefit is asserted and never quantified. No member count, traffic volume or peering figure is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Comoros | An [exchange point is installed at Moroni](https://www.comorescables.km/Technologies), letting the two operators exchange local traffic; the earlier programme-funded attempt failed. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regional communications programme that built the cable landings [tried and failed to establish an exchange point, for want of telecom-operator commitment, and its completion report of March 2023 records that as an unmet outcome](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099191503242311967/pdf/BOSIB096cafe3e0c0084f70822a76892f1a.pdf).<br><br>The state cable company's own site now [states that an exchange point has been installed at Moroni, enabling local traffic exchange between Comores Telecom and Yas Comores, alongside IP transit at Moroni and the 2Africa cable in service](https://www.comorescables.km/Technologies).<br><br>That page is undated and is the only record of it; no traffic figure, membership list, operating date or independent confirmation exists on file. What changed between 2023 and now, and who paid, is not stated anywhere.</details> | Advanced |
| Congo | [CGIX has served as the African Union's Central Africa sub-regional hub since August 2017](https://www.cgix.cg/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The Congo Internet eXchange Point was initiated by the regulator and launched in May 2013 with seven connected operators, and was selected by the African Union in August 2017 to serve as the Central Africa sub-regional hub; its own portal states 4 connected members, 1 point of presence and 70 Mb/s of traffic an hour](https://www.cgix.cg/).<br><br>The counters are the exchange's own and carry no date on the page, and the fall from seven operators at launch to four members is not explained anywhere in the base.</details> | No change |
| Cote d'Ivoire | The [national exchange point is operating with content-network peering at a 36 Gbps daily average](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2642599/economie/peeringday-2026appairage-et-interconnexion-lartci-mobilise-lecosysteme-numerique-autour-dun-internet-performant), over a decade in service. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The exchange has been operating for over a decade and was [reported peering with content networks alongside operators and internet service providers at an average daily 36 Gbps](https://www.fratmat.info/article/2642599/economie/peeringday-2026appairage-et-interconnexion-lartci-mobilise-lecosysteme-numerique-autour-dun-internet-performant).<br><br>A [memorandum was signed in June 2025 to colocate a third point of presence at a Grand-Bassam facility](https://techafricanews.com/2025/06/30/raxio-cote-divoire-and-artci-sign-mou-to-host-the-nations-third-internet-exchange-point/), and nothing on file confirms it was commissioned. No traffic series, member count or peering policy is published, so the single throughput figure is the whole of the evidence.</details> | No change |
| Djibouti | [Two exchange points operate](https://www.connectingafrica.com/data-centers/wingu-africa-secures-60m-to-fuel-east-african-expansion), a national one and an international operator's, both unchanged in the window. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [two exchanges are in operation, a national point and one run by an international exchange operator alongside it](https://www.connectingafrica.com/data-centers/wingu-africa-secures-60m-to-fuel-east-african-expansion), the second [launched with a carrier-neutral facility expansion](https://www.biometricupdate.com/202506/60m-to-speed-up-digital-connectivity-infrastructure-in-ethiopia-djibouti-tanzania).<br><br>Nothing moved in the window. No traffic figure, member count or peering policy is published for either, so what they carry is unknown - which is the same gap that sits under the country's whole transit position, where the landing count is published and the capacity is not.</details> | No change |
| DR Congo | One exchange [became distributed across two carrier-neutral facilities](https://itweb.africa/article/distributed-exchange-boosts-drc-connectivity/4r1ly7R9GkLvpmda) and another [moved into a Tier III facility](https://www.raxiogroup.com/raxio-drc-to-host-kinshasa-internet-exchange-kinix-strengthening-national-connectivity/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-05 - an exchange established in 2023 and operating from a single presence [added a second presence at a Kinshasa carrier-neutral facility, making it the country's first distributed exchange, open to licensed operators, service providers, mobile networks, cloud and content providers](https://itweb.africa/article/distributed-exchange-boosts-drc-connectivity/4r1ly7R9GkLvpmda).<br><br>2026-08-20 - a second exchange [relocated its infrastructure into a carrier-neutral Tier III data centre, the operators stating it connects more than 20 networks with over 1 Tbps of available capacity and expecting more than 2 Tbps after the move, as one of three points under a national exchange project managed by the internet service providers association](https://www.raxiogroup.com/raxio-drc-to-host-kinshasa-internet-exchange-kinix-strengthening-national-connectivity/). Capacity and network counts are the operators' own and no traffic measurement is held for either.</details> | Advanced |
| Egypt | [15% of popular web content is served from inside the country, the majority from European servers](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/blog/2026/06/beyond-the-homepage-measuring-what-it-really-takes-to-keep-web-traffic-local/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>A byte-weighted measurement puts [15% of popular web content served locally, the majority from European servers, against 13% for Algeria, 66% for South Africa and 83% for the United States](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/blog/2026/06/beyond-the-homepage-measuring-what-it-really-takes-to-keep-web-traffic-local/). No prior reading is held.<br><br>The finding sits against the cable-hub and data-centre framing: the country carries other people's transit and hosts little of its own users' content. The one edge presence on file is a [content-delivery edge location in Cairo in operation since May 2024, with a vendor-stated latency reduction of up to about 30%, and no capacity expansion or additional presence recorded](https://aws.amazon.com/about-aws/whats-new/2024/05/new-edge-location-egypt) - an edge cache, not a cloud region.</details> | No change |
| Equatorial Guinea | The exchange point [reached construction stage in 2017](https://map.au-pida.org/projects/show/8290001) and no launch has been recorded since. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The continental infrastructure registry's [entry](https://map.au-pida.org/projects/show/8290001) is the only record the base holds: construction stage in 2017, no launch, no operator, no traffic figure.<br><br>Nine years on nothing records it operating, so domestic traffic between Equatoguinean networks has no published local exchange path.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | A [national internet exchange point](https://au.int/en/newsevents/20140407/launching-internet-exchange-point-swaziland) was launched in April 2014 under the African Union's exchange-point project; nothing later is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The African Union Commission launched the exchange point with the ICT ministry and the Internet Society on 10 April 2014, to keep intra-country traffic inside the country rather than routing it abroad ([launch](https://au.int/en/newsevents/20140407/launching-internet-exchange-point-swaziland)).<br><br>Twelve years on the base holds no peering-member count, no traffic figure, and no statement that it is operating.<br><br>The one adjacent fact the base does hold points the other way: the regulator had to order the national operator in June 2026 to share its backbone and fibre topology with internet service providers, which is the interconnection problem an exchange point exists to solve at the traffic layer.</details> | No change, launched in 2014 with nothing published since |
| Ethiopia | The country's only internet exchange carries three members and 21 Gbps of cumulative port capacity ([tracker](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/ixp-tracker/ixp/1339/)). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Measurement from self-reported peering data, last updated in January 2026, records the Addis Ababa Internet Exchange with three members, 21 Gbps of cumulative port speeds, all three members peering at the route server and two of the three using route origin validation ([tracker](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/ixp-tracker/ixp/1339/)).<br><br>The exchange, the country's first, had by September 2025 been operating for over a year at a Tier III data centre in the technology park ([update](https://www.wingu.africa/latest-news/transforming-connectivity-ethiopias-first-internet-exchange-hosted-at-wingu-africa)).<br><br>The base holds no earlier capacity or membership figure, so the level is stated and the direction is not. It holds no second exchange and none outside the capital.</details> | No change, a first measurement of the sole exchange with no earlier figure behind it |
| Gabon | The exchange point [passed 250 Mbps of local traffic](https://www.gabonreview.com/souverainete-numerique-le-gabon-relance-gabix-son-point-dechange-internet-local/) after the two mobile operators reconnected, from about 80 Mbps and two members. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The two operators reconnected on 13 May 2026 and local traffic passed 250 Mbps, against about 80 Mbps unchanged since 2019 on two members ([relaunch](https://www.gabonreview.com/souverainete-numerique-le-gabon-relance-gabix-son-point-dechange-internet-local/)).<br><br>A national reboot workshop ran from 3 to 8 August 2026 with an international internet body and the regional registry, closing with certificates after six days of peering, security and sovereignty training ([reboot workshop](https://agpgabon.ga/numerique-latelier-gabix-reboot-propulse-linterconnexion-locale-au-gabon/), [workshop programme](https://agpgabon.ga/gabon-lancement-de-latelier-national-gabix-reboot/)).<br><br>The registry called for a network of trained ambassadors to carry the skills into firms and administrations, and operator representatives named broader institutional buy-in as the remaining task; the post-relaunch member count has not been published.</details> | Advanced |
| Gambia | The [Serekunda exchange](http://www.sixp.gm/about.html), founded in 2013 by fourteen members, publishes no current traffic figures. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The exchange's [own page](http://www.sixp.gm/about.html) records its founding on 25 July 2013 by fourteen members and shows no current traffic or membership data.<br><br>It is the country's only exchange point, and nothing in the base since 2013 states how much traffic it keeps local or which providers peer there.</details> | No change |
| Ghana | The Accra exchange is [past 200 Gbps](https://tech.africa/accra-three-internet-exchanges/), but only 4 of 39 connected networks peer at all three exchanges, so most local traffic still transits abroad. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The fragmentation is the finding rather than the traffic figure: an exchange's value is the traffic it keeps local, and three partly-overlapping exchanges keep less of it than one would ([exchange traffic](https://tech.africa/accra-three-internet-exchanges/)).<br><br>No membership list, peering policy or retained-traffic percentage is published for any of the three, so the base can see the overlap and not what it costs.</details> | Mixed, the main exchange grew while three exchanges keep splitting the traffic between them |
| Guinea-Bissau | No exchange point is recorded; the [national fibre backbone](https://wardip.gw/guine-bissau-lanca-concurso-internacional-para-rede-nacional-de-fibra-optica/) it would sit on is still at tender. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Nothing in the base records an internet exchange point in Guinea-Bissau, operating or planned.<br><br>The domestic network that would justify one is not built: the [national fibre backbone tender was relaunched in March 2026 after two prior rounds were cancelled](https://wardip.gw/guine-bissau-lanca-concurso-internacional-para-rede-nacional-de-fibra-optica/), and the [procurement plan records that history](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099031726185534997/txt/P176932-ff007e3d-6dc7-4a7e-b858-14a297e1be23.txt).</details> | No change |
| Kenya | A [foreign-operated exchange](https://itweb.africa/article/linx-expands-kenya-peering-network/5yONP7ErXpRMXWrb) runs in Nairobi alongside the [association-run exchange](https://www.pch.net/ixp/details/143) active there since February 2001. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Connected networks include a major video platform and a satellite operator ([exchange](https://itweb.africa/article/linx-expands-kenya-peering-network/5yONP7ErXpRMXWrb)).<br><br>The baseline is inferred from the stated twentyfold growth rather than separately published, so the direction is firmer than the level.<br><br>The country's own exchange, managed by the technology service providers' association, has been [active in Nairobi since February 2001](https://www.pch.net/ixp/details/143) on Ethernet with two subnets. The third-party registry record carries no participant count, traffic figure or facility list, so its size is not established and the base cannot compare the two exchanges.</details> | Advanced |
| Lesotho | The [exchange was relaunched after a revamp in February 2026](https://www.thereporter.co.ls/2026/02/02/lesotho-revamps-internet-exchange-infrastructure/), with a [refreshed registry record in May](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/5015). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2011 — [the regulator’s annual report covers the exchange’s founding period and the regional cable completion](https://lca.org.ls/wp-content/uploads/filr/2326/lca_annual_report_2010-11.pdf).<br><br>2026-02-02 — [the Internet Society chapter relaunched the revamped exchange](https://www.thereporter.co.ls/2026/02/02/lesotho-revamps-internet-exchange-infrastructure/).<br><br>2026-05-13 — [a refreshed registry record gives a later externally visible snapshot](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/5015).<br><br>No peer count, traffic figure or capacity for the exchange is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | The [exchange launched in 2015](https://au.int/en/newsevents/20150802/setting-and-launching-internet-exchange-point-liberia) is functional but [still under-peered](https://lta.gov.lr/lta-highlights-need-to-strengthen-liberias-internet-exchange-point-atnational-peering-forum/) as at December 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The Liberia Internet Exchange Point was [launched in Monrovia on 6 August 2015](https://au.int/en/newsevents/20150802/setting-and-launching-internet-exchange-point-liberia) by the African Union Commission with the ministry under the African Internet Exchange System project, which supplied equipment, technical support and capacity building. No separate licence or establishing regulation was located.<br><br>At the [national peering forum in December 2025](https://lta.gov.lr/lta-highlights-need-to-strengthen-liberias-internet-exchange-point-atnational-peering-forum/) the regulator's commissioner said the exchange remains functional but suffers governance problems and limited provider participation, with many providers still not peering, against upgraded equipment and a newly elected association leadership.</details> | No change |
| Libya | Libya's [only registered exchange point shows zero peers connected](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/4450). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [registry record](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/4450) for the Tripoli exchange lists no connected peers, so nothing measurable is exchanged there and domestic traffic between Libyan networks has no published local path.<br><br>Nothing in the base since records a peering agreement, a member count or a traffic figure for it.</details> | No change |
