| Madagascar | The country's [one exchange point is restarting](https://www.isocfoundation.org/2025/01/meet-the-new-grantees-improving-peering-and-interconnection-around-the-globe/) after a 2019 suspension, on a small operating footprint. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A funder announcement of January 2025 named the national research network as a peering infrastructure grantee to restart the exchange after its suspension in 2019 ([grant](https://www.isocfoundation.org/2025/01/meet-the-new-grantees-improving-peering-and-interconnection-around-the-globe/)).<br><br>A tracker page updated in February 2026 shows one active exchange with four members and under 1 per cent of domestic networks reachable through it ([tracker](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/ixp-tracker/country/MG/)).<br><br>The restart is real and the footprint is small: the figures evidence an exchange in operation rather than one most domestic networks reach.</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | The Lilongwe exchange [took a peering infrastructure grant in February 2026](https://www.isocfoundation.org/2026/02/introducing-the-2025-sustainable-peering-infrastructure-grantee/) after [standing up a new switching fabric](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/lionexmw_the-first-part-of-our-new-leaf-spine-evpn-vxlan-activity-7408382789039071232-bP85). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The older exchange has run since December 2008, operated by the internet service providers' association, and [migrated its peering to the national data centre as of 14 November 2024](http://www.mispa.org.mw/mix.html). No traffic figure, member count or port speed is published for it, at any point in eighteen years.<br><br>2025-12 - the Lilongwe exchange [stood up the first phase of a new leaf-spine switching fabric with a content delivery network installed at its facility](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/lionexmw_the-first-part-of-our-new-leaf-spine-evpn-vxlan-activity-7408382789039071232-bP85).<br><br>2026-02 - it was [awarded a sustainable peering infrastructure grant to raise port speeds and take additional members](https://www.isocfoundation.org/2026/02/introducing-the-2025-sustainable-peering-infrastructure-grantee/). Two exchanges and no published measure of what either carries.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | The [exchange launched in 2015](https://au.int/en/pressreleases/30806/au-launches-internet-exchange-point-mauritius-keeping-intracountry-internet) runs public route servers on both address families; its size is known only from a third-party tracker. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It was launched in August 2015 with African Union support, to keep intra-country traffic in country ([launch](https://au.int/en/pressreleases/30806/au-launches-internet-exchange-point-mauritius-keeping-intracountry-internet)).<br><br>Its route servers and looking glass are publicly reachable on both address families ([looking glass](https://portal.mixp.org/index.php/lg/rs1-mixp-ipv6)).<br><br>The only account of its size is an external tracker's snapshot ([tracker](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/ixp-tracker/ixp/516/)). The exchange publishes its infrastructure and not its traffic: no peer count, throughput figure or domestic-traffic share comes from the exchange itself.</details> | No change |
| Morocco | The two exchanges carry [210 Gbps](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/fr/ixp-tracker/ixp/849/) and [30 Gbps](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/fr/ixp-tracker/ixp/760/) across three members each, with net contraction over twelve months. <details><summary>Full record</summary>A [turnkey contract to build a national internet exchange point](https://anrt.ma/a-propos/communiques/mise-en-place-dun-point-dechange-internet) was documented by the regulator in December 2023.<br><br>What the trackers show is small and shrinking: the [larger exchange at 210 Gbps across three members](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/fr/ixp-tracker/ixp/849/) and the [Casablanca exchange at 30 Gbps across three, with net contraction over the trailing twelve months](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/fr/ixp-tracker/ixp/760/). Three members is not a peering fabric for a market this size.</details> | Regressed |
| Mozambique | The [national internet exchange](https://mozix.org.mz/) carries 143 Gbps of cumulative member port capacity across eighteen peers, and opened a [second point of presence](https://capacityglobal.com/news/mozambique-internet-exchange-launches-new-pop-at-digital-realtys-maputo-data-centre/) in a carrier-neutral Maputo facility in May 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The exchange is operated by the national university's computer centre in Maputo, founded in May 2002, with eighteen peers on its own site and 143 Gbps of cumulative member port capacity ([exchange](https://mozix.org.mz/)).<br><br>A second point of presence opened at a carrier-neutral Maputo data centre hosting more than fifteen network operators in May 2025, when the exchange reported peak traffic above 150 Gbps across nineteen peering members and about 35 per cent of the country's internet traffic routed through domestic exchanges ([point of presence](https://capacityglobal.com/news/mozambique-internet-exchange-launches-new-pop-at-digital-realtys-maputo-data-centre/)).<br><br>Member port capacity is an upper bound on what could be exchanged at one time and not measured traffic; no autonomous systems joined or left over the tracked period and the exchange's own peering database record has been stale since 2016 ([tracker](https://pulse.internetsociety.org/en/ixp-tracker/ixp/260/)). The peer count falls between the 2025 and 2026 accounts and neither explains it.</details> | Advanced |
| Niger | A [Niamey internet exchange point](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/2108-130856-le-niger-se-dote-d-un-nouveau-point-d-echange-internet) was inaugurated in August 2025, letting domestic networks exchange traffic locally. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The exchange entered service on 19 August 2025 ([exchange](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/2108-130856-le-niger-se-dote-d-un-nouveau-point-d-echange-internet)).<br><br>No peer count, port capacity or traffic figure is published, so the base records that domestic interconnection now exists and not how much passes through it. It sits beside a national data centre accepted three months later, which is the other half of keeping traffic and data in the country.</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | The [national internet exchange passed 1 Tbps](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/24/ixpn-hits-1tbps-traffic-milestone-boosting-nigerias-digital-growth/) of traffic in April 2025, and [close to twelve licence-exempt optical links](https://datacentresafrica.com/taara-and-liquid-use-light-beam-technology-to-expand-data-centre-capacity-to-large-enterprises-in-lagos-nigeria/) now carry urban transport across Lagos. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The exchange passed a 1 Tbps traffic milestone in April 2025 ([exchange](https://techafricanews.com/2025/04/24/ixpn-hits-1tbps-traffic-milestone-boosting-nigerias-digital-growth/)).<br><br>Two companies say they deployed close to twelve wireless optical links across Lagos over two years, distributing bandwidth from points of presence to banks, internet providers, hotels and a utility, rated at up to 20 Gbps over up to 20 km, with three further cities named next; the links need no trenching, civil works or spectrum licence, so a high-capacity urban transport layer sits outside the spectrum instruments the regulator uses to shape the market ([links](https://datacentresafrica.com/taara-and-liquid-use-light-beam-technology-to-expand-data-centre-capacity-to-large-enterprises-in-lagos-nigeria/), [deployment](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/05/taara-and-liquid-intelligent-technologies-expand-high-speed-wireless-connectivity-across-nigeria/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Rwanda | The exchange took a [new cloud member](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/gewape-cloud-group_gewapecloud-rinex-rwandacloud-activity-7468194906369593344-ao9G) in June 2026, and Rwanda was [selected to host the continental peering forum](https://www.afpif.org/2025/11/rwanda-to-host-the-2026-african-peering-and-interconnection-forum-afpif/) in August 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The exchange operates under terms tying its management to a 2014 memorandum between the regulator and the country-code registry ([terms](https://www.rinex.org.rw/docs/RINEX%20TERMS%20%20CONDITIONS.pdf)).<br><br>Rwanda was selected to host the continent's principal peering and interconnection forum in August 2026, putting the exchange and Kigali at the centre of continental interconnection discussion ([announcement](https://www.afpif.org/2025/11/rwanda-to-host-the-2026-african-peering-and-interconnection-forum-afpif/)).<br><br>A cloud infrastructure operator announced its own connection in June 2026 ([announcement](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/gewape-cloud-group_gewapecloud-rinex-rwandacloud-activity-7468194906369593344-ao9G)). No member count, traffic figure or peering ratio is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | The country's [only registered exchange point](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/2410) records zero peers and zero connections, on an entry last updated in September 2019. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The registry entry lists the exchange in Mogadishu under the communications regulator, with IPv4 and IPv6 address blocks allocated, and states zero peers, zero connections, zero open peers and no IPv6 participation ([record](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/2410)).<br><br>The record's own last-updated stamp is September 2019, which is both the most current evidence available and a fact about how little has been reported since.<br><br>Regional exchange-point support is inside the scope of the digital integration project's grant agreement ([agreement](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099062123164542123/pdf/P17618108dc2460600945a0f83353acb3fb.pdf)); nothing reports it reaching this exchange.</details> | No change |
| South Africa | The Cape Town exchange [went live at two further data-centre sites in August 2026](https://tech.africa/cinx-oadc-cape-town-expansion/), reaching six locations while one facility still holds most of its peering. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-08-03 — the Cape Town internet exchange [went live at two further data-centre sites, taking it to six locations](https://tech.africa/cinx-oadc-cape-town-expansion/). More sites is the movement; concentration is what the same record shows underneath it. [One facility holds 87 of the exchange's 122 networks and nearly every 100 Gbps port, against 208 networks and 10,132 Gbps on the more distributed Johannesburg exchange](https://tech.africa/cinx-oadc-cape-town-expansion/).<br><br>Distribution across sites is not the same thing as resilience while a single building carries most of the peering, and the base holds no traffic, outage or failover figure for either exchange to test it against.</details> | Advanced |
| Sudan | The single listed exchange point [has not been updated since January 2020](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/2320); cooperation on exchange points was [discussed in July 2026](https://www.sudanakhbar.com/1810092) without a commitment. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The registry entry for Sudan's sole listed exchange point, operated by the National Information Center, records four peers and a 4G aggregate capacity, last updated on 22 January 2020 ([entry](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/2320)). It is self-reported and shows no membership change since.<br><br>At the WSIS Forum in July 2026 the minister discussed strengthening exchange points, IPv6 expansion and internet-resource development with the regional registry and the Internet Society ([meeting](https://www.sudanakhbar.com/1810092)).<br><br>That is the only movement found, and it is a conversation rather than a peering.</details> | No change |
| Zambia | The [Lusaka exchange carries 12 peers on 76G](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/615), and a [regional DNS deployment went live on it](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/afrinic_great-news-from-lusaka-our-deployment-at-activity-7403752672203509763-lW7D). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The exchange's peering record lists 12 peers over 12 connections, 10 of them open, on 76G of total capacity with full support for the newer addressing protocol](https://www.peeringdb.com/ix/615).<br><br>2025-12 — [a regional internet registry's DNS deployment went live at the exchange, its own account putting the reduction in query latency at roughly 93 per cent](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/afrinic_great-news-from-lusaka-our-deployment-at-activity-7403752672203509763-lW7D). Local DNS resolution is the least visible and most consequential thing an exchange does: it keeps lookups from crossing an ocean before a page begins to load, and the figure is the deploying body's own rather than an independent measurement.<br><br>Traffic statistics for the exchange itself remain linked rather than published.</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | A [national internet exchange point](https://www.itu.int/en/ITU-D/Regional-Presence/Africa/Documents/2020/contributions/RI2_POTRAZ-Zimbabwe-IXP_RDFAFR2020.pdf) was reported to an international development forum in October 2020, and no traffic, peering or membership figure later than that is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The report was made in October 2020 ([exchange](https://www.itu.int/en/ITU-D/Regional-Presence/Africa/Documents/2020/contributions/RI2_POTRAZ-Zimbabwe-IXP_RDFAFR2020.pdf)).<br><br>Six years without a traffic, peering or membership figure is the record. It matters against the international-bandwidth series, because domestic peering is what would keep local traffic off that link.</details> | No change |

## Satellite broadband licensing and availability

| Country | Developments | Progress |
|---|---|---|
| Algeria | The regulator [opened a tender for two satellite-internet licences in April 2026](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D8%B2%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%B1-%D8%AA%D9%81%D8%AA%D8%AD-%D8%B1%D8%B3%D9%85%D9%8A%D8%A7-%D9%85%D8%AC%D8%A7%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D9%8F%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%81%D8%B3%D8%A9-%D9%81%D9%8A-%D8%A7); no award is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>There was no licensing route for non-geostationary satellite service at the window's opening. 2026-04-09 - the regulator [opened a tender for two licences](https://www.echoroukonline.com/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AC%D8%B2%D8%A7%D8%A6%D8%B1-%D8%AA%D9%81%D8%AA%D8%AD-%D8%B1%D8%B3%D9%85%D9%8A%D8%A7-%D9%85%D8%AC%D8%A7%D9%84-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%85%D9%8F%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%81%D8%B3%D8%A9-%D9%81%D9%8A-%D8%A7), [reported as opening the satellite communications market](https://techreviewafrica.com/news/4840/algeria-opens-satellite-communications-market-to-boost-connectivity-in-remote-areas).<br><br>No award is held as at 2026-08-03, four months on. Nothing on file states the licence terms, the spectrum on offer, the coverage obligation or the closing date, so what the two licences would oblige a winner to do is unknown.</details> | Advanced |
| Angola | A low-orbit licence [reached a multi-year capacity agreement](https://angop.ao/noticias/economia/mercury-reforca-oferta-de-conectividade-em-angola-com-novo-acordo-com-a-eutelsat/) and [a first reseller was licensed on the national satellite](https://angop.ao/noticias/tecnologia/startup-do-moxico-recebe-licenca-para-fornecer-internet-via-angosat-2/), while [one application is unresolved](https://www.novojornal.co.ao/opiniao/detalhe/starlink-em-angola-conectividade-controlo-e-a-politica-silenciosa-do-ceu-73683.html). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-06-12 — the country's [first and only licensed low-orbit operator, granted a 15-year licence in June 2025 with a Luanda ground station](https://techafricanews.com/2025/06/30/eutelsat-group-secures-15-year-license-to-operate-oneweb-leo-connectivity-in-angola/), [moved from licence to commercial deployment through a multi-year capacity agreement with a local provider](https://angop.ao/noticias/economia/mercury-reforca-oferta-de-conectividade-em-angola-com-novo-acordo-com-a-eutelsat/).<br><br>2026-07-02 — on the national satellite, [a commercial access hub opened in December 2025 with 37 firms in the integration process by April 2026, and a first startup was licensed to resell access](https://angop.ao/noticias/tecnologia/startup-do-moxico-recebe-licenca-para-fornecer-internet-via-angosat-2/). Bandwidth, pricing and operator access terms remain undisclosed.<br><br>2026-08-03 — the largest low-orbit operator [is still unlicensed, with a secretary of state saying only that the process is under way and the regulator still working out how to fit low-orbit operators into a regime written for terrestrial networks](https://www.novojornal.co.ao/opiniao/detalhe/starlink-em-angola-conectividade-controlo-e-a-politica-silenciosa-do-ceu-73683.html). Sixteen months of identical status is the finding, the position being unchanged since [the ministry said negotiations were continuing in April 2025](https://pti.ao/minttics-assegura-que-negociacoes-com-a-starlink-continuam/).</details> | Advanced, one operator now commercially deployed while another application stands unresolved |
| Benin | [ARCEP Decision 2024-108 sets the conditions for non-terrestrial network service providers](https://arcep.bj/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/DECISION-N%C2%B02024-108-ARCEP-BENIN.pdf). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The regulator's Decision 2024-108 of 11 April 2024, made under the Code du numerique, fixes the conditions under which providers of non-terrestrial network services may operate](https://arcep.bj/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/DECISION-N%C2%B02024-108-ARCEP-BENIN.pdf) — the licensing regime the satellite operators in the market run under.<br><br>No licence award, applicant list or authorisation register under the decision is held.</details> | Advanced |
| Botswana | The incumbent [connected 61 rural villages by satellite with 105 areas in progress](https://itweb.africa/article/btcs-new-reach-extends-to-61-villages-in-botswana/rxP3jqBEWB4MA2ye), and no update has been published since November 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-11-28 — [the incumbent operator reported 61 rural villages connected by satellite — schools, clinics and community centres — with 105 further areas in process](https://itweb.africa/article/btcs-new-reach-extends-to-61-villages-in-botswana/rxP3jqBEWB4MA2ye), announced with half-year results. No baseline preceded it and no update has followed in nine months.<br><br>That is the whole of the satellite record for the country: no licensing decision, no low-earth-orbit operator, no tariff and no subscriber figure appears. The one place a satellite operator does appear is in a risk register — the state wholesale operator escalated low-earth-orbit competition to its board as a structural disruption, reported under national production capacity.</details> | Advanced, on one programme with nothing reported since |
| Burkina Faso | The satellite operator [is still unlicensed with a ministerial decision pending](https://www.queenmafa.net/2026/07/26/chantier-zero-donnee-a-lexterieur-120-plateformes-deja-repertoriees/), after [the regulator declared terminal sales irregular](https://www.arcep.bf/communique-sur-la-commercialisation-irreguliere-de-starlink/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The regulator declared terminal sales irregular under the 2008 telecommunications law](https://www.arcep.bf/communique-sur-la-commercialisation-irreguliere-de-starlink/), and 2026-07-26 — [the operator remains unlicensed, with ministry negotiations reported advancing and the minister saying she hopes to rule soon](https://www.queenmafa.net/2026/07/26/chantier-zero-donnee-a-lexterieur-120-plateformes-deja-repertoriees/).<br><br>The position is unchanged across the window. The operator's own continental map still listed the country as available from 2026 without a licence, so an unlicensed service is being marketed into a market whose regulator has said the terminals are illegal.<br><br>No second satellite operator, no capacity purchase and no institutional use of satellite backhaul appears on this ledger, which matters for the white-zone programme reported under digital divides: the localities it targets are the ones terrestrial backhaul reaches last.</details> | No change |
| Burundi | Satellite broadband [is licensed but effectively unavailable on cost, the kit alone at roughly US$500](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/unreliable-connectivity-impacts-burundi-s-citizens), unchanged through the window. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[The service is licensed and effectively unavailable on price, with the terminal alone at roughly US$500](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/unreliable-connectivity-impacts-burundi-s-citizens). That was the position at the start of the window and nothing has moved it since: no tariff change, no subscriber figure, no second satellite operator and no regulatory statement appears.<br><br>It is the only alternative to a mobile network that the regulator describes as failing mainly on electricity, and it is priced beyond the population it would serve. No universal-service subsidy, terminal financing or institutional purchase of satellite capacity is on the ledger.</details> | No change |
| Cameroon | A [draft concession convention was finalised in December 2025](https://actucameroun.info/2025/12/12/internet-satellitaire-starlink-finalise-son-projet-de-convention-de-concession-avec-lart-avant-son-entree-au-cameroun/), with the [file at the ministry undecided for more than two years](https://newswatchcameroon.com/cameroon-starlink-licensing-stalls-over-security-sovereignty-concerns/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The [licence was refused in March 2025](https://www.camerounweb.com/CameroonHomePage/NewsArchive/EXCLUSIF-Le-Cameroun-refuse-d-octroyer-une-licence-Starlink-l-entreprise-d-Elon-Musk-dans-le-viseur-des-autorit-s-779264). 2025-12-12 - the regulator [finalised a draft concession convention and specification](https://actucameroun.info/2025/12/12/internet-satellitaire-starlink-finalise-son-projet-de-convention-de-concession-avec-lart-avant-son-entree-au-cameroun/), and as at July 2026 the [file at the ministry had been undecided for more than two years, with the objections on record being lawful interception, armed-group use and the incumbent's commercial position](https://newswatchcameroon.com/cameroon-starlink-licensing-stalls-over-security-sovereignty-concerns/).<br><br>The [2024 customs circular remains the operative instrument, with kits entering through indirect registration abroad and ten modems seized at one post on 12 December 2025](https://actucameroun.com/2025/12/16/la-douane-saisit-des-kits-starlink-a-nkometou/). No other satellite broadband offer is on file, so the country's whole position on the technology is one undecided file and a seizure regime.</details> | Advanced, a draft concession convention was finalised while the file stayed undecided and the seizure circular stayed operative |
| Cape Verde | The satellite entrant [reached 0.2% of internet subscriptions by June 2025](https://expressodasilhas.cv/economia/2025/09/18/starlink-conquista-02-do-mercado-da-internet-em-9-meses/99028), and the regulator [opened a consultation on satellite rules](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/08/05/cape-verde-opens-public-consultation-on-new-satellite-communications-regulatory-framework/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The entrant [began activity in January 2025 under a general authorisation granted in 2024 and holds 0.2% of internet subscriptions at 30 June 2025, up 0.12 percentage points on the quarter in a market of 484,929 subscriptions](https://expressodasilhas.cv/economia/2025/09/18/starlink-conquista-02-do-mercado-da-internet-em-9-meses/99028); the [Prime Minister had reported entry as an accomplished fact in November 2024](https://www.governo.cv/discurso-de-abertura-do-primeiro-ministro-debate-sobre-o-orcamento-de-estado-para-2025/) while the regulator dates commercial start to January 2025. No market-share figure later than that quarter is held.<br><br>2026-08-05 - the regulator [opened a public consultation on licensing, radio-spectrum use, frequency coordination, user protection, network security and emergency communications for satellite operators, submissions to 31 August 2026, a direct response to the December 2024 launch](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/08/05/cape-verde-opens-public-consultation-on-new-satellite-communications-regulatory-framework/); no draft text or adoption date is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Central African Republic | Satellite broadband [went commercially live in March 2026 with 450 government kits](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/1703-136713-starlink-en-centrafrique-entre-couverture-nationale-et-defis-d-accessibilite), and the regulator [began a field sweep against unregistered access points in August](https://www.radiondekeluka.org/104273-telecom-le-ministere-des-telecommunications-et-larcep-lancent-la-chasse-aux-fournisseurs-illegaux-dinternet). <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-03-16 — [the service went commercially live as the operator's 27th African market, with 450 government kits](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/1703-136713-starlink-en-centrafrique-entre-couverture-nationale-et-defis-d-accessibilite), on [a convention signed in December 2025 with United States embassy involvement](https://primature.cf/2025/12/19/starlink-en-rca-un-partenariat-pour-booster-economie-et-leducation/). No licence and no service existed before.<br><br>Enforcement followed at once. [Roaming was deactivated on kits acquired abroad that had not met homologation conditions, on traceability and national-security grounds](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/28/central-african-republic-suspends-starlink-kits-citing-security-and-equipment-traceability-requirements/), [six weeks after commercial launch](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/04/29/central-african-republic-suspends-unauthorised-starlink-roaming-kits-over-regulatory-and-security-concerns/). 2026-08-12 — [the regulator began a control operation across named capital neighbourhoods identifying unregistered internet-access points, with the ministry estimating about 3,000 users in the country against 200 registered, and the operation to extend progressively to provincial cities](https://www.radiondekeluka.org/104273-telecom-le-ministere-des-telecommunications-et-larcep-lancent-la-chasse-aux-fournisseurs-illegaux-dinternet). The ratio is the ministry's own estimate, no register total is published, and two of the operators visited said they had not been told registration was required.<br><br>What limits the service is its price rather than its reach, and that is reported below.</details> | Advanced |
| Chad | A [satellite operating licence](https://portail.td/starlink-obtient-sa-licence-dexploitation-au-tchad-un-tournant-majeur-pour-lacces-a-internet/) was granted and commercial launch announced, under an [order on conditions of use](https://tchadinfos.com/2025/08/28/tchad-le-gouvernement-fixe-les-conditions-dutilisation-des-services-starlink/) that mandates identification and retention ahead of any data-protection law. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The licence was granted in March 2025 and commercial launch was announced by the minister in June 2026; no subscriber count is held at any date, and the government complained to the operator in Washington in May 2026 about service quality and tariffs it judged less competitive than in other African markets ([licence](https://portail.td/starlink-obtient-sa-licence-dexploitation-au-tchad-un-tournant-majeur-pour-lacces-a-internet/), [launch](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/general-news/11399-starlink-launches-services-in-niger-and-secures-operating-license-in-chad/), [service](https://tchadinfos.com/2025/07/05/les-services-des-starlink-desormais-disponibles-au-tchad/), [complaint](https://www.digitalbusiness.africa/tchad-a-washington-ndjamena-multiplie-les-contacts-avec-starlink-intersystems-et-dautres-geants-technologiques/)).<br><br>The order on conditions of use was signed in August 2025. Its own number and text are not held, and identification and retention are mandated ahead of any data-protection law ([order](https://tchadinfos.com/2025/08/28/tchad-le-gouvernement-fixe-les-conditions-dutilisation-des-services-starlink/)).<br><br>Requiring subscriber identification and data retention in a country with no protection statute and no supervisory authority is the pattern this unit repeats.</details> | Advanced |
| Comoros | A [ministerial circular of 9 June 2025 prohibits installation, testing, deployment and use of satellite internet](https://alwatwan.net/societe/d%C3%A9ploiement-de-starlink-i-les-autorit%C3%A9s-interdisent-son-usage-pour-l%E2%80%99instant.html), pending a licence. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The circular was [signed on 9 June 2025 and announced by the regulator on 12 June, with installation, testing, deployment and use all prohibited and no licence issued](https://alwatwan.net/societe/d%C3%A9ploiement-de-starlink-i-les-autorit%C3%A9s-interdisent-son-usage-pour-l%E2%80%99instant.html).<br><br>It is still in force on the record: no licence, no lifting and no enforcement outcome is held. The circular's own text is not held, a field inspection to identify and notify users was announced with no outcome on file, and whether the operator has since been authorised is expressly not established - which for an archipelago whose inter-island connectivity is itself at feasibility-study stage is the material gap.</details> | No change |
| Congo | The regulator [completed low-orbit specialist training and met a satellite operator](https://www.adiac-congo.com/content/communications-electroniques-larpce-renforce-son-expertise-sur-les-satellites-de); no licence or service authorisation is on file. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-10 - the regulator's network-services directorate [completed three days of specialist training in France on constellations, transmission security, spectrum management and regulatory adaptability, and held a strategic meeting on low-orbit and high-altitude platform regulation](https://www.adiac-congo.com/content/communications-electroniques-larpce-renforce-son-expertise-sur-les-satellites-de).<br><br>Regulatory capacity was built and nothing has been licensed. No licence, service launch or authorisation is on file at 2026-08-06, experimental satellite terminals require express regulator authorisation, and the satellite thread runs to European operators rather than to the American one that has been licensed in most neighbouring markets.</details> | Advanced |
| Cote d'Ivoire | [Starlink began selling nationally in July 2026 on a twelve-month provisional licence](https://mybroadband.co.za/news/broadband/658586-starlink-launches-in-27th-african-country-before-south-africa.html), alongside two operator satellite offers. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The position at the window's opening was that the entrant was [not authorised to sell, the regulator having threatened sanctions in 2024 for supplying internet without authorisation](https://www.orbit.sn/cote-divoire-lartci-menace-de-sanctionner-starlink-pour-fourniture-des-services-internet-sans-autorisation/), with [frequencies since assigned](https://www.digitalbusiness.africa/cote-divoire-lartci-autorise-starlink-a-operer-officiellement-sur-le-territoire/).<br><br>2026-07-17 - it [went live nationally](https://mybroadband.co.za/news/broadband/658586-starlink-launches-in-27th-african-country-before-south-africa.html) on a [twelve-month provisional licence reviewable on service quality, hardware XOF 148,148 to 247,466 and service from XOF 28,746 a month](https://tech-ish.com/2026/07/28/starlink-28-african-countries-kenya-freeze/). The definitive individual licence under the 2024 electronic communications law is not yet issued.<br><br>Both operators sell into rural white areas, one on [leased geostationary capacity from January 2026](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/orange-c-te-d-ivoire-eutelsat-launch-satellite-broadband-services) and one on [hybrid geostationary and low-orbit capacity from April 2026](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/24/mtn-cote-divoire-partners-eutelsat-to-boost-broadband-coverage-with-konnect-satellite-capacity/), neither with a subscriber, price or coverage figure on file.</details> | Advanced |
| Djibouti | The incumbent's chief executive [states there is no clear regulatory framework for new entrants](https://african.business/2025/04/long-reads/djibouti-telecom-at-a-crossroads); no licensing decision is on file. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The position is unchanged at both ends: the [incumbent's chief executive states there is no clear regulatory framework for new entrants, and no licensing decision is on file](https://african.business/2025/04/long-reads/djibouti-telecom-at-a-crossroads).<br><br>It is a cited absence rather than an unexamined one, and it is stated by the party the absence protects. No application, refusal, consultation or draft framework for satellite market entry appears anywhere on this ledger, in a country whose interior regions the domestic fibre programme is still reaching one facility at a time.</details> | No change |
| DR Congo | A [satellite direct-to-cell service launched commercially on 14 August 2026](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/airtel-africa-starlink-launch-satellite-to-mobile-service-in-drc), while the [sovereign satellite programme is unchanged](https://actualite.cd/2025/08/31/la-rdc-se-lance-dans-un-projet-dacquisition-dun-satellite-cout-estime-400-millions-de). <details><summary>Full record</summary>The satellite provider has been [licensed since May 2025, reversing a March 2024 prohibition](https://actualite.cd/2025/05/02/rdc-starlink-obtient-une-licence-officielle-pour-operer-comme-fournisseur-dacces), a decision [civil society welcomed as a corrective to incumbent monopoly abuse](https://actualite.cd/2025/05/05/acaj-salue-lhomologation-de-la-firme-americaine-starlink-pour-mettre-fin-aux-abus-de); the licence conditions and any enforcement outcome are not established.<br><br>2026-08-14 - a [satellite-to-mobile service launched commercially, the first African operator to take such a partnership out of testing: a compatible handset with a clear view of the sky reaches messaging and voice where there is no terrestrial coverage, with no dish or router, on a phased rollout the regulator has confirmed](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/airtel-africa-starlink-launch-satellite-to-mobile-service-in-drc). A [free 30-day trial is the only access term published](https://numerico.cd/2026/08/14/airtel-rdc-et-starlink-rdc-a-la-conquete-des-zones-blanches/).<br><br>The [sovereign satellite programme has no contract, operator selection, build or launch date twelve months on](https://actualite.cd/2025/08/31/la-rdc-se-lance-dans-un-projet-dacquisition-dun-satellite-cout-estime-400-millions-de).</details> | Mixed, a satellite-to-mobile service launched commercially while the sovereign satellite programme did not move in twelve months |
| Egypt | [Satellite talks moved to a licensed local operator with no licence, entity or service date](https://www.egypttelegraph.com/article/239892/%D8%A3%D9%88%D9%86%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%8A%D9%86-%D8%AA%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%83%D9%88%D9%85%E2%80%9D-%D8%AA%D8%A8%D8%AD%D8%AB-%D9%85%D8%B9-%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%A7%D8%B1%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%86%D9%83-%D8%A5%D8%B7%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%82-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D9%86%D8%AA%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%AA), a possible 2025 launch having passed. <details><summary>Full record</summary>[Advanced ministry negotiations were reported in June 2025 with a possible launch during that year](https://www.egypttelegraph.com/article/154808/%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D9%86%D8%AA%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%AA-%D8%A7%D9%84%D9%81%D8%B6%D8%A7%D8%A6%D9%8A-%D9%8A%D8%B7%D8%B1%D9%82-%D8%A3%D8%A8%D9%88%D8%A7%D8%A8-%D9%85%D8%B5%D8%B1-%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%A7%D8%B1%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%86%D9%83-%D8%AA%D9%86%D8%AA%D8%B8%D8%B1). That expectation passed without delivery. [Talks moved to a licensed local operator, with no licence, no local entity, no service date, and the operator's own map listing the country as service date unknown in May 2026](https://www.egypttelegraph.com/article/239892/%D8%A3%D9%88%D9%86%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%8A%D9%86-%D8%AA%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%83%D9%88%D9%85%E2%80%9D-%D8%AA%D8%A8%D8%AD%D8%AB-%D9%85%D8%B9-%D8%B3%D8%AA%D8%A7%D8%B1%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%86%D9%83-%D8%A5%D8%B7%D9%84%D8%A7%D9%82-%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A5%D9%86%D8%AA%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%AA); nothing is held on a June 2026 meeting or after.<br><br>The standing instrument is a [framework in force since 9 July 2024 for any foreign satellite operator serving earth-station terminals in the country, granting categorical licences to operators supplying space capacity to licensed providers](https://digitalpolicyalert.org/change/12368-ntra-regulatory-framework-for-foreign-satellite-operators-providing-telecommunications-services-to-earth-stations-terminals-within-egypt), [adopted in October 2023 with a grace period](https://www.tra.gov.eg/en/the-satellite-operator-regulatory-framework/). No foreign low-earth-orbit operator has been licensed under it, and the telecommunications law criminalises unlicensed wireless equipment with a minimum of one year's imprisonment.</details> | Stalled |
| Equatorial Guinea | The satellite service is [now available in the country](https://realequatorialguinea.com/destacado/mundo/elon-musk-confirma-que-el-internet-satelital-de-starlink-ya-esta-disponible-en-guinea-ecuatorial/), after a July 2026 authorisation that covered only foreign companies' offshore operations. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The July authorisation covered North American companies' offshore operations inside jurisdictional waters and was signed at the presidential palace on 28 July 2026, with extension to individual subscribers deferred on stated security grounds ([authorisation](https://www.guineaecuatorialpress.com/noticias/guinea_ecuatorial_autoriza_uso_de_starlink_a_empresas_norteamericanas_en_alta_mar)).<br><br>On 29 August 2026 the operator confirmed the service is available in the country, following the government's opening of negotiations on national digital transformation in March 2026 ([availability](https://realequatorialguinea.com/destacado/mundo/elon-musk-confirma-que-el-internet-satelital-de-starlink-ya-esta-disponible-en-guinea-ecuatorial/)).<br><br>The confirmation is the operator's own, made on its and its owner's social-media accounts. Type-approval and competences were unresolved with the regulator a month earlier ([implementation](https://ahoraeg.com/politica/2026/07/29/el-gobierno-acelera-la-implementacion-del-internet-satelital-de-starlink-en-el-pais/)), against an entry signalled in February 2025 that was not then authorised ([entry](https://realequatorialguinea.com/sociedad/la-empresa-starlink-de-eeuu-desea-implantarse-en-guinea-ecuatorial-para-mejorar-la-conectividad-a-internet/)).<br><br>So a service that was lawful for foreign companies at sea and unlawful for citizens on land is now stated to be generally available, and the licence, tariff and subscriber figures that would show on what terms are not held.</details> | Advanced |
| Eritrea | Starlink [lists Eritrea with no planned launch date](https://mybroadband.co.za/news/broadband/658586-starlink-launches-in-27th-african-country-before-south-africa.html), one of seven African states without a timetable; the base holds no licence application or refusal. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2026-07-17 — Starlink's own availability map [still lists Eritrea as having no planned launch date](https://mybroadband.co.za/news/broadband/658586-starlink-launches-in-27th-african-country-before-south-africa.html), leaving it one of seven African states with no announced timetable, all of them in North Africa and the Horn. The position is the one the base carried at the start of the window.<br><br>What is not on the ledger is any Eritrean decision: no application, no refusal and no regulatory statement about satellite broadband appears, so the absence is recorded from the operator's side only.</details> | No change |
| Eswatini | A [government satellite gateway was commissioned](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/28/eswatini-commissions-starlink-satellite-gateway-to-expand-nationwide-internet-connectivity/) to reach mountainous and remote areas and reduce dependence on cross-border fibre, and a [business reseller channel](https://itweb.africa/article/recently-launched-paratus-eswatini-aims-to-drive-starlink-business-tech-adoption/j5alr7QAOeQ7pYQk) launched as a joint venture. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The gateway was commissioned in April 2026, its stated purpose reaching mountainous and remote areas and reducing dependence on cross-border fibre ([gateway](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/28/eswatini-commissions-starlink-satellite-gateway-to-expand-nationwide-internet-connectivity/)).<br><br>A joint venture launched as an authorised business reseller in July 2025 ([reseller](https://itweb.africa/article/recently-launched-paratus-eswatini-aims-to-drive-starlink-business-tech-adoption/j5alr7QAOeQ7pYQk)).<br><br>A government-commissioned gateway is an unusual arrangement — most units in this frame record licensing and resale rather than state operation — and no capacity, coverage or cost figure is published for it.</details> | Advanced |
| Ethiopia | Satellite services are licensable as a [class landing-right authorisation](https://www.eca.et/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/2022-03-24T06-45-04.775ZTelecommunications-Licensing-Directive-No.-792-2021-English.pdf), and no low-earth-orbit operator is authorised on the record. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The licensing directive of July 2021 carries a class authorisation for satellite voice, internet and data services provided without infrastructure or a network facility in the country, one of thirteen class licences alongside internet service provider, data centre, hosting and terminal authorisations ([licensing directive](https://www.eca.et/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/2022-03-24T06-45-04.775ZTelecommunications-Licensing-Directive-No.-792-2021-English.pdf)).<br><br>There is no orbit-specific determination and no authorised operator on record, and the regulator denied in July 2025 that any discussions with a low-earth-orbit operator had taken place ([the same record](https://www.eca.et/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/2022-03-24T06-45-04.775ZTelecommunications-Licensing-Directive-No.-792-2021-English.pdf)).</details> | No change |
| Gabon | The regulator [prohibited the sale, installation and use](https://afrique.le360.ma/economie/internet-haut-debit-en-seulement-4-ans-starlink-operationnel-dans-un-pays-africain-sur-deux_UYXWC5RPWNGGFIYLU5VHL6L7ZU/) of one satellite service in May 2026 on penalty of fines, imprisonment and equipment seizure. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The prohibition follows a warning against illegal commercialisation issued in November 2025 ([regulator warning](https://www.gabonreview.com/commercialisation-illegale-de-starlink-larcep-met-en-garde-les-usagers/)), and was announced with nationwide enforcement controls.<br><br>At the same date the operator's own map still listed the country as available from 2026, with no licence granted ([prohibition](https://afrique.le360.ma/economie/internet-haut-debit-en-seulement-4-ans-starlink-operationnel-dans-un-pays-africain-sur-deux_UYXWC5RPWNGGFIYLU5VHL6L7ZU/)).<br><br>No enforcement outcome is on file: no seizure, fine or prosecution has been reported, and no authorised satellite broadband provider exists on the record.</details> | Regressed |
| Gambia | A satellite licence remains [withheld after more than a year](https://orbitaltoday.com/2026/05/24/starlink-licence-gets-delayed-by-the-gambia/), the minister stating the applicant needs a general satellite licence rather than the permit sought. <details><summary>Full record</summary>He stated on 24 May 2026 that he had refused to approve the permit and that the applicant lacks the competencies and equipment for the terminal permit ([refusal](https://orbitaltoday.com/2026/05/24/starlink-licence-gets-delayed-by-the-gambia/)).<br><br>A fee was paid and the licence left unissued for over a year, the government citing due diligence in the face of United States pressure ([due diligence](https://standard.gm/govt-taking-due-diligence-despite-us-pressure-for-starlinks-license/)), and the delay has been argued in public as a transparency and trust question ([public argument](https://standard.gm/starlink-and-the-gambia-transparency-trust-and-the-cost-of-delay/)).<br><br>The base carries a fee paid in December 2024 and a permit payment in February 2026, and whether these are the same payment is not established.</details> | No change |
| Ghana | [29 satellite licences](https://nca.org.gh/2024/08/22/starlink-starts-operations-in-ghana-end-of-august-2024/) have been issued, eleven of them last-mile, and subscriptions reached 15,384 by mid-2025 from 2,666 before licensing. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The framework has operated since the end of August 2024 and nothing has been published on it since ([framework](https://nca.org.gh/2024/08/22/starlink-starts-operations-in-ghana-end-of-august-2024/)).<br><br>Subscriptions ran at 11,982 in the first quarter of 2025 and 15,384 in the second, from 2,666 before satellite licensing, on a regulator presentation to the international union ([subscriptions](https://www.itu.int/itu-d/meetings/wp-content/uploads/IPS/documents/35/35_ITU_EGTI2025_EmanuelNeizer_SatelliteBasedInternetConnectivityStatistics_GhanaExperience.pdf)).<br><br>No figure has been published for any quarter after mid-2025, so a fast-moving series stops eighteen months before this report.</details> | Advanced |
| Guinea | One satellite provider is [stated not to be recognised](https://mediaguinee.com/2026/05/interdiction-des-activites-des-wifi-zones-et-restriction-du-ftth-la-fesatel-hausse-le-ton-et-menace-dune-greve-ni-les-appels-ne-marchent-ni-la-connexion-internet-ne-marche/) in the country, and no licensing decision is on file. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The operators' union made the statement in May 2026 in the course of its dispute over access licensing ([union statement](https://mediaguinee.com/2026/05/interdiction-des-activites-des-wifi-zones-et-restriction-du-ftth-la-fesatel-hausse-le-ton-et-menace-dune-greve-ni-les-appels-ne-marchent-ni-la-connexion-internet-ne-marche/)).<br><br>No authorisation, refusal, framework or enforcement action on satellite broadband is held at any date, so the position is a third party's characterisation rather than a regulatory decision.</details> | No change |
| Guinea-Bissau | Starlink [has been commercially live since June 2025](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/starlink-enters-guinea-bissau) under [a provisional authorisation from the regulator](https://arn.gw/arn-tic-concede-autorizacao-geral-provisoria-a-starlink-guine-bissau/), and nothing moved in the window. <details><summary>Full record</summary>2025-06-19 — Starlink [entered service, Guinea-Bissau's seventh West African and twentieth continental market](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/starlink-enters-guinea-bissau), following [a provisional authorisation from ARN-TIC in April 2025](https://arn.gw/arn-tic-concede-autorizacao-geral-provisoria-a-starlink-guine-bissau/). Both events fall before the twelve-month window.<br><br>Nothing since records a definitive licence, a second satellite operator, a subscriber figure or a tariff, so what the base holds is the fact of availability and nothing about its reach. An initial provisional licence referred to in the launch record is not itself held.<br><br>Satellite is the only service reaching what the absent national backbone does not, which is what makes the missing uptake figure matter.</details> | No change |
| Kenya | Satellite broadband reached [24,999 subscribers and 0.9 per cent of fixed share](https://tech-ish.com/2026/06/19/starlink-hits-25000-subscribers-in-kenya/), with new orders in seven counties diverted to a waitlist. <details><summary>Full record</summary>It stood at 17,425 subscribers and 0.8 per cent share in the quarter to June 2025 on 359 net adds ([earlier quarter](https://sauce.co.ke/2025/09/starlink-adds-359-new-kenyan-customers-but-market-share-slips/)); by March 2026 it was a top-ten fixed provider with median speed of 34.55 Mbps, down 26 per cent year on year, and sign-ups in seven counties were suspended to a deposit-backed waitlist ([cap](https://techweez.com/2026/07/08/starlink-kenya-waitlist-capacity-counties/)).<br><br>A direct-to-handset pilot with the second mobile operator was declared complete and awaits regulator approval; it would be the country's first such service on compatible handsets with no extra module or charge ([pilot](https://businessday.ng/technology/article/airtel-seeks-kenya-approval-for-spacex-powered-mobile-service/)).<br><br>A spectrum-interference audit opened in March 2026 with its conclusion put at mid-year had produced no finding, clearance or refusal as at August ([audit](https://businessday.ng/technology/article/airtel-seeks-kenya-approval-for-spacex-powered-mobile-service/)).<br><br>Separately a hyperscaler's constellation has applied for a fifteen-year international gateway licence covering an earth station and network control centre, the wholesale route into the market rather than the household one; neither the regulator's decision nor a determination date is held ([gateway application](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/20/who-will-control-africas-satellite-internet-layer-starlink-amazon-leo-or-the-carriers/)).</details> | Mixed, subscribers grew while new sign-ups were capped in seven counties and two approvals stalled |
| Lesotho | A [ten-year satellite licence](https://publiceyenews.com/2025/04/22/mixed-reactions-over-starlink-license/) was granted in April 2025 over local-shareholding objections, and the [service is live](https://lestimes.com/?p=87368) through a sole reseller. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The communications authority granted a ten-year licence on 14 April 2025, contested over full foreign ownership: the incumbent appealed for local shareholding and a rights group objected ([licence](https://publiceyenews.com/2025/04/22/mixed-reactions-over-starlink-license/), [objection](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/starlink-faces-opposition-in-lesotho)). Nothing later on record resolves the objection.<br><br>The service launched through T-Connect as exclusive reseller on 30 June 2025 ([launch](https://lestimes.com/?p=87368), [account](https://publiceyenews.com/2025/07/01/starlink-blasts-off-in-lesotho/)), and by January 2026 was reported also carried over the incumbent's low-earth-orbit backhaul ([backhaul](https://www.techinafrica.com/starlink-partners-airtel-vodacom-rival-ally/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Liberia | [Satellite communications guidelines](https://lta.gov.lr/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/SATELLITE-COMMUNICATIONS-GUIDELINES.clean_.fv_.2.25.26-FINAL-VERSION.pdf) were issued in August 2026 setting licensing, tax and service rules, with no licence yet issued under them. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The regulator frames the rules around national security and commercial transparency as space-based solutions become the route to territories terrestrial networks have not reached ([market rules](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/telecom/licences-taxes-services--le-liberia-definit-les-regles-du-marche-des-telecoms-par-satellite), [guidelines](https://lta.gov.lr/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/SATELLITE-COMMUNICATIONS-GUIDELINES.clean_.fv_.2.25.26-FINAL-VERSION.pdf)).<br><br>It frames them as opening licensed satellite broadband to the named constellations ([framework](https://www.liberianobserver.com/news/lta-signs-new-satellite-framework-targets-digital-access-across-liberia/article_e178022e-dcdf-47e5-a31b-9abefbb594c2.html)), and no licence has yet been issued on the record.</details> | Advanced |
| Libya | The state operator signed a capacity partnership with [Arabsat](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/07/arabsat-and-ltt-sign-strategic-partnership-to-strengthen-libyas-digital-infrastructure/) in August 2026 for the 30.5 degrees east position; no commercial terms are stated. <details><summary>Full record</summary>LTT and Arabsat announced a partnership on 7 August 2026 for capacity on Arabsat-6A at 30.5 degrees east ([partnership](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/07/arabsat-and-ltt-sign-strategic-partnership-to-strengthen-libyas-digital-infrastructure/)).<br><br>No value, duration, service date or retail offer is published, and the base holds nothing on licensing terms for any other satellite provider, including the low-orbit operators active elsewhere in the region.</details> | Advanced |
| Madagascar | A [direct-to-cell satellite trial](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/general-news/28982-madagascar-conducts-first-direct-to-cell-satellite-trial-advancing-digital-inclusion/) ran in July 2026 on operator spectrum, a [low-earth-orbit rural partnership](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/01/airtel-madagascar-and-eutelsat-deepen-push-for-rural-broadband-access/) deepened, and [ten enclaved zones](https://2424.mg/telecommunication-linternet-par-satellite-a-deployer-dans-dix-zones-enclavees-dans-le-cadre-du-projet-decim-et-de-la-politique-rse-de-starlink/) remain unconnected. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The first direct-to-cell trial ran at Sadabe on 11 July 2026 on an operator's spectrum, the third African country to do so, with commercial launch pending the regulator ([trial](https://www.telecomreviewafrica.com/articles/general-news/28982-madagascar-conducts-first-direct-to-cell-satellite-trial-advancing-digital-inclusion/), [authorisation](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/05/19/madagascar-set-to-launch-starlink-direct-to-cell-services/)).<br><br>A separate low-earth-orbit rural broadband partnership was deepened in June 2026, with no rural site count or subscriber figure published ([partnership](https://techafricanews.com/2026/06/01/airtel-madagascar-and-eutelsat-deepen-push-for-rural-broadband-access/)).<br><br>An arrangement to connect ten enclaved zones under the connectivity project was discussed in March 2025 and nothing has been connected, with no site or date published ([zones](https://2424.mg/telecommunication-linternet-par-satellite-a-deployer-dans-dix-zones-enclavees-dans-le-cadre-du-projet-decim-et-de-la-politique-rse-de-starlink/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Malawi | [Thirty rural schools were connected by satellite](https://technomag.co.zw/starlink-connects-100000-students-and-1500-teachers-in-rural-malawi/), and an operator [signalled interest in satellite backhaul](https://techafricanews.com/2026/05/22/airtel-malawi-eyes-satellite-connectivity-as-it-boosts-network-expansion-strategy/) at its annual meeting. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Thirty rural schools were reported connected, serving 100,000 students and 1,500 teachers on a company announcement ([schools](https://technomag.co.zw/starlink-connects-100000-students-and-1500-teachers-in-rural-malawi/)); responsibility for the recurring subscription cost is not stated, and the student figure averages over 3,000 per school without reconciliation ([schools](https://technomag.co.zw/starlink-connects-100000-students-and-1500-teachers-in-rural-malawi/), [preparation](https://www.worldbank.org/en/results/2025/06/23/digitalizing-afe-malawi-to-improve-access-to-education-public-services-and-income-opportunities)).<br><br>An operator signalled interest in satellite connectivity to complement terrestrial networks in hard-to-reach areas and improve resilience, with no partner, licence, capital allocation or timetable stated ([interest](https://techafricanews.com/2026/05/22/airtel-malawi-eyes-satellite-connectivity-as-it-boosts-network-expansion-strategy/)).<br><br>The base holds no satellite licence date, licence terms or subscriber figure for the country.</details> | Advanced |
| Mali | An operator's [satellite 3G and 4G service](https://techafricanews.com/2025/05/07/orange-mali-and-intelsat-bring-4g-connectivity-to-malis-hard-to-reach-regions/) reaches about 360,000 remote residents, while the regulator is [seizing unauthorised terminals](https://libreinfos.com/2026/07/02/telecommunications-letat-declare-la-guerre-aux-exploitants-illegaux-de-wifi-zone-starlink-et-talkie-walkie/) nationwide. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The satellite-backhauled service is in service reaching about 360,000 remote residents, with no later coverage figure published ([service](https://techafricanews.com/2025/05/07/orange-mali-and-intelsat-bring-4g-connectivity-to-malis-hard-to-reach-regions/), [loan](https://www.ifc.org/en/pressroom/2025/ifc-invests-in-orange-mali-to-expand-digital-connectivity-and-support-women-s-digi)).<br><br>A six-month lifting of the import ban on low-orbit kits was reported in October 2024 ([lifting](https://www.voaafrique.com/a/le-pouvoir-malien-l%C3%A8ve-l-interdiction-des-kits-starlink/7817463.html)), the prohibition on unauthorised equipment was restated in March 2026 with a warning of sanctions ([prohibition](https://lepays.ml/starlink-wifi-zone-non-homologues-lamrtp-rappelle-linterdiction/)), and control, seizure and dismantling operations were announced in June 2026 against unauthorised kits, shared Wi-Fi zones and walkie-talkies, with operators ordered to cease immediately ([enforcement](https://libreinfos.com/2026/07/02/telecommunications-letat-declare-la-guerre-aux-exploitants-illegaux-de-wifi-zone-starlink-et-talkie-walkie/)). Imports and use remain banned pending authorisation.</details> | Mixed, a licensed operator's satellite service reaching remote residents while unauthorised terminals are seized |
| Mauritania | [Three firms were provisionally selected](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/0707-139950-mauritanie-trois-entreprises-selectionnees-pour-fournir-des-services-telecoms-par-satellite) for satellite internet licences across two lots in July 2026, with no award or operating framework published. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The tender opened in January 2026 and nine bids were received, from which three firms were provisionally retained, two of them required to match the leading offer ([selection](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/0707-139950-mauritanie-trois-entreprises-selectionnees-pour-fournir-des-services-telecoms-par-satellite), [tender](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/29/mauritania-launches-tender-for-satellite-internet-licenses-to-expand-connectivity/)).<br><br>Thirteen firms took the dossier, and the leading bid was US$800,000 for the mobile lot and US$3m for capacity against US$50,000 and MRU 6.5m from the other two ([bids](https://www.agenceecofin.com/actualites-numerique/0707-139950-mauritanie-trois-entreprises-selectionnees-pour-fournir-des-services-telecoms-par-satellite)).<br><br>The regulator's own communique is not held and the figures reach the base through a trade account of it. No award, launch date or operating framework has been published.</details> | Advanced |
| Mauritius | The [satellite authorisation regime](https://www.icta.mu/documents/2025/05/decision_authorising_internet_through_non_geostationary_satellite_orbit.pdf) requires a locally licensed provider, a local point of presence and provider-retained terminals, and no service has been authorised under it. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Raphael Island in the Saint Brandon archipelago was connected to the internet ([report](https://lexpress.mu/s/lile-raphael-se-connecte-a-internet-562034)).<br><br>The regulator's decision of May 2025 permits service only through a locally licensed provider, with a local point of presence carrying Mauritian-geolocated addresses, real-time terminal dashboards and provider-retained terminal ownership ([decision](https://www.icta.mu/documents/2025/05/decision_authorising_internet_through_non_geostationary_satellite_orbit.pdf)).<br><br>No satellite service has been authorised under it. Domestic experts are divided on its relevance given the country's existing fibre and mobile coverage ([debate](https://newsmoris.com/starlink-expands-to-30-african-markets-sparking-debate-over-mauritius-digital-future/)), which is the argument a small dense island makes against low-orbit service that larger neighbours cannot.<br><br>The ICT authority awarded a two-year contract on 28 August 2026 to provide satellite-based internet and communications to Raphael Island in the St Brandon archipelago, a first for the archipelago, intended to strengthen maritime monitoring, meteorological capability and safety in a cyclone-prone area ([contract](https://platformafrica.com/2026/08/28/emtel-is-awarded-icta-contract-to-bring-internet-connectivity-to-st-brandon/)). No service date, capacity or contract value is stated.</details> | Advanced |
| Morocco | [Starlink has no licence](https://medias24.com/2026/03/16/starlink-etend-sa-couverture-dans-le-monde-quelle-situation-au-maroc-1643995/) eighteen months after incorporating a local subsidiary, while a [satellite terminal licence](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f) was renewed in February 2026 to 2035. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The subsidiary was incorporated in November 2024 with capital of MAD 1,000 and still had no authorisation in May 2026, with regulator negotiations reported continuing and the operator's own map listing the service date as unknown ([status](https://medias24.com/2026/03/16/starlink-etend-sa-couverture-dans-le-monde-quelle-situation-au-maroc-1643995/), [subsidiary](https://www.maroc-hebdo.com/article/starlink-lance-sa-filiale-marocaine), [observation](https://www.challenge.ma/starlink-pourquoi-le-maroc-joue-t-il-la-carte-de-lobservation-320501/)).<br><br>A licence requires a competitive call under the telecommunications law and security clearance, which is the procedural reason the base records rather than a refusal.<br><br>The incumbent's satellite terminal licence was renewed in February 2026 for a further ten years to 2035 ([renewal](https://www.iam.ma/documents/66341/0/Maroc+Telecom+-+Rapport+financier+S1+2026+%282%29.pdf/261de29d-cee8-407f-1e28-dbb3a53f985f)).</details> | Mixed, an incumbent terminal licence renewed to 2035 while the low-orbit operator is still unlicensed |
| Mozambique | [Low-orbit service is live](https://www.techinafrica.com/starlink-partners-airtel-vodacom-rival-ally/) with an operator backhaul partnership, [donated terminals went to flood centres](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/starlink-vodacom-bring-hope-to-flood-hit-mozambique/), and [trials of an Angolan satellite](https://www.itweb.africa/article/tmcel-begins-angosat-2-satellite-trials/JN1gPvOAwolMjL6m) began in August 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The low-orbit service is live, with an operator backhaul and equipment-resale partnership; no licence date, subscriber count or terminal count is held ([service](https://www.techinafrica.com/starlink-partners-airtel-vodacom-rival-ally/), [deployment](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/starlink-vodacom-bring-hope-to-flood-hit-mozambique/)).<br><br>Donated terminals were deployed by the regulator with open access at flood accommodation and coordination centres in February 2026, plus four operator connectivity kits at four named locations; the terminal count is not stated ([terminals](https://clubofmozambique.com/news/starlink-vodacom-bring-hope-to-flood-hit-mozambique/)).<br><br>The state operator began proof-of-concept testing of the Angolan satellite in August 2026 as a step towards a commercial agreement, implementing a bilateral memorandum of June 2026; no commercial terms are published ([trials](https://www.itweb.africa/article/tmcel-begins-angosat-2-satellite-trials/JN1gPvOAwolMjL6m)).</details> | Advanced |
| Namibia | A [satellite licence application was declined](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/06/22/namibias-communications-regulator-confirms-dismissal-of-starlinks-licence-appeals/) and 622 of 624 reconsideration requests failed, under a [51 per cent Namibian ownership requirement](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/06/22/namibias-communications-regulator-confirms-dismissal-of-starlinks-licence-appeals/) the applicant cannot meet. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Licence and spectrum were declined in March 2026 on three of six statutory criteria; 622 of 624 reconsideration requests failed the legal threshold, two were refused on merits, and the operator's own request was dismissed as out of time ([dismissal](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/06/22/namibias-communications-regulator-confirms-dismissal-of-starlinks-licence-appeals/), [rejection](https://www.connectingafrica.com/regulation/namibian-regulator-rejects-starlink-again), [block](https://www.connectingafrica.com/regulation/starlink-blocked-from-operating-in-namibia)).<br><br>The ownership requirement is in force with no exemption granted; the regulator held that wholly foreign ownership raises questions of jurisdiction, enforceability of compliance obligations and effective oversight, and states the power to waive the rule rests with the minister rather than with it ([requirement](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/06/22/namibias-communications-regulator-confirms-dismissal-of-starlinks-licence-appeals/)). The applicant stated its global shareholding rules bar it from accepting local ownership ([exemption](https://www.connectingafrica.com/regulation/starlink-blocked-from-operating-in-namibia)).</details> | Regressed |
| Niger | [Satellite retail service](https://www.actuniger.com/technologie/20854-starlink-enfin-disponible-officiellement-au-niger-lespoir-dune-connexion-fiable-et-rapide-devient-realite.html) has been available since March 2025 and an [operator-integrated version](https://www.actuniger.com/societe/21684-connectivite-et-innovation-airtel-niger-lance-sa-solution-starlink-by-airtel-pour-plus-dinternet-aux-usagers.html) launched in January 2026, while the regulator [moved against unauthorised resale](https://www.actuniger.com/societe/21307-niger-larcep-declare-la-guerre-aux-wifi-zones-pirates-utilisant-starlink.html). <details><summary>Full record</summary>Retail service became commercially available through approved sellers in March 2025 under an agreement signed with the operator in October 2024 ([availability](https://www.actuniger.com/technologie/20854-starlink-enfin-disponible-officiellement-au-niger-lespoir-dune-connexion-fiable-et-rapide-devient-realite.html), [launch](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/starlink-beams-into-niger), [distribution](https://www.lesahel.org/lancement-officiel-des-activites-de-distribution-des-kits-starlink-par-ninetec-garantir-une-connexion-internet-fiable-accessible-partout-et-a-moindre-cout/)).<br><br>A telco-integrated service was commercialised in January 2026, with the operator rather than the customer holding the satellite relationship ([integration](https://www.actuniger.com/societe/21684-connectivite-et-innovation-airtel-niger-lance-sa-solution-starlink-by-airtel-pour-plus-dinternet-aux-usagers.html)).<br><br>The regulator served a formal notice against unauthorised resale in September 2025 with a deadline of 3 October, carrying three months to a year and fines to 60m FCFA ([notice](https://www.actuniger.com/societe/21307-niger-larcep-declare-la-guerre-aux-wifi-zones-pirates-utilisant-starlink.html)), invoking a prior-authorisation regime that has been in force and is now enforced ([regime](https://www.actuniger.com/societe/21307-niger-larcep-declare-la-guerre-aux-wifi-zones-pirates-utilisant-starlink.html)).</details> | Advanced |
| Nigeria | [Satellite service became the second-largest provider](https://techcabal.com/2025/03/10/starlink-now-nigerias-second) then paused orders over congestion, a [second landing permit](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/15/ncc-grants-landing-permit-to-amazons-project-kuiper-for-satellite-operations-in-nigeria/) was granted with no service, and [two national satellites](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/telecom/internet-le-nigeria-choisit-iai-et-thales-alenia-space-pour-acquerir-deux-nouveaux-satellites) were approved for 2028 and beyond. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The incumbent satellite service became the country's second-largest internet provider on late-2024 figures, then paused new orders in parts of two cities over congestion in September 2025, and was brought inside the regulator's biometric subscriber-verification regime with more than 66,000 users required to submit identity numbers and headshots ([provider](https://techcabal.com/2025/03/10/starlink-now-nigerias-second), [congestion](https://techcabal.com/2025/09/15/starlink-halts-new-orders-in-lagos-abuja-over-congestion/), [verification](https://techcabal.com/2025/12/31/starlink-new-biometric-update-for-nigerians/)).<br><br>A landing permit for a second constellation was granted in January 2026 with no service ([permit](https://techafricanews.com/2026/01/15/ncc-grants-landing-permit-to-amazons-project-kuiper-for-satellite-operations-in-nigeria/)).<br><br>Cabinet approved the acquisition of two high-throughput communications satellites in August 2026 on an operator timetable of 2028 and 2029 or 2030, with no contract value published and negotiation still to follow ([satellites](https://www.wearetech.africa/fr/fils/actualites/telecom/internet-le-nigeria-choisit-iai-et-thales-alenia-space-pour-acquerir-deux-nouveaux-satellites), [approval](https://saharareporters.com/2026/08/22/nigerian-government-approves-nigcomsat-2a-2b-satellites-expand-broadband-strengthen)); a low-earth-orbit partnership announced in January 2025 has no service ([partnership](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/nigcomsat-eutelsat-partner-for-leo-services-in-nigeria)) and a direct-to-device memorandum targeted for end-2025 has nothing on file ([direct to device](https://itweb.africa/article/nigeria-joins-race-to-deploy-direct-to-device-satellite-connectivity/5yONP7Erb3QMXWrb)).</details> | Mixed, the largest satellite provider paused orders over congestion and was brought inside biometric verification while three further satellite programmes remain unbuilt |
| Rwanda | A [satellite reseller authorisation](https://www.newtimes.co.rw/article/33378/news/featured/bscs-starlink-authorisation-enhances-internet-reliability-in-rwanda) was certified by the space agency in February 2026, on a partnership running since December 2025. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The reseller was certified in February 2026 and the partnership dates from December 2025; the held body is partial and flagged for verification ([authorisation](https://www.newtimes.co.rw/article/33378/news/featured/bscs-starlink-authorisation-enhances-internet-reliability-in-rwanda)).<br><br>No subscriber count, tariff or coverage figure is published, so the authorisation records a licensing step rather than availability.</details> | Advanced |
| Sao Tome and Principe | [Satellite internet service was activated](https://spaceinafrica.com/2025/12/11/starlink-begins-service-in-sao-tome-and-principe/) in December 2025, about ten months after the operator stated an intention to invest. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Intention to invest was reported in February 2025 and service began in December ([service](https://spaceinafrica.com/2025/12/11/starlink-begins-service-in-sao-tome-and-principe/), [intention](https://rstp.st/2025/02/24/empresa-starlink-de-elon-musk-pretende-investir-em-stp-trazendo-internet-de-alta-qualidade/)).<br><br>No licence terms, subscriber count, tariff or regulatory condition is published. In an island state whose only submarine cable to its second island is still in build, satellite is the redundancy that arrived first.</details> | Advanced |
| Senegal | A [five-year satellite operating authorisation](https://aps.sn/starlink-une-autorisation-dexploitation-de-cinq-ans-accordee-au-fournisseur-dacces-a-internet-par-satellite-tutelle/) was granted in February 2026, a [teleport gateway](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/10/sonatel-expands-gandoul-teleport-with-eutelsat-oneweb-satellite-gateway/) is in service, and the [universal connectivity project](https://lesoleil.sn/actualites/technologie/connectivite-universelle-le-senegal-vise-1-million-de-beneficiaires-dinternet-gratuit-dici-fin-2026/) slipped through the first half. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The authorisation runs five years, with commercial launch about 4 February 2026 and the country the operator's twenty-sixth African market; the regulator separately warned against illegal wireless resale ([authorisation](https://aps.sn/starlink-une-autorisation-dexploitation-de-cinq-ans-accordee-au-fournisseur-dacces-a-internet-par-satellite-tutelle/), [framework](https://www.rts.sn/actualite/detail/a-la-une/lartp-clarifie-le-cadre-dautorisation-de-starlink-au-senegal), [warning](https://techafricanews.com/2026/04/02/senegal-issues-warning-over-illegal-wi-fi-networks-using-starlink/)).<br><br>A teleport satellite gateway is in service; the source states no commissioning date so the publication date is used ([gateway](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/10/sonatel-expands-gandoul-teleport-with-eutelsat-oneweb-satellite-gateway/)).<br><br>The universal connectivity project opened a deployment tour in April 2026; the target was restated between February and April and the first half closed with nothing published ([tour](https://lesoleil.sn/actualites/technologie/connectivite-universelle-le-senegal-vise-1-million-de-beneficiaires-dinternet-gratuit-dici-fin-2026/), [deployment](https://lesoleil.sn/actualites/technologie/new-deal-technologique-alioune-sall-lance-loffensive-contre-la-fracture-numerique-a-kolda/), [kits](https://lesoleil.sn/actualites/technologie/deploiement-de-starlink-letat-a-negocie-5-000-kits-pour-connecter-un-million-de-senegalais/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Seychelles | [Satellite internet soft-launched](https://statehouse.gov.sc/news/7148/starlink-begins-operations-in-seychelles-expanding-internet-choices-for-consumers) at a State House briefing in July 2026, after Cabinet approved the licence in February and the operator incorporated locally by May. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Cabinet approved the service provider licence on 18 February 2026, the operator incorporated locally and was in final licensing by May, and the soft launch was confirmed at a State House briefing in July ([soft launch](https://statehouse.gov.sc/news/7148/starlink-begins-operations-in-seychelles-expanding-internet-choices-for-consumers), [clearance](https://www.nation.sc/articles/29683/starlink-cleared-for-takeoff-in-seychelles), [incorporation](https://www.nation.sc/articles/30901/in-the-national-assembly), [live](https://www.connectingafrica.com/connectivity/starlink-live-in-c-te-d-ivoire-and-seychelles), [coverage](https://tech-ish.com/2026/07/28/starlink-28-african-countries-kenya-freeze/)).<br><br>Requiring local incorporation before licensing is the design choice worth recording: several units in this frame licensed the same operator on a full foreign-ownership basis, and this one did not.<br><br>No subscriber count, tariff or coverage figure is published.</details> | Advanced |
| Sierra Leone | Satellite service has been [licensed since June 2024 with three local resellers](https://sierraloaded.sl/news/natca-boss-rural-network-expansion-global/), and [twelve universities were connected](https://www.itedgenews.africa/sierra-leone-partners-with-starlink-to-expand-university-internet/) under the national broadband strategy. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The service has been licensed since June 2024 and operates through three local resellers ([licence](https://sierraloaded.sl/news/natca-boss-rural-network-expansion-global/), [universities](https://www.itedgenews.africa/sierra-leone-partners-with-starlink-to-expand-university-internet/)).<br><br>Twelve universities were connected through a local reseller under the broadband strategy ([connection](https://www.itedgenews.africa/sierra-leone-partners-with-starlink-to-expand-university-internet/)).<br><br>Licensed satellite service reaching universities and, separately, health facilities makes one foreign operator the connectivity of last resort for two national systems at once.</details> | Advanced |
| Somalia | [Satellite service became available nationwide](https://www.hiiraan.com/news4/2025/Aug/202463/starlink_launches_in_somalia_bringing_satellite_internet_to_rural_areas_and_narrowing_the_digital_divide.aspx) in August 2025, four months after licensing, on a full foreign-ownership model without major public debate. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The service was licensed in April 2025 and available nationwide from August, with a local reseller network following ([availability](https://www.hiiraan.com/news4/2025/Aug/202463/starlink_launches_in_somalia_bringing_satellite_internet_to_rural_areas_and_narrowing_the_digital_divide.aspx), [licence](https://www.hiiraan.com/news4/2025/Apr/201121/starlink_launches_in_somalia_expanding_satellite_internet_to_underserved_regions.aspx), [reseller](https://www.hiiraan.com/news4/2025/Sept/203052/astaanconnect_becomes_somalia_s_first_official_starlink_reseller.aspx)).<br><br>Licensing on a full foreign-ownership model without major public debate is the fastest such accommodation on this base's West and East African record. No local-content, data-routing or emergency-access condition is published.</details> | Advanced |
| South Africa | The ministerial policy direction was [rejected by the regulator](https://www.citizen.co.za/news/south-africa/south-africas-starlink-dream-hits-legal-dead-end-icasa-rejects-malatsis-bee-plan/) and the 30% equity requirement stands, while [a distributor deal took the route the Act already allows](https://www.dailymaverick.co.za/article/2026-07-15-herotel-chosen-as-amazon-leo-distributor-in-maziv-race-for-satellite-internet/). <details><summary>Full record</summary>An instrument was issued and neutralised inside five months. A policy direction gazetted on 12 December 2025 to unblock satellite licensing was [rejected in May 2026](https://www.citizen.co.za/news/south-africa/south-africas-starlink-dream-hits-legal-dead-end-icasa-rejects-malatsis-bee-plan/), and the 30% historically-disadvantaged equity requirement stands.
