The minister [publicly backs entry while conceding licensing is the regulator's alone](https://www.itweb.co.za/article/malatsi-backs-leo-operators-as-icasa-calls-the-shots/Gb3BwMWaPAEv2k6V), and at the spectrum hearings the largest prospective entrant [pressed the regulator on how that requirement applies to the new gateway and user-terminal licence categories](https://www.advanced-television.com/2026/08/24/spacex-appeals-to-south-africas-regulator/) — which the draft does not say.<br><br>2026-07-15 — the market entered anyway by another door: [a satellite operator appointed a domestic distributor](https://www.dailymaverick.co.za/article/2026-07-15-herotel-chosen-as-amazon-leo-distributor-in-maziv-race-for-satellite-internet/), the acquisition route the Act already permits, while [the other large constellation launched in its 27th African country without entering South Africa](https://mybroadband.co.za/news/broadband/658586-starlink-launches-in-27th-african-country-before-south-africa.html).<br><br>2026-05/08 — the fee rules moved. [Draft amendments to the spectrum and fees regulations were gazetted, with oral hearings on 19 and 20 August 2026](https://www.icasa.org.za/news/2026/icasa-to-hold-public-hearings-on-draft-amendments-to-radio-frequency-spectrum-regulations-and-fees-regulations) covering satellite registration, blanket licensing for terminal networks and protection of radio astronomy; the prospective entrant's [representations ask for flat per-licence fees and a ten-year minimum term](https://www.icasa.org.za/uploads/files/Space-X_Redacted.pdf).</details> | Mixed, the licensing framework went backwards while a distribution route opened and fee rules were consulted |
| South Sudan | No [satellite broadband licence, authorisation or access terms](https://www.eyeradio.org/nca-reports-huge-internet-growth-in-south-sudan/) are held; one account names a rural partnership and nothing more. <details><summary>Full record</summary>One account names a rural satellite partnership and no licence, authorisation or access terms are held ([account](https://www.eyeradio.org/nca-reports-huge-internet-growth-in-south-sudan/)).<br><br>In a country whose fibre backbone is untendered and whose coverage extensions are refused on revenue grounds, the absence of any satellite licensing record is a gap in what can be said rather than evidence that no service exists.</details> | No change |
| Sudan | [Satellite service was never officially licensed](https://www.france24.com/en/live-news/20240403-smuggled-starlink-dishes-throw-lifeline-to-some-in-war-torn-sudan) and has run informally through smuggled terminals, and an [armed group ordered services cut](https://sudantribune.net/article/317878) at a locality from 26 August 2026. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The service has never been officially licensed on any source held, reaching the country through smuggled dishes described as a lifeline ([terminals](https://www.france24.com/en/live-news/20240403-smuggled-starlink-dishes-throw-lifeline-to-some-in-war-torn-sudan), [lifeline](https://globalvoices.org/2024/08/20/starlink-in-sudan-a-lifeline-or-war-facilitator/)).<br><br>An armed group ordered services cut at a West Kordofan locality from 26 August 2026 ([cut](https://sudantribune.net/article/317878)), having banned the service at the same place in December 2025 ([ban](https://www.darfur24.com/en/2025/12/12/rsf-bans-satellite-internet-use-in-al-nuhud-confiscates-starlink-devices/)).<br><br>Connectivity in the conflict areas is therefore governed by whoever holds the ground rather than by the regulator, and the same terminals are described in the record as both a lifeline and a war facilitator ([conflict](https://timep.org/2024/09/19/internet-in-conflict-sudans-battle-for-connection/)).</details> | Regressed |
| Tanzania | [Satellite broadband went live](https://www.techinafrica.com/starlink-partners-airtel-vodacom-rival-ally/) in November 2025 through operator partnerships, and no subscriber, licence or pricing figure for the country is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The service went live at month precision, with the satellite operator moving from rival to partner of two mobile operators ([service](https://www.techinafrica.com/starlink-partners-airtel-vodacom-rival-ally/), [partnership](https://www.mwananchi.co.tz/mw/habari/kitaifa/airtel-yatangaza-kushirikiana-na-spacex-kuleta-mtandao-wa-starlink-barani-afrika-5030464)).<br><br>No subscriber, licence or pricing figure is held. Arriving through operator partnerships rather than a direct consumer licence is a different market structure from most units in this frame, and no licensing instrument is on file either way.</details> | Advanced |
| Togo | A [satellite licence](https://afrique.le360.ma/economie/internet-haut-debit-en-seulement-4-ans-starlink-operationnel-dans-un-pays-africain-sur-deux_UYXWC5RPWNGGFIYLU5VHL6L7ZU/) has not been granted twelve months after the operator listed the country as available, and [spectrum royalties for satellite and machine-to-machine services](https://www.republiquetogolaise.tg/gestion-publique/3107-12146-conseil-des-ministres-du-30-juillet-2026) were amended by decree. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The operator lists the country as available from 2026 with no licence granted; twelve months without movement is the finding, and the regulator has issued no statement on any application ([licence](https://afrique.le360.ma/economie/internet-haut-debit-en-seulement-4-ans-starlink-operationnel-dans-un-pays-africain-sur-deux_UYXWC5RPWNGGFIYLU5VHL6L7ZU/), [regulation](https://techs.tg/2025/01/14/starlink-en-afrique-une-revolution-de-lacces-a-internet/), [coverage](https://www.republicoftogo.com/toutes-les-rubriques/high-tech/gouvernance-numerique-connecter-les-zones-oubliees)).<br><br>The royalty amendment was adopted in July 2026 and framed as encouraging innovation and connectivity; the parent decree is held only as an image scan with no text layer, so the baseline rates are unreadable ([royalties](https://www.republiquetogolaise.tg/gestion-publique/3107-12146-conseil-des-ministres-du-30-juillet-2026), [adjustment](https://actu-togo.tg/2026/07/31/numerique-le-togo-ajuste-sa-reglementation-pour-soutenir-linnovation-et-la-connectivite/)).<br><br>Adjusting satellite spectrum royalties while granting no satellite licence is the sequence the record shows.</details> | No change |
| Tunisia | A [satellite licence is withheld](https://carthagemagazine.com/starlink-in-tunisia/) with pre-orders opened in February 2026, the unresolved point being routing through a local ground station and a national interconnection point. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Pre-orders opened in February 2026 with no licence issued ([status](https://carthagemagazine.com/starlink-in-tunisia/), [pre-orders](https://www.thd.tn/starlink-ouvre-les-precommandes-en-tunisie-avec-un-acompte-de-9-dollars-lancement-commercial-annonce-en-2026/)).<br><br>The unresolved point is routing: the state wants low-earth-orbit traffic through a local ground station and a national interconnection point.<br><br>That is a substantive sovereignty position rather than an administrative delay, and it is stated more clearly here than in any other unit in this frame that has withheld or granted the same licence.</details> | No change |
| Uganda | [Commercial service began on 2 September 2026](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/business/govt-welcomes-starlink-internet-service-provi-NV_239935_092026) under a five-year licence requiring a national gateway, a point of presence and registration of every device activated in the country. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The licence text is not held; a January 2026 wholesale-model ruling and a May 2026 direct licence are unreconciled, and no subscriber or tariff figure is published ([switch-on](https://redpepper.co.ug/starlink-lands-in-uganda-roke-telkom-paratus-uganda-first-to-fire-up-satellite-internet/), [licence](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/business/starlink-enters-ugandas-telecom-sector-after-NV_233702_052026), [wholesale](https://www.ceo.co.ug/ugandans-to-access-elon-musks-starlink-internet-through-telecoms-ucc-rules/), [blocking](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/science/why-starlink-accepted-ugandas-request-to-bloc-NV_225713_032026), [shutdown](https://www.developingtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/satellite-communications-networks/19569-starlink-shuts-down-access-to-its-satellite-internet-service-in-uganda.html)).<br><br>Two providers switched the service on in August 2026 and the held accounts differ on which was first, one naming a regional operator alone ([reseller](https://developingtelecoms.com/telecom-technology/satellite-communications-networks/20727-paratus-launches-starlink-satellite-services-in-uganda.html)) and the other naming it alongside a domestic operator ([switch-on](https://redpepper.co.ug/starlink-lands-in-uganda-roke-telkom-paratus-uganda-first-to-fire-up-satellite-internet/)). Either way it is a reseller launch rather than a licensing decision, with no subscriber count, price or coverage figure published.<br><br>Partner states opened a joint funding account for a regional communications satellite study with deposits due 30 September 2026, and neither the study's cost nor each state's share was disclosed ([satellite](https://chimpreports.com/east-african-states-open-bank-account-to-fund-joint-communications-satellite-study/)).<br><br>2026-09-02 — the operator began commercial service, ending the regulatory process that followed a memorandum and a five-year licence signed with the regulator in May 2026. The licence requires a national gateway and physical point of presence, an operational office and registration of every device activated in the country, conditions the regulator framed as security, revenue assurance, accountability and compliance with Ugandan law ([launch](https://www.newvision.co.ug/category/business/govt-welcomes-starlink-internet-service-provi-NV_239935_092026), [pricing](https://www.pulse.ug/story/starlink-uganda-launch-prices-mtn-airtel-2026090200285472149)). The January wholesale ruling and the May direct licence remain unreconciled on the record.</details> | Advanced |
| Zambia | The country is [among the top three African satellite markets](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/07/22/starlink-expands-to-26-african-markets-as-three-countries-account-for-64-of-subscribers-space-in-africa-report-shows/) by June 2026 after entry in October 2023, and no national subscriber count, licence fee, contract term or procurement route is held. <details><summary>Full record</summary>Market entry was October 2023 and the country ranks among the top three African markets by June 2026 ([market](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/07/22/starlink-expands-to-26-african-markets-as-three-countries-account-for-64-of-subscribers-space-in-africa-report-shows/), [appraisal](https://documents1.worldbank.org/curated/en/099012025023033676/txt/P505094-630a5ef9-d23a-4221-b7c8-397c94a90b4d.txt), [chiefdom](https://techafricanews.com/2025/09/29/smart-zambia-brings-starlink-internet-to-remote-moomba-chiefdom/), [skills](https://techafricanews.com/2026/08/05/smart-zambia-and-itu-equip-mpika-youth-resource-centre-to-boost-digital-skills/)).<br><br>A top-three continental position with no licence fee, contract term or procurement route on record is worth stating as exactly that.<br><br>Direct-to-cell satellite trials name the country as a trial site on an operator statement, with no licence, spectrum decision or trial result held ([trials](https://techafricanews.com/2026/07/28/mtns-ebenezer-asante-on-leo-satellites-inclusion-and-building-a-borderless-african-digital-economy/)).</details> | Advanced |
| Zimbabwe | [Satellite service launched in September 2024](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/05/06/starlink-drives-zimbabwes-vsat-subscriptions-to-a-record-67057/) after a cut-off order, [two licences were issued](https://www.techzim.co.zw/2024/09/potraz-starlink-licence-disruption/) with no coverage obligation or duration stated, and [terminal subscriptions](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/05/06/starlink-drives-zimbabwes-vsat-subscriptions-to-a-record-67057/) are counted as a category rather than by operator. <details><summary>Full record</summary>The service launched in September 2024 after the 2024 cut-off order, and licence terms, fee and duration are not held ([service](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/05/06/starlink-drives-zimbabwes-vsat-subscriptions-to-a-record-67057/), [rural](https://techcabal.com/2025/06/26/starlink-in-zimbabwe/)).<br><br>Two licences were issued in September 2024 and announced four days later; no coverage obligation, duration or universal-service term is stated in the held account ([licences](https://www.techzim.co.zw/2024/09/potraz-starlink-licence-disruption/), [award](https://www.techzim.co.zw/2024/09/potraz-starlink-licence-disruption/)).<br><br>The regulator counts satellite terminals as a category and not the operator separately ([terminals](https://spaceinafrica.com/2026/05/06/starlink-drives-zimbabwes-vsat-subscriptions-to-a-record-67057/)), and the country's share of the African satellite market is reported off that same series ([share](https://techcabal.com/2026/05/26/africa-risk-losing-billions-to-satellite-internet-operators/)); the state fixed operator's own satellite reseller revenue is reported in its 2025 annual report ([reseller](https://www.newsday.co.zw/business/article/200057743/govt-debt-to-telone-doubles-to-us42m)).</details> | Advanced |
